# Showing new listings for Wednesday, 14 October 2026
## Keyword: SLAM
### Title:
          FoundationVIO: Enforcing Metric Scale in Feed-forward Visual-Inertial Odometry
 - **Authors:** Jin Rhee, Yuhao Zhang, Frank Dellaert, Maurice Fallon
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Feed-forward geometric foundation models (GFMs) directly predict dense geometry and camera poses from batches of images. Emerging visual SLAM methods chain together consecutive GFM batches to form dense point cloud maps. A core challenge is how to address the ambiguous geometric scale of GFM predictions: independent predictions are locally consistent up-to-scale but not constrained by any direct physical measurements. Models trained on data with known scale improve the consistency of GFM reconstructions but again do not directly measure it. Consequently, trajectories and 3D reconstructions from GFM-enabled SLAM methods inherit this issue. Motivated by parallels with classical visual-inertial odometry, we introduce FoundationVIO which integrates inertial sensing with GFM predictions to recover metric scale reconstruction using a factor-graph based approach. We demonstrate odometry estimates competitive with classical state-of-the-art VIO methods and accurate dense reconstructions that outperform GFM-enabled SLAM methods. We also add a minimal GFM-based loop-closure mechanism to create a full Visual-Inertial SLAM system capable of creating globally accurate reconstructions and pose estimates. We evaluate our method on three datasets with well known sensor systems and demonstrate its compatibility on ubiquitous camera and IMU sensors. The method runs at approximately real-time on a consumer-grade laptop GPU. Code: this https URL.
### Title:
          RootSLAM: Square Root Bundle Adjustment for Visual-Inertial SLAM
 - **Authors:** Tomás Santucci, Mateo de Mayo, Daniel Cremers, Taihú Pire
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Efficient perception is a prerequisite for the small, low-power devices targeted by robotics, mixed reality, autonomous driving, and drone applications, all of which rely on Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (SLAM) to estimate their motion within their surroundings. Learning-based SLAM systems achieve state-of-the-art accuracy but are demanding in compute, whereas classical geometry-based methods retain a strong efficiency advantage with little to no loss in accuracy. In this work we present RootSLAM, a fully-featured visual-inertial SLAM system for multi-camera setups built on Basalt. RootSLAM achieves accuracy competitive with the state of the art while running up to 20 times faster, facilitating the deployment of perception systems in resource constrained environments. We adapt the square-root bundle adjustment formulation from structure from motion into the multi-camera visual-inertial SLAM setting through a novel reformulation that leverages the system's pose-graph, letting the global optimizer run in single-precision floating point, thus doubling its performance. We provide a thorough benchmark against the state of the art together with an ablation study of our contributions. Our new implementation, including its map management and loop detection module, pose-graph optimizer, and global mapping components, will be open-sourced.
### Title:
          All-St3R SLAM: Real-time RGB Dynamic SLAM with Pixel-wise Motion Masking
 - **Authors:** Yewon Kim, Paul Hongsuck Seo, Sang Chul Ahn
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 SLAM (Simultaneous Localization and Mapping) is a core technology in autonomous systems that enables them to estimate their own position and build a map of unknown environments simultaneously. SLAM systems in dynamic environments are of great practical importance. However, existing dynamic SLAM approaches still have several limitations like over-removal and high computational costs. To address these limitations, we propose a real-time RGB Dynamic SLAM framework that precisely removes moving objects at the pixel level without relying on predefined labels or segmentation models. Our system combines pixel motion predicted by ego-motion using DUSt3R with point tracking from All-Tracker, removing only actually moving pixels by analyzing discrepancies between the two motion sources. Unlike object-level masking methods, our approach eliminates only currently moving regions, allowing temporarily static parts to be utilized for tracking. Operating at approximately 10 FPS, the system achieves real-time performance on standard 30 FPS video streams by subsampling every 3 frames. Relying solely on RGB inputs, this significantly improves upon state-of-the-art dense dynamic SLAM approaches (0.5-3 FPS), presenting a highly practical solution.
### Title:
          Dense Endoluminal Mapping and Localisation from Monocular Bronchoscopy
 - **Authors:** Salvatore Esposito, Matias Mattamala, Daniel Rebain, Liang Zhao, Kev Dhaliwal, Mohsen Khadem, Subramanian Ramamoorthy
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Bronchoscopy inserts a camera through the airways to diagnose endoluminal disease. Locating the camera along the airway usually requires additional equipment, such as an electromagnetic tracker and a preoperative CT scan. In this paper, we investigate the use of the monocular camera stream to localise the bronchoscope without requiring additional sensing. We introduce a monocular SLAM system that localises the tip of a bronchoscope and reconstructs the airway surface around it from RGB video alone. A Depth Anything 3 backbone, fine-tuned on over a million photorealistic airway renderings, returns metric per-pixel depth and is the system's only source of scale. DINOv3 features routed through a custom cross-attention matcher supply sub-pixel correspondences on mucosa where classical descriptors fail. Both enter a factor graph alongside a dense constraint from projective iterative closest point (ICP) registration against the system's own running reconstruction, so surface evidence stabilises the directions the visual factors leave weakly observable. On six airways rendered from patient CT scans, the system reaches 26.1 mm mean absolute trajectory error and 77.5% mean surface F-score. Its mean surface accuracy exceeds that of five recent monocular SLAM and feed-forward baselines. MASt3R-SLAM, which fits a free scale to the ground truth, has a lower mean trajectory error (22.0 mm). ORB-SLAM3 completes only two of the six airways, whereas our system completes all six and also reconstructs two ex vivo specimens.
### Title:
          Physical SLAM: Physics-aware Simultaneous Localisation and Mapping
 - **Authors:** Guangming Wang, Zhishuo Liu, Haibing Wu, Wanli Ma, Yixiong Jing, Olaf Wysocki, Brian Sheil
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 To reconstruct a predictive 4D representation of the physical world from monocular video captured by a moving camera, we jointly reconstruct dynamic Gaussian radiance fields and infer their underlying physical dynamics. This paper proposes \emph{Physical SLAM}, a physics-aware method for constructing 4D Gaussian fields of deformable objects and predicting their future state when subjected to external actions, given a sequence of RGB-D images. Our method separates the scene into a static background Gaussian field for camera localization and a dynamic object Gaussian field driven by a spring--mass physical model of elastic deformation, inertia, and damping. To integrate visual observations with physical dynamics, we introduce virtual observation forces that gradually pull simulated surface nodes toward observed depth points, avoiding abrupt replacement of simulated node positions. Meanwhile, the physical parameters of the spring--mass model are updated from simulation errors aggregated over the full reconstruction observation window, enabling the reconstructed dynamics to align with observations. To couple appearance, geometry, and physics, we further design a physics-aware Gaussian transformation and surface alignment strategy, where dynamic Gaussians are transformed along the deforming physical surface of the spring--mass model and constrained to remain aligned with the surface during rendering-based optimization. The resulting representation supports observed-frame rendering and action-conditioned simulation, extending SLAM maps to describe how deformable objects respond to manipulation. On the PhysTwin benchmark, our method improves reconstruction and prediction PSNR over PhysTwin by 10.4\% and 11.0\%, respectively. On the TUM RGB-D benchmark, it reduces average camera tracking error by 20.6\%, improves SSIM by 3.6\%, and reduces LPIPS by 8.3\% compared with 4DGS-SLAM.
### Title:
          Gaussian Splatting for Real-Time Scene Reconstruction: Methods, Systems, and Trade-offs
 - **Authors:** Ashim Dahal, Rabab Abdelfattah, Nick Rahimi
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) allows for high-quality real-time novel-view synthesis. Since its inception in 2023, its explicit and highly configurable scene representation has been extended toward sparse-view generation, dynamic reconstruction, efficiency and compression, streaming, and simultaneous localization and mapping (SLAM). Although Gaussian Splatting can provide real-time rendering, rendering an existing representation is distinct from constructing, updating, or maintaining the scene itself. In this survey, we synthesize current literature, categorize them and provide a connection of current approaches and tools towards an end-to-end pipeline. Explicitly, we categorize GS scene reconstruction into five overlapping families: single-shot generation, dynamic representations, efficiency and compression, streaming and immersive systems, and Gaussian Splatting SLAM. We distinguish render-only throughput from task-specific scene generation, update, tracking, mapping, and optimization costs, and combine controlled sparse-view benchmarking with native evaluations to identify cross-family quality, latency, memory, and update-cost trade-offs.
### Title:
          Autonomous 3D Visual-Thermal Inspection for Utility-Scale Solar Farms
 - **Authors:** Lachlan Tychsen-Smith, Mark Cox, Brett Wood, David Haddon, Kenrick Anderson, Benjamin Duck, Jim O'Malley, Noel W Duffy, Peyman Moghadam
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Utility-scale photovoltaic (PV) inspection is dominated by aerial surveys, which are effective for identifying module-level hot cells but provide limited visibility of critical components located under the array, including connectors, cabling, fasteners and structural elements. These assets are difficult to inspect consistently at scale, and their defects are often spatially small, partially occluded and difficult to localise from isolated images. We present an automated 3D visual-thermal inspection system (Tricorder) for under-panel PV asset inspection. The approach combines LiDAR-inertial Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (SLAM) with geometrically calibrated RGB and thermal imagery and machine-learning-based object detection and segmentation. Detected assets are associated with the reconstructed 3D environment, allowing visual and thermal observations from multiple viewpoints to be spatially fused and localised. We demonstrate the approach on utility-scale PV data for MC4 connector detection and connection-state recognition, thermal anomaly identification, and detection of structural components including bolts, nuts and torque tubes. The results demonstrate a pathway from image-based inspection toward spatially grounded autonomous inspection, where defects are detected and linked to specific assets and their 3D locations within the solar farm.
## Keyword: odometry
### Title:
          ToF-Wheel Odometry: Navigating Degeneration of Localization With Limited Sensing Capabilities
 - **Authors:** Xu Yang, Wenhao Li, Zhengyu Wei, Zening Li, Longxiang Huang, Bo Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Wheel odometry provides an efficient motion prior for ground robots but is prone to transient violations such as wheel slip or terrain-induced tilt. In filtering-based odometry, these violations frequently trigger divergence when they coincide with constrained geometric observability (e.g., featureless corridors). We address this challenge by proposing ToF-Wheel Odometry (TWO), a tightly-coupled filtering framework that integrates wheel kinematics with geometric constraints from Time-of-Flight (ToF) cameras. The primary contribution of this work is a degeneracy-aware, directional covariance-shaping strategy that replaces heuristic online covariance tuning with geometry-conditioned inflation. The core idea is to infer process noise from geometric corrections with a strict reliability check. We decompose the posterior-to-prior correction into a wheel-explainable subspace and an orthogonal remainder. The process covariance is anisotropically adjusted according to the observability of the ToF update: if the geometry is degenerate in non-kinematic directions, the remainder is suppressed as sensor noise; if the geometry is distinct, it is admitted as a valid kinematic violation. Additionally, we introduce an intra-frame feedback loop that retrospectively repairs the process covariance within the current frame to reduce structural lag during bursty disturbances. Extensive experiments on challenging scenarios demonstrate that TWO improves trajectory accuracy and robustness compared to static and adaptive Gaussian filtering, robustly navigating the trade-off between kinematic reliability and limited sensing capabilities.
### Title:
          FoundationVIO: Enforcing Metric Scale in Feed-forward Visual-Inertial Odometry
 - **Authors:** Jin Rhee, Yuhao Zhang, Frank Dellaert, Maurice Fallon
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Feed-forward geometric foundation models (GFMs) directly predict dense geometry and camera poses from batches of images. Emerging visual SLAM methods chain together consecutive GFM batches to form dense point cloud maps. We evaluate our method on three datasets with well known sensor systems and demonstrate its compatibility on ubiquitous camera and IMU sensors. The method runs at approximately real-time on a consumer-grade laptop GPU. Code: this https URL.
### Title:
          PointWalker: Mapless Quadruped Locomotion Directly From Raw LiDAR Point Clouds
 - **Authors:** Sicen Li, Zhen Chu, Chao Li, Jun Zheng, Qiuguo Zhu, Jun Wu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Quadruped robots deployed for inspection and field missions must cross heterogeneous terrain, from stairs, slopes, and gaps to high platforms, stepping stones, and balance beams, where safe support is sparse or see-through. Existing methods interpose a terrain map or a depth image between sensing and control. Maps are costly to maintain, corrupted by odometry drift and outliers, and misled by see-through gratings, while depth cameras degrade with lighting and surface material. In this paper, we present PointWalker, a mapless framework whose single reinforcement learning policy directly consumes raw point clouds from four light detection and ranging (LiDAR) sensors. Voxelized points are encoded by linear self-attention and fused with proprioception through cross-attention, so terrain geometry reaches the policy without maps or foothold estimation. One proximal policy optimization (PPO) run, with terrain-aware rewards and dual-channel simulation of see-through gratings, yields one policy for all seven terrain classes with a 90.1% mean success rate in simulation. Deployed zero-shot on a Deep Robotics X30 at 50 Hz onboard, it crosses 0.8-m gaps, climbs 0.8-m platforms and 45° steel-grating stairs, carries a 50-kg payload, and completes a 2.5-km mountain route.
### Title:
          A Hardware-Partitioned micro-ROS Mini-Vehicle for Operator-Supervised Neural Driving
 - **Authors:** Gustavo Claudio Karl Couto, Eric Aislan Antonelo, Gabriel George Zipperer
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 We present a low-cost miniature Ackermann vehicle for embedded neural-driving research, organized as a hardware-partitioned perceive-decide-act agent: an ESP32 camera node streams frames over Wi-Fi to a host computer, where neural inference and state estimation run, while an ESP32-S3 chassis controller executes safety-critical actuation in a 100 Hz per-wheel proportional-integral-derivative (PID) loop beneath a micro-ROS client. The partition isolates real-time control and safety on the vehicle while learning-based components run off-board, supervised by an operator from a gamepad. We characterize the complete pipeline during real closed-loop neural driving. Wireless sensing, rather than neural inference, dominates latency, with a 59.5 ms median capture-to-actuation delay; the platform absorbed wireless variability by holding the last safe command under a firmware watchdog, operating across twelve autonomous sessions with zero collisions or manual interventions; and encoder-inertial-measurement-unit (IMU) fusion cut odometry loop-closure error by 22%. These results indicate that carefully partitioned microcontroller-class vehicles support reproducible experiments in embedded AI, wireless control, and operator-supervised autonomous driving. The platform software is open source.
### Title:
          Recovering from Pose-Selection Failures in Repeated Visual Localization
 - **Authors:** Jaehyun Kim, Wonseok Kang, Tae-Wan Kim
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Repetitive structures can give an incorrect pose candidate more inlier support than an accurate one, causing visual localization to select it. History-Guided Pose Reselection (HPR) uses coarse position forecasts from up to five past localization outputs to distinguish well-separated current-query candidates. The best-fitting forecast proposes an alternative; HPR accepts it only if every well-fitting forecast is much closer to it than to the localizer's pose. HPR needs no odometry and returns the selected candidate unchanged, using prediction for selection rather than pose refinement. With one development-set threshold, HPR improves recall within 5 meters and 10 degrees in all 12 main configurations of Hierarchical Localization (HLoc), MASt3R-based pose generation, and JIST-based retrieval, losing successes on fewer than 0.4 percent of queries. On NAVER Gangnam B1, HPR corrects 97 HLoc failures and loses six successes; gains persist within 0.25 meters and 2 degrees on all three HLoc test sets. Median HPR runtime on HLoc candidates is below one millisecond, excluding pose generation. Recovery weakens under less frequent localization.

### Title:
          Manifold Navigation Learning Using Recurrent Networks: Efficient Training Techniques
 - **Authors:** Sarah Harkins Dayton, Sathya Rengaswami, Vasileios Maroulas, David L. Boothe, Ioannis D. Schizas
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Position tracking is a well-studied problem in Euclidean navigation, yet comparatively little attention has been given to navigation on general manifolds, where motion is governed by the underlying geometry of the space. In this work, we formalize the problem of manifold navigation as a velocity-driven tracking task and propose a coordinate-chart-based learning framework in which learning models operate entirely in a Euclidean representation. Predicted trajectories are mapped to the manifold via an immersion function prior to error computation, allowing geometric consistency to be evaluated without explicitly enforcing manifold constraints during learning. Within this framework, we focus on Gated Recurrent Units (GRUs) for sequential position prediction on curved manifolds after comparisons with alternative recurrent architectures. We evaluate our GRU models, trained with pertinent custom loss functions, against both a standard MSE loss function and a naive vector-addition baseline. We evaluate our approach on both analytically (and numerically) defined manifolds, as well as a surface reconstructed from a LiDAR point cloud. Our results show that recurrent models can learn to approximate manifold-constrained dynamics from velocity sequences and consistently outperform Euclidean baselines. Our results demonstrate that certain custom loss functions significantly improve long-horizon prediction accuracy compared to conventional point-wise loss functions.
### Title:
          TartanMatch: Towards Universal Dense Correspondence Across Modalities
 - **Authors:** Hyeokjoon Kwon, Jiting Cai, Ruogu Li, Kritan Bhandari, Geethika Hemkumar, Parv Maheshwari, Yuheng Qiu, Yuchen Zhang, Sebastian Scherer, Wenshan Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Machine Learning (cs.LG); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Robotic platforms combine multi-modal sensors to operate across diverse conditions, but most dense correspondence methods are limited to RGB-RGB or a small set of modality pairs. Training a model for each modality pair scales poorly with the number of sensors and forgoes potential positive transfer across modality pairs. We introduce TartanMatch, a unified model for dense correspondence across all 25 ordered pairs among RGB, event, thermal, depth, and projected LiDAR. TartanMatch adapts an RGB-pretrained correspondence model to all modality pairs within one shared network using lightweight modality adaptation and a mixture of real and synthetic data. On zero-shot real-world benchmarks, TartanMatch reduces Endpoint Error (EPE) over the strongest baselines by 61.9% for cross-modal matching and 49.6% for same-modal matching. We further show that joint training across all modality pairs improves average matching accuracy over pair-specific training, especially on challenging cross-modal pairs. These results demonstrate that a single model achieves broad multi-modal coverage and state-of-the-art matching accuracy.
### Title:
          VbotNav-0: Asynchronous Cloud-Edge Dual-Policy Navigation across Indoor and Outdoor Environments
 - **Authors:** Shiqi Tan, Yujun Zhang, Keyu Li, Shuaixi Pan, Siyu Zhou, Hailong Qin, Yinan Yu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Continuous indoor-outdoor navigation connects destination-level reasoning with motion decisions that must respond to current observations. We present VbotNav-0, a cloud-edge dual-policy framework that links these processes through asynchronous policy-to-policy conditioning. The cloud policy produces subgoal guidance from visual history, navigation instructions, and route tokens; the edge policy combines available guidance with current RGB and LiDAR observations to generate local trajectories. This formulation allows the local perception-action loop to continue between cloud updates. A common route representation connects indoor paths and outdoor map routes, and the training recipe combines navigation demonstrations, corrective data, and grounded visual question answering (VQA). In the reported closed-loop evaluation of 258 episodes across 145 scenes, VbotNav-0 achieves 55.04% success rate and 55.03% success weighted by path length.
### Title:
          nanoflann: A Header-Only KD-Tree Library for Exact Nearest-Neighbor Search on Compile-Time Product Manifolds
 - **Authors:** José Luis Blanco-Claraco
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Data Structures and Algorithms (cs.DS)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Nearest-neighbor (NN) search underlies countless robotics algorithms, but many state spaces are not Euclidean: orientations live on $SO(2)$ or $SO(3)$ and poses on $SE(2)$ or $SE(3)$, and a Euclidean KD-tree on their raw coordinates returns wrong neighbors. We present a header-only, dependency-free C++ KD-tree library for exact NN search on user-defined product manifolds declared at compile time: the space is composed from base manifolds ($\mathbb{R}^n$, $SO(2)$, $SO(3)$, $S^n$; products $SE(2)$, $SE(3)$, tori) with a single type alias, and the library synthesizes a metric whose pruning is provably admissible, wrap-aware for circular coordinates and double-cover-aware for unit quaternions. Experiments confirm exactness (recall $1.0$ vs an exhaustive scan under the same product metric), where the Euclidean-on-raw shortcut collapses to recall $\approx 0.5^{m}$ for $m$ rotation blocks, with up to three orders of magnitude speedup over brute force. We further contribute an incremental, self-balancing single-tree index for points under heavy churn (e.g. sliding-window LiDAR maps) or $SE(3)$ trajectories: bounded memory and faster updates than prior work, with an optional asynchronous tail-latency-bounding mode. The implementation is open source: this https URL

 - **Authors:** Sicen Li, Zhen Chu, Chao Li, Jun Zheng, Qiuguo Zhu, Jun Wu

Robotics (cs.RO)

 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/

### Title:
          Multimodal Sensor-Gated Mid-Air Launch and Vision-Based Recovery of a Child UAV on a Hovering Mothership Drone
 - **Authors:** Aashish Sahu, R. Prasanth Kumar
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Launching a micro unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) from a hovering carrier and recovering it in flight requires reliable confirmation that the child is securely seated before the carrier is allowed to descend. This is challenging because vision provides relative guidance but does not directly indicate load transfer, and a downward-facing camera can lose its fiducial near touchdown. This letter presents a mothership-child quadrotor system in which mission transitions are gated by complementary nonvisual sensing. An upward-facing single-beam LiDAR on the recovery deck provides a binary occupancy state using hysteresis and a debounce interval derived from the child's occluding footprint and crossing speed; real-time kinematic (RTK) positioning conditions the carrier hover; and carrier thrust provides a proprioceptive indication of load transfer. In ten outdoor missions using an F450-class mothership and a Q250-class child with camera-guided recovery, nine completed successfully while RTK-fixed positioning was maintained through release and capture. A complementary MuJoCo study of fully autonomous fiducial-based recovery achieved touchdown 0.077 m from the deck center, while marker detection was lost within the final approximately 0.2 m above the docked configuration. In simulation, carrier thrust changed by -17.0 N at release and +17.3 N at capture, agreeing within 6% with the child's 18.0 N hover thrust. The results support a sensing architecture in which vision guides the approach, while range and proprioceptive information corroborate the final docking decision.
### Title:
          SpikeOcc: Towards Resilient Multimodal 3D Occupancy Perception via Spiking Experts
 - **Authors:** Yiding Sun, Zijie Xu, Hongqiang Lin, Jiajun Cao, Dongxu Zhang, Kai Tang, Jihua Zhu, Wenxuan Liu, Zhaofei Yu, Tiejun Huang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Multimodal 3D occupancy prediction underpins safe autonomous driving through comprehensive scene understanding. However, existing methods have largely pursued higher accuracy through sophisticated fusion, leaving resilience to missing sensors underexplored. In this paper, we investigate spiking neural networks (SNNs) as a means of building more resilient multimodal perception. Still, the discrete nature of spiking neurons makes optimization challenging. To overcome this difficulty, SpikeOcc is introduced with two complementary components, a Spiking Mixture-of-Experts (SMoE) and Sensor Availability Learning (SAL). Specifically, SMoE organizes RGB, LiDAR, and joint fusion into specialized pathways, enabling full-width unimodal representations while retaining the original multimodal features via a bounded residual. Beyond sensor dropout, SAL employs a staged curriculum with explicit routing supervision, progressively improving robustness under severe sensing conditions. Remarkably, SAL also yields notable gains for conventional models, highlighting its broader potential for resilient multimodal learning. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SpikeOcc maintains competitive full-sensor accuracy while markedly raising the performance floor under sensor outages. SpikeOcc illustrates the promise of neuromorphic computing, pointing toward more reliable and energy-efficient autonomous driving.
### Title:
          Model-Based Trailer-Sway Suppression for Autonomous Passenger Vehicles
 - **Authors:** Alec McFadyen, Pablo Covarrubias De La Torre, Connor McGovern
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Systems and Control (eess.SY)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Trailer sway is a persistent contributor to severe crashes among light passenger vehicles towing trailers, yet it remains largely unaddressed by the autonomous vehicle systems now entering this new era of automation. Improper cargo placement rearward of the trailer axle reduces, and can invert, the tongue load transmitted through the hitch. The resulting loss of vertical load at the tow vehicle's rear axle diminishes the lateral tire force available there and degrades yaw stability of the coupled system, admitting a divergent lateral oscillation of the trailer known as trailer sway. Under adverse combinations of speed and loading, this oscillation can progress to jackknifing, in which the articulation angle diverges rapidly and directional control is lost. This work develops a linear state-space model of the yaw-plane dynamics of a light vehicle-trailer combination, parameterized to expose tongue load as an explicit stability-determining quantity. From this model a linear quadratic regulator is synthesized to suppress articulation-angle divergence. Because consumer trailers carry no sensors, the two trailer states the controller needs, trailer yaw rate and articulation angle, are estimated by a Kalman filter from measurements taken only on the tow vehicle. The controller is then implemented and evaluated in a MuJoCo multibody simulation across a range of longitudinal speeds and tongue-load conditions, including the negative-tongue-load regime. Results show a significant decrease in trailer oscillation when controlling a given vehicle's steering with an LQR using tow-vehicle IMU and LiDAR data. These findings indicate that LQR controlled anti-sway systems are a viable control method for future autonomous vehicles.
### Title:
          PathLift: Depth-Conditioned Metric Lifting for Visual-Navigation Priors
 - **Authors:** Gwanjun Shin, Hyeongseok Suh, Hongmo Jung, Jongwoo Lim
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Generalist visual-navigation policies tell a robot which direction to go but not how far. A separate lifting step is needed at deployment to convert their unitless trajectory into a metric path that a controller can track; existing scalar lifters apply uniform scaling that misses per-waypoint corrections, while in-policy approaches that inject monocular depth into the policy inherit the prediction errors of the depth model near obstacles and at close range. We present PathLift, a per-waypoint depth-conditioned adapter that lifts a pre-trained visual-navigation policy by consuming an explicit, sensor-grade depth source (RGB-D, stereo, or projected LiDAR) and fitting per-waypoint magnitude and lateral residuals to a canonicalized prior. PathLift consistently improves closed-loop navigation across diverse visual-navigation policies in simulation and on a real wheeled robot under both stereo and LiDAR depth. By turning the policy's unitless intent into a depth-feasible metric path in a single feed-forward pass, the adapter lifts real-world success without test-time optimization.
### Title:
          Mining Cross-Modal Perception Gaps in Autonomous Driving: When Camera and LiDAR Disagree
 - **Authors:** Hongsen Cao, Mona Jaber, Shanxin Yuan, Ahmed Sayed
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Multi-modal fusion for 3D object detection assumes that cameras and LiDAR are broadly complementary. In practice, how much each modality is trusted at a given location is decided by learned weights that are neither characterized nor auditable. We label every ground-truth object by which modalities detected it, then mine the resulting 0.4M-object database across nuScenes, KITTI, and Waymo. Because the patterns shift with distance from the ego vehicle, we read the mined model through Spatially Stratified Feature Attribution (SSFA), which conditions Shapley attribution on distance zones and exposes structure that global attribution averages away. Three assumptions built into the current fusion design do not hold. The modality-preference boundary tracks the object itself: a traffic cone is nearly six times as likely as a truck to be seen by the camera alone (16.8% vs. 2.9%), regardless of range. Camera-only detections peak at 10-30 m, inside the band usually ceded to LiDAR, and fall away on either side. The drivers of failure are not additive either: the distance - density interaction alone outweighs the entire main effect of viewing direction. We further find that modality dominance is directionally non-stationary, with objects behind the vehicle being markedly more LiDAR-dominant than those in front. We compile the mined structure into a Modality Reliability Map and inject it into four pretrained fusion detectors through a lightweight attention layer. This improves performance by up to 1.3 mean Average Precision (mAP) while fine-tuning under 4% of their parameters in 2-3 GPU-hours. Our work lifts reliability out of the weights and treats it as a quantity that can be examined before deployment. That is what a safety measure for multi-modal perception must rest on.
### Title:
          LiDAR-based Humanoid Navigation in Dynamic, Non-Convex Environments via Spatio-Temporal Safe Corridors
 - **Authors:** Shubham S. Kumbhar, Panagiotis Artemiadis
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Navigating humanoid robots through dynamic, non-convex environments requires tightly coupling onboard perception with footstep planning and dynamic locomotion control. Existing methods frequently discard complex free space through simplified convex obstacle bounds and fail to account for real-time dynamic occupancy predictions. This paper presents a real-time framework for humanoid navigation that directly constructs spatio-temporal safe corridors from LiDAR-based particle representations and couples them with Linear Inverted Pendulum (LIP)-based planning and Whole Body Control (WBC) to enable navigation around and within moving non-convex structures without restrictive geometric assumptions. Simulations on the Digit humanoid yield a 96% success rate in navigating around and inside moving non-convex obstacles in a 24 by 24 m^2 arena. Ablation studies confirm that the proposed iterative polytope generation significantly outperforms single-iteration baselines in narrow corridors, while evaluations demonstrate robustness to clutter, sensor noise, control delays, and force perturbations.
### Title:
          Autonomous 3D Visual-Thermal Inspection for Utility-Scale Solar Farms
 - **Authors:** Lachlan Tychsen-Smith, Mark Cox, Brett Wood, David Haddon, Kenrick Anderson, Benjamin Duck, Jim O'Malley, Noel W Duffy, Peyman Moghadam
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Utility-scale photovoltaic (PV) inspection is dominated by aerial surveys, which are effective for identifying module-level hot cells but provide limited visibility of critical components located under the array, including connectors, cabling, fasteners and structural elements. These assets are difficult to inspect consistently at scale, and their defects are often spatially small, partially occluded and difficult to localise from isolated images. We present an automated 3D visual-thermal inspection system (Tricorder) for under-panel PV asset inspection. The approach combines LiDAR-inertial Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (SLAM) with geometrically calibrated RGB and thermal imagery and machine-learning-based object detection and segmentation. Detected assets are associated with the reconstructed 3D environment, allowing visual and thermal observations from multiple viewpoints to be spatially fused and localised. We demonstrate the approach on utility-scale PV data for MC4 connector detection and connection-state recognition, thermal anomaly identification, and detection of structural components including bolts, nuts and torque tubes. The results demonstrate a pathway from image-based inspection toward spatially grounded autonomous inspection, where defects are detected and linked to specific assets and their 3D locations within the solar farm.
### Title:
          Multimodal Fusion of RGB and Complementary Modalities for Semantic Segmentation
 - **Authors:** Hu Cao, Yifeng Cheng, Mu He, Mengyu Li, Yinlong Liu, Alois Knoll
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Multi-modal semantic segmentation adds an auxiliary sensing stream to RGB images (RGB-X), such as thermal, LiDAR, depth, event, polarization, or light field. This can improve robustness in poor lighting and under motion blur. But different sensors often produce features that do not match well, and fusion can become unstable. To alleviate these issues, we propose a bidirectional polarity-aware cross-modality fusion (BPCF) module that effectively captures complementary cues while enhancing feature alignment. We evaluate the framework on six modality pairings: RGB-Thermal, RGB-LiDAR, RGB-Depth, RGB-Event, RGB-Polarization, and RGB-Light Field. Our model achieve state-of-the-art (SOTA) results on eight public datasets, including MFNet, KITTI-360, DELIVER, DDD17, DSEC, MCubeS, ZJU, and UrbanLF. The codes and trained models will be released upon acceptance.

### Title:
          RootSLAM: Square Root Bundle Adjustment for Visual-Inertial SLAM
 - **Authors:** Tomás Santucci, Mateo de Mayo, Daniel Cremers, Taihú Pire
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Efficient perception is a prerequisite for the small, low-power devices targeted by robotics, mixed reality, autonomous driving, and drone applications, all of which rely on Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (SLAM) to estimate their motion within their surroundings. Learning-based SLAM systems achieve state-of-the-art accuracy but are demanding in compute, whereas classical geometry-based methods retain a strong efficiency advantage with little to no loss in accuracy. In this work we present RootSLAM, a fully-featured visual-inertial SLAM system for multi-camera setups built on Basalt. RootSLAM achieves accuracy competitive with the state of the art while running up to 20 times faster, facilitating the deployment of perception systems in resource constrained environments. We adapt the square-root bundle adjustment formulation from structure from motion into the multi-camera visual-inertial SLAM setting through a novel reformulation that leverages the system's pose-graph, letting the global optimizer run in single-precision floating point, thus doubling its performance. We provide a thorough benchmark against the state of the art together with an ablation study of our contributions. Our new implementation, including its map management and loop detection module, pose-graph optimizer, and global mapping components, will be open-sourced.

### Title:
          Quantum Annealing for Resource Allocation in Next-Generation Wireless Communication Networks: A QUBO Modeling Guide
 - **Authors:** Nguyen Xuan Tung, Le Tung Giang, Mai Dinh Cong, Pham Dang Anh Duc, Nguyen Hoang Viet, Trinh Van Chien, Lajos Hanzo
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Information Theory (cs.IT)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Quantum annealing (QA) offers a physics-inspired approach for such subproblems by mapping them onto an Ising Hamiltonian, which is algebraically equivalent to a Quadratic Unconstrained Binary Optimization (QUBO) model executable on quantum annealers or hybrid solvers. However, their practical employment in wireless systems critically depends on the specific problem formulation, on the particular variable encoding, constraint handling, solver selection, hardware embedding, and fair benchmarking. This paper develops wireless-oriented QUBO modeling guidelines for QA-assisted resource allocation. We first present a beginner-friendly toy example in which a terrestrial-aerial resource-allocation problem with a fractional signal-to-interference-plus-noise ratio (SINR) maximization objective is reformulated into a two-bit QUBO via Boolean interpolation, converted into an Ising representation, and decoded back into a wireless scheduling decision. The same scheduling is applicable to UAV, aircraft, satellite, and terrestrial links in SAGIN systems. We then review the QA foundations and solver ecosystems, including binary quadratic, discrete quadratic, constrained quadratic, and hybrid models from the perspective of NG wireless optimization. Furthermore, we discuss both binary and discrete encoding, penalty-based constraint conversion, embedding overhead, solution decoding, and feasibility verification. A QUBO Compatibility Index is introduced as a lightweight diagnostic metric for assessing the associated QUBO formulation readiness, rather than a predictor of runtime or solution quality. Finally, a case study on max-min uplink power control in cell-free massive MIMO illustrates how a fairness-oriented wireless problem can be discretized, constrained, converted into the concept of a QUBO, and evaluated against classical baselines. A host of key challenges and future directions are also discussed.
### Title:
          Speech event detector: Timestamped detection of non-verbal vocalizations and background sounds for rich speech transcription
 - **Authors:** Ayush Kumar Bar, Promila Ghosh
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Sound (cs.SD)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Automatic speech recognition produces words but drops the non-verbal content of a recording: the breaths, coughs, sighs and laughter of the speaker, and the horns, music or barking around them. We describe Speech Event Detector, a 5.8-million-parameter convolutional-Transformer model that outputs probabilities every 40 ms for 54 event classes (20 speaker vocalizations and 34 background sounds), together with post-processing that converts these probabilities into timestamped events and a procedure for merging the events into a Whisper transcript. The model is trained on material drawn from seven public datasets whose labels come in incompatible forms: single detected regions, free-text tags with timestamps, temporally strong ontology labels, symbols embedded in transcripts, isolated recordings and environmental recordings. We unify them using rule-based label mapping, recover timestamps for transcript-embedded labels through CTC forced alignment, locating 6,360 of 8,620 sounds, and combine about 168 hours of real annotated training recordings with synthetic mixtures and augmentation using real background noise. Per-class thresholds for two operating profiles are tuned on real held-out recordings, and performance is estimated using two-fold cross-validation. On whole held-out recordings from four datasets, considering the 48 classes with at least ten held-out events, the sensitive profile detects an average of 49 percent of a class's events, covering 67 percent of all 9,367 events, at a macro precision of 0.30. The balanced profile achieves a macro recall of 0.37 at a precision of 0.36, with macro event F1 scores of 0.32 and 0.33, respectively. The detector runs about 39 times faster than real time on eight CPU threads.
### Title:
          Mapping Tire-Road Friction Coefficients Along Driven Trajectories
 - **Authors:** Risto Ojala
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Information of the tire-road friction coefficient is critical for intelligent vehicle systems, as friction establishes the limits at which the vehicle can be manoeuvred without loss of control. While there is extensive literature on online friction estimation, no previous studies have explored post-processing and mapping friction coefficients along a driven trajectory based on recorded sensor data. Mapped friction information could be shared by connected vehicles, enabling proactive measures in low-friction areas. This paper presents an offline post-processing method, FrictionMap, for mapping friction coefficients from recorded driving data. The method operates on commonly available on-board vehicle sensor information, and requires only a basic vehicle parameter set and a slip threshold as prior knowledge. FrictionMap runs an extended Kalman filter combined with a Rauch-Tung-Striebel smoother to estimate vehicle dynamics. Data points where the friction limit is observable are identified by filtering points based on tire slip. A Gaussian process is fitted to these points to acquire a continuous estimate of the friction coefficient along the trajectory. FrictionMap is validated via a simulation study, and shown to outperform commonly used online friction estimation techniques. The code implementation and the simulation benchmark are released as open-source after paper acceptance to enable further research.
### Title:
          Controllable Diverse Sampling through Pullback Directions
 - **Authors:** Sylvain Dehayem Kenfouo, Aaron Zweig, Mingxuan Zhang, Elham Azizi
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Conditional diffusion models can generate high-quality images that align closely with a given prompt. However, samples generated from different random seeds may remain visually similar, limiting creative exploration. Existing approaches can increase variation, but often require additional optimization or sampling overhead, while stronger perturbations may introduce distortions that degrade image quality or prompt alignment. We propose a training-free method that leverages the local pullback geometry induced by the mapping from the conditioning-embedding space to the image space to identify directions of maximal output sensitivity. By steering different sampling trajectories along these directions, we encourage distinct generations while maintaining consistency with the conditioning signal. Experiments on MS-COCO and BrushNet show the efficacy of the method in improving diversity on prompt-conditioned image generation and inpainting.
### Title:
          GMM: Generalized OS Memory Management for Programmable XPUs
 - **Authors:** Weixi Zhu, Bin Wang, Zhiyuan Fang, Jiahua Wang, Yao Guo, Gingfung Yeung, Adam Barker, Xinwei Hu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Operating Systems (cs.OS)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Since the AI boom, the fast evolving programmable XPU devices utilize high-bandwidth local memory to accelerate domain-specific tasks offloaded from the host CPU, resulting in a proliferation of XPU memory management systems implemented in proprietary drivers. However, existing OS core memory management (MM) continues to lack a high-level software abstraction, excluding the XPUs from leveraging OS advanced memory features. The XPU manufacturers have to reimplement independent XPU MM drivers, exacerbating the fragmented XPU software ecosystem. This paper presents GMM, generalized memory management that provides unified MM subsystems for XPUs. By adding an explicit logical mapping abstraction, GMM 1) provides universal NUMA-aware memory API for XPUs; 2) allows ease of XPU MM delegation; 3) enables sharing of advanced XPU memory features. Lightweight XPU MM drivers are implemented for two representative XPU manufacturers, Nvidia and Huawei. Both GMM-based drivers obtain competitive performance compared with their vendor-supplied drivers. GMM also obtains up to 41% higher fault-driven page migration throughput. Two real-world industrial case studies are exercised, where GMM reduces XPU memory utilization by 10% with up to 22.6x faster inference serving switch latency.
### Title:
          Global Optimality of Pose Graph Optimization
 - **Authors:** Shuan Ding, Ruishuang Chen, Mengfan Cao, Zaiyue Yang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Signal Processing (eess.SP)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Pose Graph Optimization (PGO) is a classical and widely applied problem in state estimation for robotics, playing a fundamental role in localization, mapping, and navigation. Yet, it is nonconvex in nature and may have many local minimizers, thus difficult to analyze. In this paper, we investigate the optimization landscape and establish a sufficient condition of global optimality for PGO. The ultimate goal of this paper is to theoretically confirm that the optimization landscape is benign and attractive around the global minimizer, if noise is smaller than an explicit and a priori bound. To reveal such an insight, this paper develops three main technical results. First, we identify a proper basin of attraction (BOA) containing global minimizer, and characterize its geometric property by showing that its radius is controlled by noise in an explicit way. Then, by exploring the first order optimality conditions of PGO, we prove that a local minimizer is globally optimal if a noise-controlled criterion is satisfied. Finally, by combining above two results, we prove that one and only one local minimizer exists in the BOA and this minimizer is globally optimal, when the noise is smaller than an explicit bound that can be verified a priori. Numerical simulations validate the proposed theory under various random cases, showing that the obtained conditions provide an effective criterion for certifying the global optimality of PGO.
### Title:
          Learning Optimal Robust-Pathwise Control of Elliptic Equations with Random Coefficients
 - **Authors:** Qi Sun, Yanhan Cui, Ling Guo, Lili Ju
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Numerical Analysis (math.NA)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Conventional control approaches to optimization problems under uncertainty entail a practical compromise: deterministic controls offer robustness at the expense of pathwise accuracy while stochastic controls can adapt to individual realizations but suffer from the curse of dimensionality. To achieve both robustness and adaptivity, a neural operator-based approach is proposed in this paper for distributed elliptic optimal control problems with high-dimensional random inputs. A data-driven training algorithm is first established to learn the mapping from coefficient realizations directly to their optimal pathwise controls. To bypass the burden of generating training data pairs, a physics-informed training algorithm is then proposed and recast as weakly supervised learning using pseudo label targets produced by standard adjoint-based control updates. Numerical experiments with rough coefficients are conducted to validate the effectiveness and efficiency of our robust-pathwise controls, which outperforms the benchmark robust deterministic control on unseen coefficient realizations owing to the generalization capability and fast inference of trained models.
### Title:
          Training-Free BioNEL-RAG: Advancing Biomedical Entity Linking through Retrieval-Augmented Generation and Large Language Model
 - **Authors:** Ziyi Chen, Hui Liu, Ziyang Ling, Ronald X.Xu, Mingzhai Sun
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Biomedical Named Entity Linking (BioNEL) is the task of mapping mentions extracted from biomedical texts to canonical entities or concepts in the standardized knowledge base. Although existing methods have achieved remarkable performance in mapping accuracy, most rely on training with domain-specific datasets, which fundamentally limits their cross-domain adaptability. To address this limitation, we propose BioNEL-RAG, a novel training-free framework that combines retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) with large language models (LLMs). Within this framework, the RAG module focuses on retrieving candidate entities, while the large language model module plays the role of selecting the most appropriate candidate for entity normalization, which is based on the analysis of the mention's context, with the aid of its remarkable text comprehension capabilities. Experimental results demonstrate that BioNEL-RAG achieves performance comparable to existing methods without requiring training on subdomain datasets, whereas these methods necessitate fine-tuning on specific subdomains such as genes, diseases, or chemical compounds.
### Title:
          Accelerating MoE Training through Expert Placement and Replica Coordination
 - **Authors:** Zhiqiang Tan, Maoxin Wang, Wenxiang Lin, Sijie Wang, Qiang Wang, Shaohuai Shi, Bo Li
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Distributed, Parallel, and Cluster Computing (cs.DC)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Distributed training of sparsely activated mixture-of-experts (MoE) models is often bottlenecked by workload imbalance and all-to-all (A2A) communication induced by dynamic token routing. Existing token deduplication, expert placement, and replication techniques alleviate these bottlenecks but do not jointly capture their distinct load semantics. Under hierarchical token deduplication, communication counts each token once per destination group at each hierarchy level, whereas expert computation processes every token-expert assignment. With replication, both costs depend jointly on physical layout and token-to-copy mapping, making separate optimization suboptimal. We present PlaceMoE to address this coupling. First, we formulate the joint optimization of expert layout and source-aware token-to-copy mapping for E experts distributed across P devices, accounting for both hierarchical communication traffic and expert-compute load. Second, we develop a solver that combines co-selection-aware expert grouping, bounded dynamic programming for replica allocation, and alternating refinement of layout and mapping. Third, PlaceMoE overlaps replica-gradient synchronization with attention backward. We evaluate PlaceMoE on a 32-GPU cluster and a 64-NPU cluster using Qwen3-VL and DeepSeek-V3. Experimental results show that PlaceMoE achieves up to a 6.94x speedup in A2A communication and 1.74-2.33x speedups in end-to-end training over the state-of-the-art training framework VeOmni.
### Title:
          A Reference Architecture for Integrating Agentic AI into Enterprise Software: Responsibilities, Interactions, and Variability
 - **Authors:** Mingqin Yu, Fethi Rabhi, Liming Zhu, Qinghua Lu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Agentic AI moves generative AI applications toward systems that coordinate reasoning, enterprise context, model inference, and external actions. Integrating these capabilities into enterprise software introduces diverse architectural concerns, from orchestration, knowledge and memory, and model access to tools and actions, security, runtime infrastructure, and evaluation. Existing research and industry architectures address many of these concerns but differ in scope, abstraction, responsibility organization, interaction representation, and assumptions about which capabilities are required. This paper presents a technology-neutral reference architecture for enterprise agentic AI integration. We synthesize architectural capabilities from research publications, industry architecture guidance, and interoperability specifications by architectural purpose, organize them into ten responsibilities, and define boundary invariants, typed cross-responsibility interactions, and variation points that distinguish responsibilities from condition-dependent capabilities and replaceable realization mechanisms. We instantiate the architecture as an ESG portfolio-analysis and pre-trade compliance agent over an existing service-oriented ESG metric computation and reporting system, and complement the case with a mapping of an independently published agentic architecture. The case realizes seven of the ten responsibilities; different questions take different interaction paths, controls activate only under their conditions, and the probed boundary invariants hold. The independent architecture maps onto the proposed responsibilities without requiring an additional one. These results provide initial evidence for the architecture's organization and applicability rather than its completeness. The deployment and recorded results are available as a replication package.
### Title:
          BSIPipe: Bidirectional Sequence-Level Interleaved Pipeline Parallelism for Long-Sequence Large Language Model Training
 - **Authors:** Yuzhou Ni, Ling Chen
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Distributed, Parallel, and Cluster Computing (cs.DC)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Pipeline parallelism has become a critical technique for training large language models. To address the throughput limitation of existing sequence-level methods that schedule subsequences unidirectionally, we propose bidirectional sequence-level interleaved pipeline parallelism (BSIPipe), which co-schedules two opposing V-shaped interleaved pipelines at the subsequence level. To bound the activation accumulation from subsequence scaling, BSIPipe introduces a basic-unit concatenation mechanism that keeps activation memory invariant to the subsequence count. To eliminate communication deadlock risk from bidirectional scheduling, BSIPipe employs a communication optimization strategy that combines topology-aware device mapping with communication-computation overlap. In addition, BSIPipe composes with tensor parallelism to overcome the memory limitation of single-dimensional parallelism, neutralizing its bidirectional memory overhead while maintaining throughput. Experiments on GPT and LLaMA3 demonstrate that BSIPipe achieves average speedups of 1.11$\times$ to 1.13$\times$ in long-sequence training over SOTA sequence-level baselines.
### Title:
          Compositional Average Velocity Modeling for Efficient Diffusion Offline Reinforcement Learning
 - **Authors:** Thanh Nguyen, Abu Hanif Muhammad Syarubany, Hwanhee Kim, Chang D. Yoo
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Systems and Control (eess.SY)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Diffusion-based policies have recently emerged as a powerful paradigm for offline reinforcement learning, but their reliance on multi-step denoising introduces substantial computational overhead and requires backpropagation through time during training, limiting scalability and practical deployment. In this work, we propose \textbf{Instant Flow Q-Learning (InsQL)}, a single-step generative policy learning framework that retains the expressive capacity of diffusion models while eliminating iterative sampling and BPTT. InsQL builds on the flow-matching perspective of diffusion policies, but replaces marginal velocity parameterization with \emph{average-velocity} modeling and introduces a principled compositional mechanism, yielding a theoretically grounded objective that learns average velocity consistently across scales. This formulation provides a direct noise-to-action mapping and enables single-step action generation. InsQL further unifies compositional average-velocity modeling with behavior-regularized actor--critic optimization in a single framework, requiring only a single policy network and avoiding distillation, multi-stage procedures, and Jacobian computations during training. At inference time, actions are generated in a single forward pass and can be refined via value-guided candidate selection to improve performance. Extensive experiments on standard offline RL benchmarks and real-world robotic manipulation tasks show that InsQL matches or surpasses state-of-the-art diffusion-based methods while substantially reducing both training and inference time. Overall, these results establish compositional average-velocity modeling as an efficient and scalable alternative to multi-step generative policies for offline RL.
### Title:
          Toward System One Clinical Intelligence: Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning and Counterfactual Explainability of Decision Foundation Models
 - **Authors:** S. M. Mahbub Uddin Ahmed
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Automated disease classification from patient-reported symptoms requires balancing the speed of lightweight discriminative models against the contextual understanding and latency costs of autoregressive large language models (LLMs). Decision foundation models offer an alternative by mapping representations directly to candidate classes without free-form text generation. We adapt Clef-Flash, a 9-billion-parameter decision model built on a Qwen3.5 backbone, for 24-class disease classification on the Symptom2Disease benchmark. Using 4-bit quantized low-rank adaptation (QLoRA) with differential learning rates and a stratified split of 960 training and 240 test cases, the model achieves 95.42% diagnostic accuracy and a macro F1 score of 0.9551. This improves upon the reported zero-shot baselines of 61.67% for a remote API and 65.00% for a local unadapted initialization, and outperforms the ClinicalBERT baseline (90.90%). By evaluating candidate logits in a single forward pass without autoregressive decoding, inference takes under 80 milliseconds per record on a single GPU, yielding a reported 31-fold speedup over generative LLM baselines. To examine symptom-level evidence, we introduce a Leave-One-Symptom-Out (LOSO) counterfactual perturbation framework. Removing disease-defining symptom clauses in a psoriasis example reduces predicted-class confidence from 100.00% to 0.12% and shifts the prediction to impetigo with 99.59% confidence, while removing secondary systemic symptoms produces less than 0.01% change in confidence. These findings suggest that decision-based architectures can combine the linguistic capabilities of large foundation models with low-latency inference and symptom-level interpretability for clinical triage.
### Title:
          RootSLAM: Square Root Bundle Adjustment for Visual-Inertial SLAM
 - **Authors:** Tomás Santucci, Mateo de Mayo, Daniel Cremers, Taihú Pire
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Efficient perception is a prerequisite for the small, low-power devices targeted by robotics, mixed reality, autonomous driving, and drone applications, all of which rely on Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (SLAM) to estimate their motion within their surroundings. Learning-based SLAM systems achieve state-of-the-art accuracy but are demanding in compute, whereas classical geometry-based methods retain a strong efficiency advantage with little to no loss in accuracy. In this work we present RootSLAM, a fully-featured visual-inertial SLAM system for multi-camera setups built on Basalt. ### Title:
          Using Evidence-Centered Design to Guide Prompt Engineering in Measurement: An Illustration with Automated Essay Scoring
 - **Authors:** Jiawei Xiong, Cheng Tang, Hong Jiao, Hanna Choi
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Human-Computer Interaction (cs.HC)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Generative artificial intelligence (GenAI) models, particularly large language models (LLMs), are increasingly used in educational settings for tasks such as item generation, automated scoring, and feedback delivery. The quality of these outputs depends not only on the AI model but also on how users construct the prompts that guide model behaviors. Current prompt engineering practices are largely ad hoc, relying on trial and error rather than principled design. This lack of structure introduces validity threats including construct misrepresentation, construct-irrelevant variance, and limited reproducibility. We propose that Evidence-Centered Design (ECD), a framework in educational measurement for structuring valid inferences from observed performance, can serve as a principled foundation for prompt engineering in educational applications. We map the three core ECD models, the student model, the evidence model, and the task model, onto the components of educational prompt design, and describe a workflow for ECD-based prompt engineering. We derive five design principles from this mapping and present an empirical study contrasting ECD-designed prompts with state-of-the-art (SOTA) prompting baselines on an automated scoring task. Our empirical study demonstrates the usefulness of this framework on automated scoring. Results suggest that treating prompting as assessment design rather than informal instructions strengthens the validity, reliability, and fairness of AI-generated educational outputs.
### Title:
          SuperAI: Merging Capabilities Across Heterogeneous Foundation Models
 - **Authors:** Jingtong Dou, Chuancheng Shi, Wenhua Wu, Wei Bao, Fei Shen, Zhiyong Wang, Tat-Seng Chua
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Enabling the reuse and composition of knowledge and capabilities distributed across foundation models is an important step toward building scalable artificial intelligence (AI) systems. However, architectural differences and incompatible representations make it difficult to transfer and integrate these capabilities through a unified cross-model interface. To address this challenge, we propose SuperAI, a cross-architecture multi-model fusion framework that integrates complementary capabilities from multiple source models into a host model through orthogonal alignment of low-dimensional task-relevant representations. Specifically, we first extract task-relevant representations from the source models and the host model using shared anchor samples, and apply principal component analysis (PCA) to construct low-dimensional functional subspaces with the same dimensionality. Next, we use orthogonal Procrustes mappings to align the source and host coordinate systems, establishing cross-model correspondences while preserving the internal geometry of each source subspace. Finally, we train a lightweight residual multilayer perceptron (MLP) with joint supervision on task outputs and source-model increments, learning input-dependent residual increments that are injected into the host model for multi-model capability fusion. Experiments on three host models across 15 capability domains show that SuperAI achieves higher average domain scores than the evaluated cross-architecture fusion methods and better preserves general capabilities as the number of source models increases. Ablation studies further support the effectiveness of orthogonal alignment, nonlinear residual mapping, and joint supervision.
### Title:
          StimFlow: Synthesizing Brain Activity from Images via Stimulus-conditioned Flow Matching
 - **Authors:** Bui Van Hung, Dang Thanh Vu, Hoang-Son Vo, Mai Duc Minh Huy, Mai Tien Dung, Soo-Hyung Kim
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 The inherent variability of fMRI responses across trials encourages a generative view of the stimulus-to-response mapping. Prior generative encoders often operate in compressed latent spaces and condition predominantly on semantic features, which provide limited low-level cues for early visual cortex. We propose StimFlow, which synthesizes fMRI directly in native voxel space using conditional flow matching with a linear path. This design aligns training and inference distributions, enabling competitive performance with a single sample and reducing reliance on averaging. To match the visual hierarchy, StimFlow combines structural DINOv2 features with semantic CLIP features, which prove complementary along the cortical processing stream. With a single sample, StimFlow achieves a per-voxel Pearson correlation of 0.371, matching baselines that average five draws. Generation in voxel space allows direct ROI analysis and concept localization without a decoder. Furthermore, it recovers over half of full-data performance from just one hour of scans, supporting efficient cross-subject adaptation.
### Title:
          LERR-Nav: Learning to Coordinate Exploration, Recovery, and Reminiscing for Object Navigation
 - **Authors:** Jingzhi Huang, Junkai Huang, Yuhang Peng, Keyu Mu, Hailong Huang, Haoyang Yang, Renbo Luo, Lujia Wang, Yi Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Object Navigation in unseen environments remains a fundamental challenge in embodied AI, as it requires agents to generalize visual and spatial reasoning to novel layouts. While recent advances in large language model (LLM) and vision-language model (VLM) have enhanced semantic mapping, spatial memory, and waypoint selecting for navigation, successful navigation requires more than selecting the next promising waypoint. A robot must also recognize stalled progress, escape unproductive behaviors, and revisit overlooked target cues. Addressing these challenges requires both specialized navigation strategies and a high-level policy that coordinates their execution. Motivated by the analysis of failed navigation episodes, we propose LERR-Nav, an object navigation framework that coordinates exploration, recovery, and reminiscing through a learned high-level policy. The framework comprises four complementary navigation strategies: value-function-guided exploration, LLM-guided exploration, recovery from stalled or repetitive behavior, and memory-guided reminiscing. To support history-aware coordination, we maintain the execution memory that preserves textual summaries of all strategy executions alongside a sliding window of map sequences. To learn the coordination policy, we initialize a high-level vision-language agent from Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct and train it through a two-stage curriculum. The first stage uses map-centric supervised fine-tuning (SFT) to establish spatial grounding. The second stage performs SFT on variable-length navigation segments to learn which navigation strategy to invoke next based on current maps and the hybrid execution memory. Experiments on HM3D and MP3D demonstrate state-of-the-art navigation performance, while ablation studies validate the contributions of individual components.
### Title:
          Have You Seen It Clearly? Uncertainty-Guided Belief Refinement for Zero-Shot Object Navigation
 - **Authors:** Jingzhi Huang, Junkai Huang, Yuhang Peng, Keyu Mu, Jiaru Zhong, Yi Wang, Lujia Wang, Lap-Pui Chau, Siyang Gao
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Zero-Shot Object Navigation (ZSON) requires an agent to locate target objects in unfamiliar environments without additional training for the test categories or environments. Recent approaches leverage large language models and vision-language models to construct spatial representations and guide exploration. However, whether the available evidence sufficiently supports a navigation choice, and how new evidence changes the search value of previously visited locations, remain insufficiently addressed. We propose RefBeliefMap, an uncertainty-guided mapping framework that distinguishes target-location belief from spatial and semantic evidence uncertainty. By jointly modeling intra-observation uncertainty and inter-observation disagreement over a sliding memory window, RefBeliefMap assesses the reliability of both evidence channels. When competing frontiers remain insufficiently resolved, these uncertainty estimates guide active observation and semantic reasoning to refine the evidence supporting target-location beliefs before navigation commitment. Building on the refined map, we introduce RefBeliefNav, which jointly evaluates unexplored frontiers and historical waypoints according to their target-finding potential, expected information gain, and navigation cost. This unified objective allows historical locations to be reconsidered as new evidence changes their search value, coordinating exploration and revisitation. Experiments on HM3D and MP3D demonstrate state-of-the-art performance among the evaluated zero-shot baselines, with ablation studies examining the contributions of modules. Real-world experiments on a Unitree Go2 quadruped robot further demonstrate the sim-to-real transferability of our method.
### Title:
          Training-Data Extraction from Genomic Language Models
 - **Authors:** Tianhao Mao, Zihao Wang, Dongfang Zhao, Rui Zhu, Xiaofeng Wang, Haixu Tang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Cryptography and Security (cs.CR); Genomics (q-bio.GN)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Genomic language models generate long nucleotide continuations from short prefixes, exposing a direct route for studying training-sequence extraction through the generation interface. We present an output-only black-box attack on Evo 2 that selects convergent groups of repeated generations without gradients, token likelihoods, or training-corpus access during candidate selection. Recovered content is then scored by an evaluator combining aligned length, nucleotide identity, match multiplicity, and sequence complexity. Across 304 runs on Evo 2 7B with bacteriophage and plasmid prefixes, the attack yields 888 candidate clusters, of which 85 match training segments of at least 500 bp, giving a cluster-level attack success rate of 9.57%. Final filtering retains 22 uniquely matched extraction events at the 500-bp threshold and 15 at 1,000 bp, covering 14 and 7 distinct training-sequence regions, respectively. Within-category source-homology screening retains 11 of the 22 events at a 99% exclusion threshold and four at 98%, with the latter mapping to two reference regions. The results establish convergence among repeated nucleotide generations as a practical output-only signal for recovering long training-sequence segments and characterize how recovery depends on prefix length, cluster size, perplexity, early stopping, and sequence domains.
### Title:
          A Decade of PhotoMappers: A Longitudinal Study of Crowdsourced Disaster Photos Geolocalization with Vision-Language Models and Geospatial Reasoning
 - **Authors:** Hao Li, Wenping Yin, Fabian Deuser, Jia Jia, Ziqi Liu, Levente Juhász, Fan Zhang, Filip Biljecki
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Crowdsourced disaster imagery provides timely, ground-level observations when conventional disaster mapping is delayed or incomplete, yet its geographic value depends on where observations are produced, how they are interpreted, and whether they can be reliably located as actionable Geospatial Intelligence (GEOINT). Using more than a decade of PhotoMappers, a crowdsourced disaster-mapping initiative, this study examines the changing geography of disaster information and asks how emerging GeoAI models compare with human geographic interpretation. We combine longitudinal spatial analysis with cross-view geolocalization using street view imagery (SVI) and remote sensing imagery (RSI), and evaluate vision-language models (VLMs) reasoning through 30 indicators spanning geographic cues and reasoning evidence. The results reveal a geographically and temporally uneven archive shaped by changing disaster types, Community Lifelines, and operational priorities.
