Integrating VGI, SVI, and RSI with Sinkhorn re-ranking achieves 40.90% R@1, 61.10% R@5, and 69.45% R@10, while yearly R@1 varies markedly from 25.00% to 65.07%. Geographic reasoning is similarly uneven: Qwen3.6-27B achieves an overall agreement of 67% compared with 51% for InternVL3-8B, with both models showing greater difficulty with localized and place-specific evidence than broad spatial configurations. We argue that the value of GeoAI lies less in replacing volunteered geographic interpretation than in complementing it, scaling candidate retrieval while retaining human judgment for ambiguous and consequential cases. This provides a pathway toward more human-centered and responsible GeoAI for disaster mapping. All data and code are openly available at this https URL.
### Title:
          Rethinking Supervision in Document Unwarping: A Self-consistent Flow-free Approach
 - **Authors:** Shaokai Liu, Hao Feng, Wengang Zhou
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 In recent years, the proliferation of smartphones has led to an upsurge in the digitization of document files via these portable devices. However, images captured by smartphones often suffer from distortions, thereby negatively affecting digital preservation and downstream applications. To address this issue, we introduce DRNet, a novel deep network for document image rectification. Our approach is based on three key designs. Firstly, we exploit the intrinsic geometric consistency inherent in document images to guide the learning process of distortion rectification. Secondly, we design a coarse-to-fine rectification network to leverage the representations derived from the distorted document image, thereby enhancing the rectification result. Thirdly, we propose a unique perspective for supervising the learning of rectification networks, where undistorted document images are employed for supervision, which is free of warping mesh as ground truth in existing methods. Technically, both low-level pixel alignment and high-level semantic alignment jointly contribute to the learning of the mapping relationship between deformed document images and distortion-free ones. We evaluate our method on the challenging DocUNet Benchmark dataset, where it sets a series of state-of-the-art records, demonstrating the superiority of our approach compared to existing learning-based solutions. Additionally, we conduct a comprehensive series of ablation experiments to further validate the effectiveness and merits of our method.
### Title:
          BridgeCodec: Mamba Enhanced Neural Audio Codec with Schrödinger Bridge at Low Bitrate
 - **Authors:** Zijian Lin, Jing Yang, Jinghao Luo, Zhuo Wang, Fan Fan, Zhiyong Wu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Sound (cs.SD); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Machine Learning (cs.LG); Audio and Speech Processing (eess.AS)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Neural audio codecs achieve remarkable performance but operate as closed ecosystems with rigidly co-trained encoderdecoder pairs, severely hindering interoperability. To resolve this, we propose BridgeCodec, a versatile framework that decouples these pairs by translating latent representations between mismatched, frozen endpoints. We formulate this cross-codec translation as a Schrödinger Bridge optimal transport problem, enabling robust mapping between disparate distributions without predefined noise priors. To efficiently capture longrange speech temporal dependencies, we integrate a Mambaenhanced U-Net architecture. We validate BridgeCodec on an extreme deployment scenario: mapping a lightweight 8 kHz source encoder directly to a 48 kHz target decoder at an ultralow bitrate of 1 kbps. Experimental results demonstrate that BridgeCodec achieves superior wideband reconstruction and high perceptual quality, successfully bridging heavily bottlenecked, heterogeneous codecs.
### Title:
          Concept Representations in Vector Space: Quantifying Sharedness and Idiosyncrasy of Meaning
 - **Authors:** Simon Josef Durstewitz, Denis Buehler
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Concepts are the basic units through which we navigate the world and communicate meaning. But do you and I really mean the same thing when we think or speak about a concept? The present study develops a measurement framework for modeling and analyzing interindividual variation in concept representations through sentence embeddings, dense vector representations mapping entire utterances into a shared geometric space. The framework is developed in a theoretical argument and validated, demonstrated, and subjected to robustness checks in a pre-registered study. Short personal definitions, elicited from $N = 125$ participants, are encoded as sentence embeddings and submitted pairwise to a crossed random effects model yielding two concept-level quantities: sharedness ($S_b$), the expected dissimilarity between two randomly drawn individuals, and idiosyncrasy ($I_b$), the person-specific share of total dyadic variance. Two validity tests support the approach: the embedding space fully recovers concept and domain organization from participant definitions even with the target word excluded, and a parameter-free classifier achieves 0.630 accuracy in a within-participant extensional prediction task against a chance baseline of 0.500 and a consistency ceiling of 0.893. The framework finds theoretically interpretable variation in $S_b$ and establishes $I_b$ as a reliable source of dyadic variance across concepts. Results replicate across three encoders varying in architecture and capacity, providing a reproducible and scalable instrument for the empirical study of meaning variation.
### Title:
          A Step in the Right Place: Understanding and Optimizing Timestep Placement in Continuous Diffusion Language Models
 - **Authors:** Shutian Zheng, Zhihong Yu, Linye Wei, Zeyang Zhang, Meng Li
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Continuous diffusion language models represent an emerging paradigm for language modeling. They generate text in parallel by iteratively refining continuous representations and mapping them back to tokens through a decoder. However, realizing the efficiency advantage of parallel generation requires maintaining generation quality with only a few denoising steps. Even when the number of steps is fixed, their placement along the denoising trajectory remains flexible. In this paper, we demonstrate that changing timestep placement alone can substantially alter the final latent representations and thereby affect the decoded token distributions. Building on these findings, we introduce StepTune, an algorithm that searches for timestep placements that improve generation quality. The search is conducted on a small calibration set and guided by a language-aware objective, with the model and sampling budget fixed. Once calibrated, the timestep placement is fixed and reused across inputs. Experiments demonstrate consistent improvements in unconditional generation perplexity and conditional task metrics across all evaluated sampling budgets. In particular, StepTune enables 16-step sampling with a frozen model to outperform its native 64-step baseline on both XSum summarization and WMT14 translation and reduces end-to-end latency by approximately $3.8\times$. These results establish timestep placement as a practical means of improving the quality-efficiency trade-off in continuous diffusion language models.
### Title:
          ELECT: Settling Identity Before Rendering for Scalable Story Visualization
 - **Authors:** Mehrajul Abadin Miraj, Md. Mahmudul Hasan, Meherun Farzana, Aniket Joarder, Mahmudul Hasan, Abir Chakraborty Partha, Md. Ahasanul Alam, Redwan Ahmed Rizvee, Md. Fahim Arefin, Md Mahmudur Rahman, Md. Tanvir Alam, Md Mosaddek Khan
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Training-free story visualization without reference images must preserve recurring entities across frames while scaling to long narratives. Existing methods typically maintain consistency by conditioning each frame on other frames of the same story, coupling generation time, VRAM, or both to story length. We introduce ELECT, which instead settles cross-frame identity before final rendering through a language-guided scene-entity mapping and stationary anchors elected from the model's own drafts. This makes the per-frame cost of the final rendering pass independent of story length. Joint attention exceeds 96 GB of VRAM at 32 scenes, whereas ELECT remains at 8.3 s per frame and under 20 GB VRAM from 10 to 1000 scenes. On the Story50 long-story benchmark, ELECT reaches 0.833 average Character Consistency Similarity (aCCS), compared with 0.826 for ten-round refinement, at one seventh of its generation cost, with lower text alignment (0.327 vs. 0.340). At 1000 scenes, to our knowledge, the first evaluation of training-free story visualization at this length, ELECT exceeds ten-round refinement by 0.017 aCCS and the nearest flat-cost baseline by 0.091, while also surpassing refinement in text alignment at one twenty-fourth of its generation time. These results show that long-horizon consistency need not require increasingly expensive inter-frame conditioning as stories grow.
### Title:
          An Attention-Guided Graph Neural Network Surrogate for Boundary Integral Computation in Physical Field Reconstruction
 - **Authors:** RongZe Gao, YuWei Wu, XinRan Ji, XingYue Ren
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 In practical engineering and scientific computing, a real physical system can be reduced to a boundary value problem, and the adequate boundary information to determine the interior spatial distribution and evolutionary state of the physical field can be solved with linear equations, alternatively, the the real physical problem can be treated with boundary problems with in adequate information problems in field of machine learning. This paper proposes a deep learning framework for the recovery of missing boundary information using an attention-guided graph neural network (GNN) surrogate. The framework extracts features from the physical information on the discretized boundary into a latent space, and the missing boundary quantities are then predicted by the network. Thus, the nonlinear mapping from known boundary quantities to missing boundary quantities is learned using a variational autoencoder (VAE) architecture, while an attention mechanism is incorporated to better model the correlations among different types of boundary information. Furthermore, the interior physical field can be reconstructed using the conventional boundary element method (BEM) integral formulation. The effectiveness of the GNN-BEM framework is evaluated using numerical examples of the two-dimensional Laplace and Poisson equations, which demonstrate good performance in reconstruction accuracy, local detail preservation, and model stability. The results show that GNN-BEM provides a feasible computational framework for boundary information recovery and fast interior physical field reconstruction for partial differential equations under complex boundary conditions.
### Title:
          Generalizable Model Editing Could Be More Than Direct Generalization
 - **Authors:** Xiaotian Ye, Chunyao Yang, Xiaohan Wang, Junkai Chen, Yongqi Duan, Mengqi Zhang, Shu Wu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 A central desideratum in model editing is generalization to complex tasks, which prior work formalizes under a direct generalization paradigm: treating and evaluating LLM behavior as an input-output mapping and expecting a factual update to alter outputs on reasoning tasks. Yet despite substantial empirical effort, the field consensus remains that such generalization is highly challenging and elusive. However, when we deploy the latest models in an in-the-wild chatbot-user setting, we surprisingly find that even classical editing methods exhibit remarkably fluent generalization behavior. This motivates us to revisit the obstacles to generalization and the limitations of the prevailing paradigm. We find that direct generalization is fundamentally constrained both theoretically and practically: theoretically, it suffers from an identifiability issue; and practically, existing approaches attempting to circumvent this are further hindered by the ambiguity of their constructed biases and by vulnerability to being overridden by other factors such as optimization dynamics. We instead identify compositional generalization, empowered by the increasingly strong reasoning and composition abilities of modern LLMs, as a theoretically grounded and empirically validated alternative. We validate this phenomenon across multiple models and editing methods, showing that such generalization behavior is widespread. Overall, we argue that generalizable model editing could be more than direct generalization, and call for a paradigm shift toward more generalizable and practically deployable knowledge update techniques.
### Title:
          Distributed Observation Scheduling and Sensor Fusion For Large Agile Constellations
 - **Authors:** Nicholas Niziolek, Ryan Draves, Nolan Ales, Nolan Stevenson, Eli Jones, Eric Frew, Nisar Ahmed
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Systems and Control (eess.SY)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 With the rapid increase in interest towards large scale earth observing satellite constellations, the problem of properly commanding and utilizing these systems has become increasingly relevant. This work focuses on a complete distributed approach to this problem, incorporating an observation task allocation and scheduling algorithm in addition to a sensor data fusion algorithm for end-to-end target coverage and processing. Observation task allocation and scheduling is performed in this work through the combination of the bid warped consensus based bundle algorithm (BWCBBA) and a lightweight observation scheduler for bid scoring. This method produces a unique mapping of tasks to command agents when allowed to iterate sufficiently. The approach to distributed data fusion in this work occurs primarily through an ownership model in which one satellite takes ownership of a system track in the constellation. After local information is shared between the sensing nodes to create the system tracks, the dedicated fusion nodes assume ownership for further information fusion and track information is disseminated throughout the constellation for data association. This paper analyzes the performance of these two algorithms over a set of increasingly decentralized architectures to highlight the performance losses compared to more traditional centralized methods.
### Title:
          Cross-Protocol Personality Assessment with LLMs: Aligning Behavioral Evidence with Human Ratings
 - **Authors:** Wei Shan, Tianyi Zhang, Yuan Zong, Wenming Zheng
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Personality assessment from asynchronous video interviews (AVIs) plays a crucial role in recruitment by enabling standardized and behaviorally grounded evaluation for job applicants. However, most of the previous works are confined to a single interview protocol. Independently collected protocols differ in question sets and question-trait mapping. Thus, the human-rated evidence from one protocol cannot be used directly under another. To address this limitation, we propose the Cross-protocol Evidence-grounded Personality Assessment (CEPA) framework, which aligns behavioral representations across interview protocols. CEPA addresses protocol heterogeneity by projecting source behavioral records into the target protocol's construct-oriented representation space. It retrieves behaviorally relevant and personality-complementary human-rated exemplars to ground an ensemble of LLM raters, which enables behavior-to-personality associations without target-specific supervision. In cross-protocol experiments, CEPA reduces average mean squared error (MSE) by 43.8% (Forward) and 17.0% (Reverse) relative to the strongest baseline. Our empirical experiments show that human-rated evidence from one protocol can support personality assessment under another without target-specific supervision or retraining.
### Title:
          From Failure Prediction to Component Selection in Semantic Segmentation
 - **Authors:** Minhui Kang, Yiyao Zhang, Peizhe Long, Jiafeng Qi, Hongyu Jin, Jiayun Zheng
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Reliable scene understanding requires deciding which predictions to trust, retain, or inspect. In semantic segmentation, component failure predictors support these decisions, but their predictive performance does not fully describe the value of the resulting selection. We develop a decision-centered evaluation framework that connects three questions: what an available score predicts, what can be learned from its existing fields, and what its ranking delivers. Across three segmentation systems on Cityscapes and ACDC at 90% retention, adapted MetaSeg reduces retained failure relative to forecast entropy in all twelve architecture-population comparisons, but does not establish a failure advantage over direct probability ranking. Image-nested source experiments reveal a different opportunity: reusing the full field set improves predictive and retained-loss performance under both learners in all three systems, while the raw OCRNet probe improves prediction with unresolved selective gains. Mapping a fixed prediction pair to conditional log-loss risk produces mixed selection effects, which we examine through the components exchanged by the two rankings. These findings distinguish the performance of a fitted score from the value obtainable from its inputs and the consequences of its use. The framework provides a practical basis for deciding whether to adopt an additional score, relearn existing fields, or revise a selection rule, and for explaining the gains and failures of component-level reliability methods.
### Title:
          Memorization in image classification: test set behavior and scaling laws
 - **Authors:** Jeevesh Attri, Florentin Guth
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Deep neural networks are capable of both learning generalizing solutions on natural tasks and memorizing arbitrary mappings. We argue that understanding the former requires understanding the latter, which is much less studied. In what ways do networks that generalize or memorize differ in their behavior or their training dynamics? Do memorizing networks simply function as a lookup table where input-output mappings are stored independently? We investigate these questions in the context of CNNs trained on image classification tasks with randomized labels or pixels. We first study the behavior of such memorizing networks on unseen images. We find that independently initialized networks trained on the same random labels learn different predictive functions: they make different predictions on test examples, in contrast with generalizing networks. We then examine the effect of dataset size on memorization through the required network capacity and training time to interpolate the training set. Counter-intuitively, we find that the number of presentations of a given example required for complete memorization can decrease with dataset size even when input-output pairs share no common structure. Although memorizing networks do not learn a universal solution, they exhibit interesting behaviors which call for further investigations into the underlying mechanisms.
### Title:
          All-St3R SLAM: Real-time RGB Dynamic SLAM with Pixel-wise Motion Masking
 - **Authors:** Yewon Kim, Paul Hongsuck Seo, Sang Chul Ahn
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 SLAM (Simultaneous Localization and Mapping) is a core technology in autonomous systems that enables them to estimate their own position and build a map of unknown environments simultaneously. SLAM systems in dynamic environments are of great practical importance. However, existing dynamic SLAM approaches still have several limitations like over-removal and high computational costs. To address these limitations, we propose a real-time RGB Dynamic SLAM framework that precisely removes moving objects at the pixel level without relying on predefined labels or segmentation models. Our system combines pixel motion predicted by ego-motion using DUSt3R with point tracking from All-Tracker, removing only actually moving pixels by analyzing discrepancies between the two motion sources. Unlike object-level masking methods, our approach eliminates only currently moving regions, allowing temporarily static parts to be utilized for tracking. Operating at approximately 10 FPS, the system achieves real-time performance on standard 30 FPS video streams by subsampling every 3 frames. Relying solely on RGB inputs, this significantly improves upon state-of-the-art dense dynamic SLAM approaches (0.5-3 FPS), presenting a highly practical solution.
### Title:
          ML-Assisted Power Side-Channel Attacks on ML Accelerators in Shared FPGAs
 - **Authors:** Anthony Etim, Kenan Erol, Jakub Szefer
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Cryptography and Security (cs.CR)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Cloud providers offer on-demand access to Field-Programmable Gate Arrays (FPGAs), and multi-tenant FPGAs let multiple users share one device to improve utilization. This sharing introduces security risks: an adversary co-located with a victim on the same FPGA can mount power side-channel attacks against the victim's Machine Learning (ML) accelerator. Prior on-chip attacks on such accelerators based on Time-to-Digital Converter (TDC) sensors have recovered only small, MNIST-scale grayscale inputs, including with ML-assisted reconstruction. This work is the first on-chip, TDC-based attack to produce full-scale color reconstructions of ImageNet-scale inputs, by combining ML-based upscaling and recoloring with TDC measurements of a remote victim's ML accelerator. We frame this as a proof-of-concept leakage study: the victim implements the first convolution layer of a CNN processing 224x224 ImageNet inputs on a shared FPGA, which we isolate to characterize the input-dependent leakage, and we quantify how much of each reconstruction reflects genuinely recovered information versus ML-generated post-processing. As a preliminary countermeasure, we study a pixel-substitution defense that replaces each pixel value, within a fixed intensity budget, with one of different Hamming Weight, producing a different TDC signature. We find that this transformation costs the victim's own model more accuracy than it costs the attacker and, because it applies the same fixed, publicly reconstructable mapping to every image, is largely defeated by an attacker retrained on the transformed data; robust defenses will require randomized or input-dependent transformations.
### Title:
          ProCARE: Real-World Study Automation via Profile-Grounded Evidence Contracts
 - **Authors:** Zhaoyu Fan, Bowen Han, Jincheng Ou, Yanwei Ren, Zehua He, Kaiyu Huang, Pengcheng He, Shanshan Ni, Chengchen Gong, Shuai Xue, Qingjiang Shi
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Real-world studies (RWS) analyze routinely collected healthcare data, such as electronic health records and insurance claims, to estimate treatment effects, monitor safety, and identify risk factors outside randomized trials. Automating RWS with large language model agents is appealing but brittle: valid analyses depend on dataset-specific clinical semantics, including index dates, follow-up windows, patient/event-level units, and outcome boundaries that are often implicit in observational data. This leads to schema mismatches, temporal errors, inconsistent analytical granularity, and unsupported clinical claims. We present ProCARE, a profile-grounded framework that reformulates RWS automation as constrained compilation. ProCARE converts heterogeneous data profiles into Evidence Contracts specifying available variables, temporal attributes, distributions, and evidentiary limits, and compiles study objectives into RWS-IR, a strongly typed intermediate representation for variables, time relations, analysis units, and statistical tasks. Deterministic validation and localized repair then check and revise generated workflows across schema mapping, cohort construction, variable derivation, analysis, and interpretation. On 36 EHR-based benchmark tasks, ProCARE outperforms strong LLM-agent baselines in code executability, semantic consistency, and conclusion traceability, with automated violation diagnoses showing moderate-to-substantial agreement with blinded expert review.
### Title:
          DIGS: Distribution-Informed Gaussian Splatting for Training-Free Open-Vocabulary 3D Segmentation
 - **Authors:** Zhifan Zeng, Wenbo Xiao, Arcot Sowmya, Changming Sun
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Open-vocabulary 3D Gaussian segmentation typically binds semantic features to the per-Gaussian state via per-scene gradient training (1-4 h) or the more recent closed-form distillation. Both commit each primitive to a single feature; therefore, boundary Gaussians spanning multiple 2D instances are forced into a hard decision that degrades retrieval accuracy and yields unstable mask boundaries at render time. We present DIGS, a training-free framework with two stages, class-agnostic mapping and multi-modal encoding, that decouples instance discovery from linguistic naming. At the Gaussian level, our distribution-informed representation maintains an explicit top-$M$ label distribution per primitive, updated by a tempered Dirichlet rule; preservation of multi-peaked label distributions at boundary primitives allows a late-argmax renderer to recover clean instance boundaries. A bidirectional state machine aggregates these distributions into a persistent class-agnostic instance ontology. The instance-level encoding stage then fuses the CLIP visual embedding of each instance's isolated 3D footprint with the CLIP-text embedding of an LLM-generated attribute description, disambiguating visually similar instances. On a single A100, DIGS completes mapping and instance encoding on LERF-OVS in 1.0-3.2 min per scene, depending on whether LLM fusion is enabled, and serves native 3D queries in 5.4 ms. It reaches 71.95% mean mIoU on LERF-OVS, outperforming both the training-free SFS and training-based LaGa baselines. DIGS also achieves the highest mean mIoU on LERF-Mask and 3D-OVS and surpasses Gaga and ObjectGS on Replica under their respective evaluation protocols. Code is available at this https URL.
### Title:
          SafeDSE: Joint Exploration of Safety Architecture Patterns, Mapping, and Scheduling for Dataflow Applications With Safety Arguments (Extended Version)
 - **Authors:** Fatemeh Bahrami, Seyed-Hosein Attarzadeh-Niaki
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Hardware Architecture (cs.AR); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Logic in Computer Science (cs.LO); Software Engineering (cs.SE)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Embedded platforms increasingly host safety-related streaming functions next to less critical ones. Whether a safety architecture pattern, such as a doer/checker pair, protects a function depends on the cores that run its components. Their schedule must meet the throughput requirement, and mixing safety integrity levels (SILs) on one core raises development cost. This report presents SafeDSE, a design space exploration framework for synchronous dataflow applications. One declarative constraint model decides the pattern of each actor, the placement that the fault model requires, the static-order schedule, and the platform, and prices SIL promotion. Guards activate the chosen patterns in a fixed graph that holds all of them, and node potentials encode throughput. An independent verifier re-checks every solution, and a generator turns its checks into evidence of a safety argument in Goal Structuring Notation. On 9 benchmark instances under period bounds, no rule that fixes patterns before mapping was both always feasible and never more expensive under conservative pricing, while joint exploration was. In the generated arguments, 49 to 62% of the leaves cite verifier checks; the rest are claims a mapping cannot discharge. Optimality within the model was proven for all synthetic graphs with up to 16 actors. This extended version adds the full model, the verifier and generator in detail, the complete catalog and parameters, and a proof of the potential characterization. It also reports every experiment per instance, a mutation analysis of the verifier, and the negative results of the development.
### Title:
          Graph3R: Unifying View Graph Initialization with Feed-Forward Reconstruction
 - **Authors:** Jingwen Yu, Hengli Wang, Jianhao Jiao, Zhonghang Liu, Ping Tan, Hong Zhang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Feed-forward reconstruction recovers camera poses and dense geometry from image sets whose views are assumed to come from a single scene. In practice, uncurated image collections often contain views from disjoint scenes, and reconstructing these views jointly can corrupt camera poses and geometry. Existing pipelines typically resolve view associations in separate stages before final reconstruction, leaving view graph initialization and reconstruction inference only loosely coupled. We introduce Graph3R, which unifies view graph initialization with feed-forward reconstruction through a shared geometric representation. A lightweight verification head predicts pairwise covisibility from the multi-view features of $\pi^3$ to construct a view graph. This graph classifies the input views into scene groups. For each group, we mask the shared features before decoding camera poses and dense geometry. Reusing the multi-view features allows Graph3R to reconstruct disjoint scenes separately with a single backbone pass. Across three benchmarks, Graph3R improves average maximum recall at 100% precision by upto 8.99 percentage points over the baseline, while improving reconstruction quality on mixed-scene inputs. Regarding the multi-session mapping task, Graph3R improves cross-session localization and achieves competitive pose estimation accuracy, while delivering a runtime speedup of approximately $35.9\times$ over the baseline when reconstructing the query group. By integrating learned view association with geometric inference, our approach takes a step toward end-to-end reconstruction from uncurated image collections.
### Title:
          From Sound to Sense: An Anatomy of Semantic Supervision for Speech-Driven Visual Grounding in Remote Sensing Imagery
 - **Authors:** Zhongyu Pang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Speech-driven visual grounding in remote sensing (RSVG) removes the typing bottleneck of text-based interaction and is intended to preserve prosodic intent, enabling hands-free target acquisition in time-critical missions. The direct-audio paradigm, mapping raw speech straight to a bounding box and bypassing automatic speech recognition (ASR), was recently adopted by RSTalker, but what such grounders actually learn about language remains unmeasured. SemEcho grafts a frozen Whisper speech pathway onto a VLTVG grounding host so that speech and text share one decoder, making the paired text, free by construction since speech corpora in this task are synthesized from DIOR-RSVG expressions, a training-time teacher and a fair, same-host text upper bound. On this instrument we dissect three semantic injection channels (feature-mimicry distillation, word-level CTC supervision, and two-stage ASR pretraining) with linear semantic probes, and release Audio-DIOR-R (80,973 training clips; 10 timbres x K=3; Qwen3-TTS synthesis with online sox noise lambda in [0, 0.4]) and Audio-DIOR-Comp, a re-split holding out 17 attribute-category combinations under a 2x2 acoustic-semantic protocol. Our anatomy yields four findings. (i) Whether direct alignment beats a domain-trained ASR cascade is decided by recognizer quality: against a weak in-domain cascade (CTC 0.53) direct wins by 9.0 Acc@0.5 points with transcription errors concentrated on spatial-relation words, but a strong in-domain cascade (CTC 0.029) reaches the text upper bound (80.8 vs. 81.0) and beats even the strongest direct model by 8.8 points; the anti-ASR premise holds only against weak recognizers.
### Title:
          StructZO: Structured Zeroth-Order Optimization for Memory-Efficient LLM Fine-Tuning
 - **Authors:** Xuanlin Yang, Jia Li, Zhouchen Lin
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Fine-tuning large language models at scale is increasingly limited by the memory cost of backpropagation. Zeroth-Order (ZO) optimizers reduce this burden by relying only on forward function evaluations, but they still exhibit a substantial performance gap to first-order (FO) optimizers such as Adam. We argue that this gap does not arise solely from the absence of explicit gradients: FO fine-tuning also benefits from informative low-rank optimization geometry, large-scale signal aggregation, and well-conditioned update directions. Motivated by this, we introduce StructZO, a structured Zeroth-Order optimization framework for memory-efficient LLM fine-tuning. StructZO performs large-query ZO estimation within an activation-induced informative subspace and applies spectral control to the resulting reduced-space update before mapping it back to the original parameters. This lets ZO aggregate useful directional signals in a compact, geometry-aware space while avoiding the activation and optimizer-state memory costs of backpropagation. We show theoretically that, when the induced subspace captures concentrated optimization-relevant directions, StructZO reduces the effective ZO estimation dimension from $mn$ to $mr$, with the remaining projection error governed by the spectral tail of the activation Gram matrix. Empirically, across OPT-series and Llama3.1-8B on diverse downstreamtasks, StructZO improves over the strongest existing ZO baselines by up to $8.7%$ relative on average and up to $26.9%$ relative on individual tasks, while remaining within $1.5%$ of FO-Adam on average. These results suggest that structured and spectrally controlled ZO updates can substantially narrow the gap between ZO and FO optimization.
### Title:
          GQL Datatypes as an RDF Vocabulary
 - **Authors:** Dominik Tomaszuk, Daniel Szałaj
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Databases (cs.DB)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 The ISO GQL standard introduces a rich value-type system for property graphs, including scalar, temporal, byte-string, list, record, and dynamic union datatypes. The vocabulary presented here additionally covers a vector extension. At the same time, RDF-based knowledge graph systems identify literal datatypes by IRIs and interpret typed literals through value spaces, lexical spaces, and lexical-to-value mappings. This paper presents the GQL Datatypes RDF Vocabulary, a lightweight RDF vocabulary that represents selected GQL datatypes and the vector extension as RDF datatype IRIs and describes their hierarchy, aliases, and selected constraints using RDF, RDFS, OWL, and XML Schema Datatypes vocabulary terms. The vocabulary is intended to support interoperability between GQL-oriented property graph systems and RDF-based knowledge graph systems. We describe its namespace and datatype model, summarize its datatype coverage, and show how the accompanying generator and validator use the vocabulary. In the reference implementation, RDF literals retain GQL datatype IRIs; XSD mappings declared in the vocabulary support scalar validation, while lists, records, and vectors are encoded directly as JSON lexical forms of vocabulary-typed literals.
### Title:
          NFMap: Topographic 2D Manifold Learning with Invertible Normalizing Flows
 - **Authors:** Héléna Alexandra Gaspar
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 We present NFMap, a dimensionality reduction method for 2D visualisation that extends Generative Topographic Mapping (GTM) with a bijective normalizing flow. Like GTM, NFMap places a fixed mixture grid prior over a 2D latent map, giving a parametric embedding, an explicit likelihood, and the ability to project new points onto an existing map; the likelihood is an effective out-of-distribution signal on chemical datasets. Unlike GTM, whose latent-to-data mapping is an RBF network of bounded flexibility, NFMap's mapping is a normalizing flow, and is therefore fully invertible: any location on the map can be carried back to data space. To alleviate map crowding on large datasets, NFMap additionally supports heavy-tailed Student-t mixture components. NFMap maps data to a latent space of the same dimension, split into a 2-dimensional navigable map and an off-manifold residual. Because the flow is bijective and its objective penalises volume contraction, variance squeezed out of the residual must reappear in the navigable map. The residual scale is the main control between local neighbourhood fidelity and global distance fidelity; its effect depends on the dimensionality of the data and is coupled to the extent of the latent grid.
### Title:
          From Suboptimal to Optimal: One-Step Latent Flow Policies for Offline Reinforcement Learning
 - **Authors:** Shenghua Lu, Zixuan Yang, Rongxu Zhao, Bohao Qu, Jifeng Hu, Hechang Chen
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Offline reinforcement learning aims to learn effective policies from fixed datasets without environment interaction. Although generative models such as diffusion and flow matching enhance policy expressiveness, they typically start from uninformative Gaussian noise and generate actions through multi-step iteration, leading to high computational costs and failing to effectively leverage suboptimal trajectories in datasets. We propose MeanFlow2Better (MF2B), which reformulates offline RL as generative decision chunk optimization from current performance status to better performance status in a high-level latent decision space. MF2B maps trajectory chunks to high-level latent decision representations through an encoder, placing generation starting points within a structured decision space sculpted by behavioral data. The model learns a shared conditional mean velocity field in this space, constructing two flow mappings: from Gaussian-Induced Decision Sources toward positive behavioral decision regions, and from suboptimal decisions toward high-value decision regions. Both flow mappings complete decision refinement through one-step ODE integration over the unit time interval $\Delta t=1$, requiring neither multi-step iterative sampling nor backpropagation through time. Furthermore, we propose hierarchical chunk-level advantage to guide the flow direction toward high-return regions, where the high-level decisions cover multiple low-level actions, leading to sequence length compression from $T$ to $K \approx T/H$ and significantly lower computational overhead. On the D4RL Gym-MuJoCo benchmark, MF2B achieves an average score of 90.91, matching or surpassing strong baselines.
### Title:
          ReForesight: Occlusion-Robust Re-Identification and Future Occupancy Forecasting for Dynamic Semantic Mapping
 - **Authors:** Didar Seyidov, Faith Ogunwoye, Hajira Amjad, Danil Tolkachev, Yuri Sarkisov, Dzmitry Tsetserukou
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Robots sharing walkways need to know where people will be, yet semantic occupancy maps show only where they are. We present ReForesight, an open-source ROS 2 toolkit that adds instance-level future occupancy and re-identification after occlusion to a particle-based semantic mapping pipeline. A 36.6k-parameter MLP predicts each tracked person or vehicle up to 3 s ahead, guarded by a Kalman fallback and a stationary gate, and the object's own points are moved to the forecast pose, forming a "ghost" point cloud in the map frame without an occupancy decoder. An optional tracker re-associates objects after occlusion using keypoint descriptors the pipeline already computes and a metric 3D gate. On four held-out CODa sequences, the MLP lowers the mean 3 s final displacement error from 0.728 m (Kalman) to 0.662 m and beats a similar-size GRU (0.692 m) on every sequence, at 0.04 ms per instance on one CPU core. Code, all models, and a ROS-free demo are released; field demonstrations use an outdoor cleaning robot.
### Title:
          An Introduction to Contract Theory for Computer Scientists
 - **Authors:** Paul Duetting, Michal Feldman, Inbal Talgam-Cohen
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Science and Game Theory (cs.GT)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 This survey introduces contract design to computer scientists: using outcome-based payments to incentivize effort. We discuss applications that motivate a computational approach to contract design, ranging from online labor markets, to incentive-aware algorithmic classification, to emerging ecosystems of AI agents. We present the classic principal-agent model and introduce basic concepts. We show how optimal contracts are computable via linear programming, and why they can be opaque and brittle. Motivated by this we turn to simpler contract formats, specifically linear contracts. We discuss approximation guarantees, and robust (max-min) optimality of such contracts. We then move beyond the single-agent setting to combinatorial contract settings with multiple agents, mapping the algorithmic landscape of tractability and approximation. Finally, we discuss partial information settings through the lens of learning algorithms, and outline open directions. The goal is a concise, accessible entry point that connects economic foundations with algorithmic tools and highlights opportunities for new theory and applications.
### Title:
          Measure Neural Mappings
 - **Authors:** Ricardo Baptista, Edoardo Calvello, Bohan Chen, Andrew Stuart, Urbain Vaes
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Machine Learning (stat.ML)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 We introduce and define measure neural mappings (MNMs), which combine measure-dependent neural transport with ensemble consistency for learning operators on probability measures. Ensemble consistency requires outputs from growing ensembles sampled from an input law to converge to the specified population output in probability. We establish quantitative continuum limits for attention on noncompact laws under strong sub-Gaussian and tail-integrability assumptions. These laws accommodate unbounded state spaces in scientific machine learning, including Bayesian inference and uncertainty quantification. For fixed self-attention transformers, the expected output Wasserstein error is bounded by the empirical input Wasserstein error plus an additional $N^{-1/2}$ attention term. These self-attention transformers are MNMs under the stated assumptions: pre-norm architectures follow from compact stability after normalization, while post-norm architectures are covered under the stated tail conditions. We prove corresponding cross-attention estimates and extend the results to multihead attention and grouped transformer evaluation, with convergence as the group size grows. For Set Transformers viewed as measure-to-vector mappings, we obtain an $N^{-1/2}$ bound on the expected Euclidean output error under the stated assumptions. Experiments on seven operators in dimensions $d=1,2,3,5,10$ support the predicted particle-count dependence, using one-dimensional Wasserstein computations and Sinkhorn approximations in higher dimensions. The observed Wasserstein and sliced Wasserstein decay rates are close to their respective theoretical sampling benchmarks.
### Title:
          A Method for Exact Enforcement of Boundary Conditions on General Curved Triangular Domains: Algorithm and Application to Scientific Machine Learning
 - **Authors:** Suchuan Dong, Yuchuan Zhang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Numerical Analysis (math.NA); Machine Learning (cs.LG); Computational Physics (physics.comp-ph)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 We present a method for enforcing exactly Dirichlet, Neumann, and Robin conditions on general triangular domains with arbitrarily curved boundaries. The algorithmic constructions developed here complements those in a previous work for general quadrilateral domains. Our method combines (i) the exact mapping between a general curved triangular domain and the standard triangle, (ii) TFC constrained expressions, and (iii) Barnhill transfinite interpolations, to provide explicit closed-form trial ansatze that mathematically satisfy the imposed boundary conditions exactly. Corner compatibility induced by the boundary conditions is pivotal, and enforcing exactly the imposed boundary conditions hinges on the exact enforcement of the induced compatibility constraints. The corner compatibility associated with the acute vertices of the standard triangle and the coupling between gradient components when the hypotenuse corresponds to a Neumann/Robin boundary pose particular challenges. Two strategies are critical to resolving these issues, by reformulating conditional corner constraints into equivalent unconditional forms and by introducing a separate TFC-style auxiliary edge construction together with an associated linear solve on the hypotenuse. We analyze the corner compatibility constraints and present formulations for the general form of functions that exactly satisfy the imposed Dirichlet, Neumann, or Robin conditions on curved triangular domains. The proposed method has been implemented using extreme learning machine type randomized neural networks for solving PDE problems. We present ample numerical experiments with linear/nonlinear PDE problems on a variety of domains with complex boundary geometries. Numerical results show that the current method has enforced the Dirichlet, Neumann, and Robin conditions on curved domain boundaries to the machine precision.
### Title:
          Act4R: Action-conditioned 4D reconstruction for kinematics-free control
 - **Authors:** Zhaoliang Zhang, Chenhao Yu, Zhimin Shao, Xijun Liu, Cheng Peng
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Precise control of movement across various robotic embodiments is a longstanding challenge. Modeling complex robotic structures requires significant time, dedicated sensors, and manual labeling. Recent advancements in 4D representation learning offer an interesting alternative: can we leverage 4D features lifted from 2D observations to densely map a robot's spatial movement based on the action signal? To this end, we propose Act4R, a framework that builds on 3D foundation model to predict a robot's 3D scene flow with any given action. Act4R introduces a Spatial-Action Encoder, which encodes the current 2D robot state and its action signals into embeddings that represent future robot state in 3D. Then, both the current and future spatial embeddings are decoded into point maps to estimate action-conditioned scene flow. To effectively learn the mapping between action and future state, Act4R introduces spatial-action fusion in its transformer-based encoder, and a spatial alignment loss that guides the encoder for future flow prediction. After training on some samples of a given embodiment, Act4R can perform inverse control by optimizing the signal to achieve the desired scene flow. We evaluate Act4R on a public real-world dataset and synthetic data collected in simulator. Experiments on various robotic platforms show that Act4R outperforms the previous SoTA in scene flow prediction. Our method also performs better in inverse control, both on the action accuracy metric and in closed-loop control in simulation.
### Title:
          Gaussian Splatting for Real-Time Scene Reconstruction: Methods, Systems, and Trade-offs
 - **Authors:** Ashim Dahal, Rabab Abdelfattah, Nick Rahimi
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) allows for high-quality real-time novel-view synthesis. Since its inception in 2023, its explicit and highly configurable scene representation has been extended toward sparse-view generation, dynamic reconstruction, efficiency and compression, streaming, and simultaneous localization and mapping (SLAM). Although Gaussian Splatting can provide real-time rendering, rendering an existing representation is distinct from constructing, updating, or maintaining the scene itself. In this survey, we synthesize current literature, categorize them and provide a connection of current approaches and tools towards an end-to-end pipeline. Explicitly, we categorize GS scene reconstruction into five overlapping families: single-shot generation, dynamic representations, efficiency and compression, streaming and immersive systems, and Gaussian Splatting SLAM. We distinguish render-only throughput from task-specific scene generation, update, tracking, mapping, and optimization costs, and combine controlled sparse-view benchmarking with native evaluations to identify cross-family quality, latency, memory, and update-cost trade-offs.
### Title:
          Mobility-Budget-Constrained Submodular Maximization for Distributed Semantic Multi-Robot Exploration
 - **Authors:** Changxuan Yao, Sandilya Sai Garimella, Lu Gan, Panagiotis Tsiotras
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Multiagent Systems (cs.MA)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Multi-robot exploration under communication and mobility constraints requires balancing information gain with reachability. We present a distributed semantic exploration framework that combines Dirichlet mapping, budget-constrained trajectory selection, and Resource-Aware distributed Greedy (RAG) coordination. Each robot clusters frontiers, computes terrain- and heading-aware least-cost paths with a single budget-limited Dijkstra search, and selects feasible paths by marginal information gain, represented by the expected reduction in Dirichlet entropy. Each robot's per-round selection is exact, the team's joint per-round information gain is at least half the centralized optimum, and the bound degrades gracefully under limited communication. Ten-run gridworld ablations show improved coverage, semantic mIoU, and map certainty with lower entropy and traversal cost per unit distance than shortest-distance frontier selection. Budget-sensitivity experiments show that an intermediate budget best balances path reach and receding-horizon execution. In photorealistic full-system simulation against five coordination rules run in their native published forms, the proposed method attains the highest mean coverage and semantic accuracy while being the only one that respects the per-trajectory mobility budget.
### Title:
          Understanding Gene Expression-to-Image Learning: Spatial Structure and Information Fidelity
 - **Authors:** Xiaoqing Lian, Pei Liu, Jiahui Tan, Quan Zou, Chen Lin, Xiangxiang Zeng
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Machine Learning (cs.LG); Genomics (q-bio.GN)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) enables cell-level analysis of biological heterogeneity, but learning effective representations from high-dimensional, unordered gene expression profiles remains challenging. Gene Expression-to-Image (GE2I) offers an effective learning strategy by arranging gene values on pixel grids for convolutional neural networks, but its underlying mechanisms remain unclear. We study GE2I on full-transcriptome data while keeping the network and training procedure fixed. Our results reveal a trade-off between spatial smoothness and gene-level fidelity. Structured mappings produce smooth, low-frequency patterns that support faster early optimization, whereas assigning multiple genes to the same pixel obscures expression differences and can reduce predictive performance. This trade-off explains why random mappings can compete with structured layouts when many genes are represented. One-to-one gene-to-pixel assignment improves prediction across the tested visual backbones even when image smoothness decreases. We further find that the benefits of pretraining and augmentation depend on the backbone and mapping. These findings provide practical guidance for pixel allocation and the transfer of vision priors to gene expression data.
### Title:
          Autonomous 3D Visual-Thermal Inspection for Utility-Scale Solar Farms
 - **Authors:** Lachlan Tychsen-Smith, Mark Cox, Brett Wood, David Haddon, Kenrick Anderson, Benjamin Duck, Jim O'Malley, Noel W Duffy, Peyman Moghadam
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Utility-scale photovoltaic (PV) inspection is dominated by aerial surveys, which are effective for identifying module-level hot cells but provide limited visibility of critical components located under the array, including connectors, cabling, fasteners and structural elements. These assets are difficult to inspect consistently at scale, and their defects are often spatially small, partially occluded and difficult to localise from isolated images. We present an automated 3D visual-thermal inspection system (Tricorder) for under-panel PV asset inspection. The approach combines LiDAR-inertial Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (SLAM) with geometrically calibrated RGB and thermal imagery and machine-learning-based object detection and segmentation. Detected assets are associated with the reconstructed 3D environment, allowing visual and thermal observations from multiple viewpoints to be spatially fused and localised. We demonstrate the approach on utility-scale PV data for MC4 connector detection and connection-state recognition, thermal anomaly identification, and detection of structural components including bolts, nuts and torque tubes. The results demonstrate a pathway from image-based inspection toward spatially grounded autonomous inspection, where defects are detected and linked to specific assets and their 3D locations within the solar farm.
### Title:
          Mitigating Popularity Bias in Sequential Recommendations with Sparse Prior-Regularized Objective
 - **Authors:** Anton Pembek, Anton Klenitskiy, Alexander Savchenko, Alexey Vasilev
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Information Retrieval (cs.IR)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Popularity bias is a well-known challenge in recommender systems, often leading to the over-exposure of popular items and reduced catalog coverage. In this work, we propose PR-entmax, a sparse prior-regularized objective that makes popularity correction context-dependent. We derive our method from a generalized variational principle in which an item popularity prior is incorporated directly into a sparse probability mapping, yielding a popularity-aware active support: the set of items receiving non-zero probabilities and hence non-zero gradients is determined jointly by contextual relevance and item popularity. As a result, the prior affects not only item probabilities, but also which items participate in gradient updates during training. The method requires no architectural changes and can be integrated into existing training pipelines by modifying only the loss function. At inference time, the prior term is removed, and items are ranked by the learned contextual relevance alone. Experiments across multiple benchmark datasets and sequential recommendation architectures show that PR-entmax consistently reduces popularity bias while preserving or improving recommendation relevance, advancing the relevance-debiasing trade-off.
## Keyword: localization
### Title:
          SharedSAE: a unified sparse dictionary across u-net blocks and denoising time for diffusion models
 - **Authors:** Longhao Lei, Lan Yao
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Concept representations in diffusion models drift across network depth and denoising time, yet existing sparse autoencoders offer only per-block or per-timestep dictionaries, leaving no single feature dictionary for localization and intervention in the network. We present Shared Sparse Autoencoder (SharedSAE), which learns one sparse dictionary shared across four U-Net attention blocks and all 50 denoising steps of SDXL 1.0. A block-specific low-rank input adapter aligns the residual distribution of each block before the shared encoder, and a temporal conditioning module with a shared timestep branch and a block-timestep correction branch absorbs temporal non-stationarity. Despite spanning both axes, Shared SAE preserves a coherent dictionary structure. Built on the shared dictionary, localized feature suppression reduces the number of NudeNet-positive generations in the I2P detector-positive subset from 139 to 1. Tracing sparse features across the trajectory further reveals that concept representations are redistributed across U-Net blocks during generation, shifting from the earliest downsampling block toward intermediate and later blocks as denoising proceeds. SharedSAE thus provides a unified, interpretable, and intervention-ready representation space for diffusion models, enabling concept analysis and erasure at any covered block and denoising step.
### Title:
          Optimal strong convergence of a splitting mixed finite element method for a stochastic Keller--Segel system
 - **Authors:** Liet Vo
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Numerical Analysis (math.NA); Probability (math.PR)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 In this work, we develop and analyze a splitting mixed finite element method, combined with a fully implicit Euler time discretization, for a stochastic Keller--Segel system with general polynomial logistic growth and multiplicative noise. The proposed splitting formulation allows the use of standard finite element spaces without requiring the LBB inf--sup condition associated with traditional mixed finite element methods and, at each time step, avoids the need for solving a coupled nonlinear system. We first establish the well-posedness of the variational solution and derive new high-moment, higher-order regularity, and exponential stability estimates. These estimates provide the key analytical foundation for the strong convergence analysis of the numerical method. A principal contribution of this work is the derivation of error estimates directly in full expectation, without the use of localization arguments. By combining the new stability and regularity estimates with a stochastic Gronwall framework, we overcome the difficulties arising from the interaction between the nonlinear chemotactic coupling and the multiplicative noise. As a result, we establish strong convergence in high-moment norms and obtain optimal convergence rates with respect to both the temporal and spatial discretizations. In particular, the proposed method achieves the optimal rates of order $k^{\frac{1}{2}}$ in time and $h$ in space in the relevant $L^2$- and $H^1$-based error norms. These results provide, to our knowledge, the first optimal strong convergence analysis in full expectation for a splitting mixed finite element approximation of this class of stochastic Keller--Segel systems.
### Title:
          Spatiotemporal Recognition of Abdominal Wall Layers During Optical Trocar Entry
 - **Authors:** Shreeram Athreya, Andrew Melehy, Carter Prentice, Michael Robinson, Corey Arnold, James Wu, William Speier
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Objective. To evaluate whether spatial context and temporal modeling improve tissue recognition during optical trocar entry, where multiple abdominal-wall tissues may be visible simultaneously. Methods. We retrospectively analyzed 71 recordings with 1,516 annotated keyframes and 29,151 clinician-annotated sectors. Each frame was divided into 28 polar-grid sectors assigned to nine tissue classes. Color, neighboring-sector, and positional features were used by a gradient-boosted emission classifier. The full pipeline added learned temporal smoothing, spatial regularization, an anatomically ordered hidden Markov model, and posterior blending. Five-fold cross-validation was grouped by recording, with cross-fitting to prevent within-recording leakage. Primary evaluation used clinician-derived targets; ablations assessed classification, probability quality, spatial consistency, and peritoneum-or-deeper onset timing. Results. The complete pipeline achieved accuracy 0.646, macro-F1 0.507, and macro-AUROC 0.882, versus 0.600, 0.440, and 0.861 for emissions alone. Macro-F1 improved by 0.066 across all five folds. Label changes decreased from 66.2 to 19.5 per sector-minute, and median onset error decreased from 15.58 to 2.45 seconds. Frames with a tissue-depth-code span of at least three decreased from 53% to 26%, versus 22% for clinician annotations. Performance remained limited for preperitoneal fat and bowel, and the full model retained a 44% premature-onset proportion. Conclusions. Combining appearance, spatial context, and temporal constraints improved retrospective tissue recognition and onset localization. Tissue-specific limitations, residual timing errors, and reliance on future observations warrant external validation and prospective evaluation of causal models before clinical use.
### Title:
          GG-Nav: Goal-oriented Grounding Chain-of-Thought Elicits Reasoning in Navigation Foundation Models
 - **Authors:** Shaohao Zhu, Wensi Huang, Siqi Zhang, Yuqiang Yang, Meng Wei, Chenyang Wan, Tianle Liu, Tai Wang, Jinming Xu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Emerging strong Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have advanced navigation foundation models. However, these models still have unsatisfactory performance on goal-oriented navigation settings. In this paper, we first conduct a motivating study revealing that the key issue lies in the insufficient utilization of streaming observations and imprecise target localization. To tackle these challenges, we propose GG-Nav, a simple yet effective navigation framework that learns a structured chain-of-thought (CoT) mechanism via supervised fine-tuning to explicitly localize goal-relevant instances, accumulate grounding evidence, and support more reliable decision-making. Specifically, the structured CoT is designed with two key stages: (i) Observation-to-Evidence, which reasons over all intermediate frames between adjacent decision steps to retain useful goal-related evidence; and (ii) Evidence-to-Decision, which integrates the current observation with accumulated evidence to ground the target and support the generation of navigation actions or clarification questions. Extensive experiments show that GG-Nav outperforms the state-of-the-art methods in terms of success rates on the HM3D ObjectNav, VL-LN, and CoIN benchmarks by 3.1%, 16.6%, and 5.3%, respectively. Ablation studies further show that accumulating evidence from intermediate observations reduces evidence-missing failures by 11.6%, while explicit grounding reduces stopping errors by 49.4%. We also deploy GG-Nav on a Unitree Go2 robot, demonstrating its zero-shot generalization capability in unseen real-world environments.
### Title:
          DeepTelecom: A Differentiable Multimodal Framework for Channel Twinning in Dynamic Heterogeneous Wireless Environments
 - **Authors:** Bohao Wang, Tianen Yuan, William Yen, Zehua Jiang, Chongwen Huang, Li Wei, Chen Zhu, Jiguang He, Hang Zou, Lina Bariah, Brahim Mefgouda, Anis Bara, Yongliang Shen, Zhaoyang Zhang, Merouane Debbah
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Information Theory (cs.IT)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Large-scale multimodal data are a key enabler for integrating large generative artificial intelligence models into wireless networks. However, existing wireless datasets offer limited support for heterogeneous environments, particularly in terms of reusable high-fidelity scenes, scalable differentiable ray tracing, and synchronized multimodal data. These limitations are more pronounced in mobile and aerial scenarios with time-varying environments and channels. To address these limitations, we propose DeepTelecom, a material-aware, high-fidelity multimodal channel dataset and reproducible generation framework for networks involving unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) and reconfigurable intelligent surfaces. DeepTelecom refines coarse indoor/outdoor reconstructions and uses a large language model to map segmented surfaces to frequency-dependent electromagnetic parameters, yielding material-aware Level of Detail 3 digital twins. Then, a differentiable GPU-accelerated Sionna ray tracer simulates propagation across the sub-6 GHz/millimeter-wave/sub-terahertz bands, and specifically captures rotor-induced micro-Doppler signatures of stationary or moving UAVs at different pitch angles and translational velocities. The framework synchronously produces channel tensors, coverage maps, transmission videos, multi-view images, and weather-conditioned renderings, and supports differentiable material calibration from sparse channel anchors. The released dataset contains 10 material-aware indoor/outdoor scenes and 50,000 synchronized UAV samples; the framework supports scalable generation of additional data for user-defined scenes and system configurations. In 3D UAV localization, DeepTelecom reduces the 90th-percentile error from 31.05 m to 2.43 m compared with schemes considering only channel state information, demonstrating its significance for artificial-intelligence-enabled wireless systems.
### Title:
          Global Optimality of Pose Graph Optimization
 - **Authors:** Shuan Ding, Ruishuang Chen, Mengfan Cao, Zaiyue Yang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Signal Processing (eess.SP)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Pose Graph Optimization (PGO) is a classical and widely applied problem in state estimation for robotics, playing a fundamental role in localization, mapping, and navigation. Yet, it is nonconvex in nature and may have many local minimizers, thus difficult to analyze. In this paper, we investigate the optimization landscape and establish a sufficient condition of global optimality for PGO. The ultimate goal of this paper is to theoretically confirm that the optimization landscape is benign and attractive around the global minimizer, if noise is smaller than an explicit and a priori bound. To reveal such an insight, this paper develops three main technical results. First, we identify a proper basin of attraction (BOA) containing global minimizer, and characterize its geometric property by showing that its radius is controlled by noise in an explicit way. Then, by exploring the first order optimality conditions of PGO, we prove that a local minimizer is globally optimal if a noise-controlled criterion is satisfied. Finally, by combining above two results, we prove that one and only one local minimizer exists in the BOA and this minimizer is globally optimal, when the noise is smaller than an explicit bound that can be verified a priori. Numerical simulations validate the proposed theory under various random cases, showing that the obtained conditions provide an effective criterion for certifying the global optimality of PGO.
### Title:
          Does Failure Feedback Direct Agent Self-Modification?
 - **Authors:** Rong He
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Software Engineering (cs.SE); Multiagent Systems (cs.MA)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Self-improving agents use failure feedback to revise their code or instructions, but gains after selection do not establish target-specific repair or generalization. We measure directedness by comparing repair of a randomly selected target failure with repair of the parent's other failures. Across 145 eligible modifications in public Darwin Gödel Machine logs, we find no detectable target-specific advantage. Controlled instruction-revision experiments with small open models show that relevant feedback can improve target repair. In a second study with a pre-specified analysis plan, target feedback raised repair by 20 percentage points for Qwen3-4B relative to matched same-category feedback; the 7-point increase for Phi-4-mini was not significant. Target repair did not reliably imply held-out improvement. Held-out gains were concentrated in settings with identifiable, repairable failure mechanisms, including planted errors and natural tool-interface failures. Benefits of edit localization and regression screening varied across settings, while eight-step revision loops and GEPA ended at similar mean held-out accuracy. Evaluations should report target repair, transfer to related failures, and held-out net change separately.
### Title:
          BGC-Nav: Cross-Altitude Multi-UAV Collaborative Navigation via Belief-Guided Coupling
 - **Authors:** Jiuwu Hao, Yueyang Wu, Liguo Sun, Haolin Song, Zao Zhang, Pin Lv
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Despite recent advances in Aerial Vision-and-Language Navigation (VLN), single Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) search-and-rescue in outdoor scenarios remains challenging due to partial observability, making cross-altitude collaboration a promising direction. However, existing collaborative navigation methods rely on precise target localization and rigid goal-dispatching paradigms, ignoring high-altitude observation uncertainty and suppressing low-altitude exploration autonomy. To address these issues, we introduce BGC-Nav, a training-free cross-altitude collaboration framework that enables robust navigation via Belief-Guided Coupling (BGC). Specifically, the high-altitude UAV maintains a Global Belief Memory encoding task-conditioned priors, which the low-altitude agent incorporates into decision-making via Local Exhaustion Memory (LEM), Local Attraction Augmentation (LAA), and Directional Belief Prior (DBP) as an advisory bias rather than a rigid command. Extensive experiments on ESARBench show that BGC-Nav consistently outperforms state-of-the-art single-agent and collaborative methods. Code is available at this https URL.
### Title:
          DEFT-VLA: Stage-Localized Force Specialization for Contact-Rich Robotic Manipulation
 - **Authors:** Zan Huang, Yibo Qiu, Kan Ding, Xuanping Zhang, Ao Tian, Ronald X Xu, Mingzhai Sun
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Contact-rich manipulation poses a stage-dependent sensory conflict: vision and proprioception are essential for localization and approach, when force is largely uninformative and potentially distracting, whereas force becomes critical after contact, when irrelevant visual details and correlated robot states may become shortcuts. We propose DEFT-VLA (Dual-Expert Force-specialized Training for Vision-Language-Action Manipulation), which retains the task-adapted base expert for vision-dominated non-contact behavior and introduces a force-aware contact expert for critical contact stages, localizing force-conditioned adaptation to where it is needed. We further introduce a contact-localized force-attending curriculum that applies force-attending post-training only to the contact expert, progressively restoring vision and masking state tokens to suppress shortcuts. Real-robot experiments on pipette tip attachment, bimanual pipette aspiration, and whiteboard wiping show that DEFT-VLA effectively exploits force feedback to improve contact-stage performance while maintaining strong non-contact behavior and robustness to held-out grasp heights. It improves overall task success by 20-40 percentage points over the strongest baseline on each task.
### Title:
          ScalLoc: Continuous Oblique Thermal UAV Geo-Localization with a 2D Satellite Map
 - **Authors:** Xiaoran Zhang, Yu Liu, Jinyu Liang, Huaxin Xiao
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Cross-modal Thermal Geo-localization (TG) provides an illumination-robust positioning solution for Unmanned Aerial Vehicles (UAVs) in Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS)-denied environments. However, existing thermal geo-localization methods mainly focus on near-nadir observations or depend on map-side elevation priors, limiting their applicability to continuous oblique thermal localization with only geo-referenced 2D satellite maps. To address this problem, we propose ScalLoc, a scale-aligned local-to-map localization framework for continuous oblique thermal UAV geo-localization. ScalLoc follows a reconstruction--refinement--anchoring--propagation design: it reconstructs local window geometry from thermal sequences, refines camera poses using track-based pose optimization, adaptively anchors each window to the satellite map, and propagates validated states to recover frame-level global trajectories. To support systematic evaluation, we construct Thermal-Oblique, a real thermal UAV sequence dataset containing 216 urban and rural day--night trajectories and 28,112 thermal frames with frame-level annotations. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ScalLoc reduces the mean localization error from 112.69 m to 26.97 m compared with the VGGT-only configuration, corresponding to a 76.07% reduction, and achieves the lowest frame-weighted mean position error among the compared methods. These results demonstrate the feasibility of continuous oblique thermal geo-localization using only lightweight 2D satellite maps, without requiring digital surface models or preconstructed 3D maps. Code and dataset are available at this https URL.
### Title:
          DSM-Free UAV Visual Localization via Latent-Depth Variable Projection and Structure-Adaptive Coupling
 - **Authors:** Yibin Ye, Xichao Teng, Shuo Chen, Xiaokai Song, Yuli Sun, Qifeng Yu, Zhang Li
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Visual localization enables unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) to maintain absolute positioning in GNSS-denied environments. A common approach matches UAV images with geo-tagged orthographic maps and estimates camera pose using Perspective-n-Point (PnP) with elevations from Digital Surface Models (DSMs). However, accurate and up-to-date DSMs are often unavailable in practice. Orthographic-perspective geometry (OPG) enables DSM-free localization in scenes with varying elevations, yet existing methods typically estimate pose indirectly through intermediate matrix estimation and decomposition. To address this limitation, we propose Latent-Depth Variable Projection (LDVP), which treats the unknown scales along perspective viewing rays as latent depths. By analytically eliminating these depths and solving for the horizontal camera center in closed form given the rotation, LDVP reduces nonlinear estimation to a rotation-only problem, enabling direct recovery of metric horizontal position without assuming a planar scene. To improve localization robustness across different scene structures, we further introduce Structure-Adaptive Coupling (SAC), which uses relative scene structure inferred from monocular depth estimation to select LDVP when the matched points are not dominated by a single plane, and Planar-PnP otherwise. Our local observability analysis reveals how scene elevation variation affects the geometric constraints of OPG, providing a basis for this adaptive strategy. Extensive experiments show that LDVP consistently outperforms existing OPG-based DSM-free methods, while SAC further improves robustness across diverse scenes and substantially narrows the gap to DSM-based localization. Code will be released at this https URL.
### Title:
