          InCLAD: A Continual Learning Benchmark for Industrial Visual Anomaly Detection
 - **Authors:** Łukasz Marcjan, Dorota Wilk-Kołodziejczyk, Roberto Corizzo
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Industrial anomaly detection benchmarks have advanced rapidly, yet they remain largely static and therefore underrepresent deployment settings in which inspection systems must be extended from one industrial component to another over time. In our setting, continual industrial anomaly detection is framed as a task-incremental problem: each component defines a task, while the learning objective remains semi-supervised. The goal extends beyond conventional anomaly detection, since models should preserve their performance on all components as they are sequentially exposed to new components. This framing is especially relevant because many existing industrial pipelines either assume fully supervised defect labels or study only isolated datasets, leaving open the question of how semi-supervised anomaly detectors behave across a sequence of heterogeneous components. We address this gap by organizing the benchmark as a sequence of related component-level tasks and by defining multiple scenarios, including random, easy-to-hard, and hard-to-easy. In addition to single-dataset continual scenarios, we propose challenging scenarios spanning across multiple datasets. We further evaluate continual performance from two complementary perspectives: image-level anomaly detection and pixel-level anomaly localization. By recasting industrial anomaly detection as a continual benchmark rather than a static train/test problem, our work establishes a concrete path toward studying knowledge retention, adaptation, and generalization in evolving industrial environments.
### Title:
          Where Did We Go Wrong? Localizing Errors to Repair Multi-Turn Conversations
 - **Authors:** Alessandro Morosini, Wei Sun, Georgia Perakis
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Large language model (LLM) assistants are increasingly used in multi-turn conversations, where the user's intent is often unclear at first and revealed only gradually over time. The assistant must therefore interpret each exchange and form inferences about the user's intent, using them to update its working state. These updates usually refine the assistant's understanding, but a misinterpretation can instead corrupt its working state by ruling out the true intent, influencing subsequent turns and ultimately causing the conversation to fail. Efficient recovery then requires locating and revising the misinterpretation without discarding information that remains valid. We introduce Loci (Localization Of Corrupted Inferences), a framework for probabilistically localizing misinterpretations and selectively repairing the assistant's working state. Given an observed failure, Loci reasons over possible user intents and assigns each update a probability of having excluded the true intent. These probabilities rank suspected updates and quantify the model's confidence in the localization of the underlying misinterpretations. Loci then uses this information in a repair policy that decides whether to retain, verify, or remove suspected updates, enabling recovery from corrupted inferences while preserving useful information. To evaluate Loci's ability to identify misinterpretations, we first test it in a controlled setup with injected errors. We find that Loci improves localization over the baselines, with its advantage becoming larger when multiple errors occur. We then evaluate it in free-form conversations, where misinterpretations arise naturally, and measure the interactions needed to complete the task after an initial failure. Using localization confidence to guide repair, Loci reduces mean recovery cost by 46-79% relative to the strongest baseline across multiple domains.
### Title:
          RootSLAM: Square Root Bundle Adjustment for Visual-Inertial SLAM
 - **Authors:** Tomás Santucci, Mateo de Mayo, Daniel Cremers, Taihú Pire
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Efficient perception is a prerequisite for the small, low-power devices targeted by robotics, mixed reality, autonomous driving, and drone applications, all of which rely on Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (SLAM) to estimate their motion within their surroundings. Learning-based SLAM systems achieve state-of-the-art accuracy but are demanding in compute, whereas classical geometry-based methods retain a strong efficiency advantage with little to no loss in accuracy. In this work we present RootSLAM, a fully-featured visual-inertial SLAM system for multi-camera setups built on Basalt. RootSLAM achieves accuracy competitive with the state of the art while running up to 20 times faster, facilitating the deployment of perception systems in resource constrained environments. We adapt the square-root bundle adjustment formulation from structure from motion into the multi-camera visual-inertial SLAM setting through a novel reformulation that leverages the system's pose-graph, letting the global optimizer run in single-precision floating point, thus doubling its performance. We provide a thorough benchmark against the state of the art together with an ablation study of our contributions. Our new implementation, including its map management and loop detection module, pose-graph optimizer, and global mapping components, will be open-sourced.
### Title:
          StimFlow: Synthesizing Brain Activity from Images via Stimulus-conditioned Flow Matching
 - **Authors:** Bui Van Hung, Dang Thanh Vu, Hoang-Son Vo, Mai Duc Minh Huy, Mai Tien Dung, Soo-Hyung Kim
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 The inherent variability of fMRI responses across trials encourages a generative view of the stimulus-to-response mapping. Prior generative encoders often operate in compressed latent spaces and condition predominantly on semantic features, which provide limited low-level cues for early visual cortex. We propose StimFlow, which synthesizes fMRI directly in native voxel space using conditional flow matching with a linear path. This design aligns training and inference distributions, enabling competitive performance with a single sample and reducing reliance on averaging. To match the visual hierarchy, StimFlow combines structural DINOv2 features with semantic CLIP features, which prove complementary along the cortical processing stream. With a single sample, StimFlow achieves a per-voxel Pearson correlation of 0.371, matching baselines that average five draws. Generation in voxel space allows direct ROI analysis and concept localization without a decoder. Furthermore, it recovers over half of full-data performance from just one hour of scans, supporting efficient cross-subject adaptation.
### Title:
          SIMPLE: Semantic Structure-Aligned Prompt Learning for Open-Vocabulary Audio-Visual Event Localization
 - **Authors:** Hanyu Xuan, Haoran Xu, Min Yin, Shuo Wang, Cheng Zhao, Kun Li, Fei Wang, Zhiliang Wu, Guanghui Yue, Hehe Fan
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Multimedia (cs.MM); Sound (cs.SD)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Open-vocabulary audio-visual event localization aims to infer explicit segment-level categories for both seen and unseen audio-visual events. Existing solutions rely heavily on hand-crafted prompt templates, whose predictions are highly sensitive to subtle wording variations. Learnable prompts remove this manual tuning burden, but when trained solely on seen events, they tend to overfit to seen categories and distort the inter-event semantic topology required for generalization to unseen categories, a failure we term semantic topology drift. In this paper, we propose SIMPLE, a semantic structure-aligned prompt learning framework that aligns cross-category semantic topology between learnable and hand-crafted prompts across asynchronous audio and visual streams. Specifically, our SIMPLE combines Learnable Continuous Prompt Tuning (LCPT) with Semantic Topology Consistency Regularization (STCR). The LCPT automates prompt engineering through data-driven optimization, while the STCR module aligns the category-wise similarity distributions of learnable and hand-crafted prompts under random category subset sampling to alleviate semantic topology drift. Comprehensive experiments and analyses on the OV-AVEBench demonstrate that our SIMPLE framework achieves state-of-the-art performance on the seen and unseen splits, while using roughly half the trainable parameters and roughly one-third the training time.
### Title:
          A Decade of PhotoMappers: A Longitudinal Study of Crowdsourced Disaster Photos Geolocalization with Vision-Language Models and Geospatial Reasoning
 - **Authors:** Hao Li, Wenping Yin, Fabian Deuser, Jia Jia, Ziqi Liu, Levente Juhász, Fan Zhang, Filip Biljecki
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Crowdsourced disaster imagery provides timely, ground-level observations when conventional disaster mapping is delayed or incomplete, yet its geographic value depends on where observations are produced, how they are interpreted, and whether they can be reliably located as actionable Geospatial Intelligence (GEOINT). Using more than a decade of PhotoMappers, a crowdsourced disaster-mapping initiative, this study examines the changing geography of disaster information and asks how emerging GeoAI models compare with human geographic interpretation. We combine longitudinal spatial analysis with cross-view geolocalization using street view imagery (SVI) and remote sensing imagery (RSI), and evaluate vision-language models (VLMs) reasoning through 30 indicators spanning geographic cues and reasoning evidence. The results reveal a geographically and temporally uneven archive shaped by changing disaster types, Community Lifelines, and operational priorities. Integrating VGI, SVI, and RSI with Sinkhorn re-ranking achieves 40.90% R@1, 61.10% R@5, and 69.45% R@10, while yearly R@1 varies markedly from 25.00% to 65.07%. Geographic reasoning is similarly uneven: Qwen3.6-27B achieves an overall agreement of 67% compared with 51% for InternVL3-8B, with both models showing greater difficulty with localized and place-specific evidence than broad spatial configurations. We argue that the value of GeoAI lies less in replacing volunteered geographic interpretation than in complementing it, scaling candidate retrieval while retaining human judgment for ambiguous and consequential cases. This provides a pathway toward more human-centered and responsible GeoAI for disaster mapping. All data and code are openly available at this https URL.
### Title:
          Lesion-Aware Attention for Mammography Segmentation with Heterogeneous Supervision
 - **Authors:** Di Dai, Bo Liu, Liwei Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Accurate lesion segmentation in mammography is important for computer-aided breast cancer diagnosis, but dense pixel-level annotations remain costly and limited. Existing public segmentation datasets usually cover only a small set of lesion types with manually delineated masks, whereas larger mammography datasets often provide only image-level diagnostic labels. This annotation mismatch makes it difficult to train segmentation models that can generalize to rare or weakly annotated lesion categories. To address this problem, we study open-vocabulary mammographic lesion segmentation under a mixed-supervision setting, where pixel-level masks are available for base lesion categories and image-level labels are available for a broader set of categories. We propose a two-branch framework that jointly learns from segmentation data and classification data. The segmentation branch learns lesion boundaries from annotated masks, while the classification branch introduces additional image-level semantic supervision. To better transfer image-level supervision to lesion-relevant regions, we further introduce a Lesion-wise Attentive Classifier that maintains category-specific feature prototypes and generates lesion-aware attention maps for foreground feature pooling. This design reduces the influence of background tissue and encourages more compact lesion representations. Experiments on multiple mammography datasets show that the proposed method consistently improves open-vocabulary segmentation baselines on both base and novel lesion categories, while reducing the gap to fully supervised models that require dense target-domain masks. Qualitative results further indicate that our method produces more complete lesion localization for small, low-contrast, and visually subtle mammographic findings.
### Title:
          Rate-Resolved Text Evaluation with Masked Diffusion Language Models
 - **Authors:** Yiqi Liu, Kun Zhao, Chenghua Lin, Nafise Sadat Moosavi
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 A single evaluation score can rank generation systems but rarely reveals what failed. We propose rate-resolved text evaluation, which turns the masking rate of masked diffusion language models into a diagnostic axis. By reconstructing text under different fractions of masked tokens, one diffusion score becomes a vector of band-conditioned scores that reflect reconstruction difficulty at varying levels of visible context. On SummEval, preserving this rate structure yields complementary signal: the best masking-rate band improves fusion with autoregressive likelihood over a collapsed diffusion scalar, survives a sample-shuffle control that breaks rate ordering, and adds residual signal to a fixed prompted judge. A second diffusion model reproduces the effect more weakly. On WMT MQM zh-en, roundtrip mask-and-reconstruct probing produces a tunable localization curve in which ranking-oriented AUROC and span-detection F1 peak at different masking rates. Factuality benchmarks bound the interpretation: band semantics are model- and task-dependent, so each application requires empirical validation.
### Title:
          ADAPT: Audio-Depth Action Planning with Transition-Aware Rewards for Lights-Out Industrial Navigation
 - **Authors:** Wenjun Huang, Shenghao Fu, Yian Jin, Yang Ni, Yifan Li, Lichi Li, Xingjian Diao, Pengfei Zhang, Yutong Song, Hanning Chen, Jiarui Cui, Tianyu Yang, Shilong Li, Yu Kong, Mohsen Imani
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Vision-language-action models for embodied navigation typically rely on RGB observations, limiting their applicability in lights-out industrial environments where visible illumination may be unavailable. We study illumination-independent navigation using spatial audio for target localization and depth for obstacle-aware geometry. To support this setting, we introduce DarkNav-Bench, a benchmark with 24 industrial scenes and four progressively challenging acoustic conditions. We further propose ADAPT, an audio-depth action-planning framework equipped with spatial-audio perception and optimized for closed-loop navigation through reinforcement learning (RL). Our key RL contribution, transition-aware reward shaping (TARS), defines a pose-aware shortest-action potential over a position-heading graph. Unlike conventional geodesic-progress rewards, TARS accounts for both feasible movements and in-place rotations, providing action-consistent feedback for policy optimization. Experiments show that ADAPT consistently outperforms training-based navigation baselines and zero-shot multimodal models on both seen and unseen scenes. On unseen environments, it achieves 90.6% success under Clean conditions and retains 60.0% success under the most challenging Localized Noise setting. These results demonstrate the potential of spatial audio and depth as complementary modalities for reliable RGB-free navigation in lights-out industrial environments.
### Title:
          All-St3R SLAM: Real-time RGB Dynamic SLAM with Pixel-wise Motion Masking
 - **Authors:** Yewon Kim, Paul Hongsuck Seo, Sang Chul Ahn
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 SLAM (Simultaneous Localization and Mapping) is a core technology in autonomous systems that enables them to estimate their own position and build a map of unknown environments simultaneously. SLAM systems in dynamic environments are of great practical importance. However, existing dynamic SLAM approaches still have several limitations like over-removal and high computational costs. To address these limitations, we propose a real-time RGB Dynamic SLAM framework that precisely removes moving objects at the pixel level without relying on predefined labels or segmentation models. Our system combines pixel motion predicted by ego-motion using DUSt3R with point tracking from All-Tracker, removing only actually moving pixels by analyzing discrepancies between the two motion sources. Unlike object-level masking methods, our approach eliminates only currently moving regions, allowing temporarily static parts to be utilized for tracking. Operating at approximately 10 FPS, the system achieves real-time performance on standard 30 FPS video streams by subsampling every 3 frames. Relying solely on RGB inputs, this significantly improves upon state-of-the-art dense dynamic SLAM approaches (0.5-3 FPS), presenting a highly practical solution.
### Title:
          Seg2Click: Learning Pixel-Level Action Masks for GUI Visual Grounding
 - **Authors:** Jingchao Wang, Dingjiang Huang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Visual grounding is a central challenge for GUI agents, requiring an instruction to be mapped to a target within a crowded screenshot. Coordinate generation with single-point supervision reduces a spatially extended target to one position, despite the presence of many valid click locations. Region-based patch scoring alleviates this ambiguity, but its spatial decisions remain tied to visual-token footprints that may span the target, neighboring controls, and background. To address these limitations, we propose Seg2Click, which reformulates GUI grounding as executable-region prediction: identifying where a click can validly execute the given instruction. Using bounding-box supervision, Seg2Click predicts an executable-region mask and computes the final click point through a deterministic rule. This formulation captures the spatial extent of valid click locations and enables finer localization beyond the visual-token grid, making it well suited to small, densely arranged controls without requiring a verifier or zoom-in pass. Experiments across five backbone configurations from two model families demonstrate the effectiveness of Seg2Click. Seg2Click-9B achieves 92.06\%, 94.10\%, and 55.22\% on ScreenSpot, ScreenSpot-v2, and ScreenSpot-Pro, respectively, while matched-backbone comparisons show improvements over both coordinate generation and patch-based grounding. Code will be available at this https URL.
### Title:
          Graph3R: Unifying View Graph Initialization with Feed-Forward Reconstruction
 - **Authors:** Jingwen Yu, Hengli Wang, Jianhao Jiao, Zhonghang Liu, Ping Tan, Hong Zhang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Feed-forward reconstruction recovers camera poses and dense geometry from image sets whose views are assumed to come from a single scene. In practice, uncurated image collections often contain views from disjoint scenes, and reconstructing these views jointly can corrupt camera poses and geometry. Existing pipelines typically resolve view associations in separate stages before final reconstruction, leaving view graph initialization and reconstruction inference only loosely coupled. We introduce Graph3R, which unifies view graph initialization with feed-forward reconstruction through a shared geometric representation. A lightweight verification head predicts pairwise covisibility from the multi-view features of $\pi^3$ to construct a view graph. This graph classifies the input views into scene groups. For each group, we mask the shared features before decoding camera poses and dense geometry. Reusing the multi-view features allows Graph3R to reconstruct disjoint scenes separately with a single backbone pass. Across three benchmarks, Graph3R improves average maximum recall at 100% precision by upto 8.99 percentage points over the baseline, while improving reconstruction quality on mixed-scene inputs. Regarding the multi-session mapping task, Graph3R improves cross-session localization and achieves competitive pose estimation accuracy, while delivering a runtime speedup of approximately $35.9\times$ over the baseline when reconstructing the query group. By integrating learned view association with geometric inference, our approach takes a step toward end-to-end reconstruction from uncurated image collections.
### Title:
          ClothAtlas: Joint Garment Identification and State Estimation under Self-Occlusion
 - **Authors:** Jens Lundell, Alberta Longhini, Lei Shu, Hemal Arora, Evan Robert, Carlota Parés-Morlans, Marcel Büsching, Mårten Björkman, Jeannette Bohg
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Garment manipulation requires inferring both garment identity and complete configuration from partial, self-occluded observations. This creates a chicken-and-egg problem: completing a partial observation requires a garment-specific deformation prior, while identifying the garment requires a configuration that explains the observation. We introduce ClothAtlas, which jointly identifies garments and estimates their complete configurations from partial point clouds and a library of canonical meshes. A shared graph-conditioned flow-matching model samples configurations for each candidate mesh and ranks candidates by observation fit. The model accommodates different mesh shapes, vertex counts, and connectivities, allowing new garments to be added from canonical meshes alone without retraining. On held-out DexGarmentLab garments, ClothAtlas ranks the correct mesh first among 221 candidates for all 22 test garments from a single flat observation. From a single self-occluded observation, it identifies 15/22 garments, compared with 8/22 for a deterministic graph estimator. Persistent vertex identities support grasp-target localization on real garments, reducing mean error from 15.4 to 6.4 cm overall and from 17.0 to 8.3 cm on occluded targets compared with dense descriptor matching. Project page: this https URL
### Title:
          VideoSlate: Stateful Commit Control for Temporally Grounded Agentic Video Reasoning
 - **Authors:** Shuo Zheng, Yuanjie Dang, Peng Chen, Jianjun Xiang, Ronghua Liang, Mohammed Bennamoun, Qiuhong Ke
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Agentic video reasoning systems gather and assess video evidence across stages, selecting relevant spans as visual evidence for answering questions. New candidates and observations prompt reassessment of the current span, but revisions can correct localization errors or discard useful evidence already present in the adopted span. Systems therefore need to connect evidence organization across stages with temporal state control, allowing accumulated information to jointly inform revision assessment while explicit adoption decisions govern how assessment outcomes affect downstream visual evidence. We introduce VideoSlate, a stateful commit-control framework built around shared evidence. A typed evidence blackboard links candidate spans, provenance, construction, and verifier observations, enabling an incumbent-relative certifier to assess revision support from the candidate, current span, and related proposals. Based on certification, the controller authorizes a revision or retains the current span, separating evidence accumulation from state updates and recording each decision and its evidence on the blackboard. Extensive evaluations across video grounding and grounded question-answering benchmarks show that VideoSlate can improve temporal localization through selective revision.
### Title:
          Recovering from Pose-Selection Failures in Repeated Visual Localization
 - **Authors:** Jaehyun Kim, Wonseok Kang, Tae-Wan Kim
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Repetitive structures can give an incorrect pose candidate more inlier support than an accurate one, causing visual localization to select it. History-Guided Pose Reselection (HPR) uses coarse position forecasts from up to five past localization outputs to distinguish well-separated current-query candidates. The best-fitting forecast proposes an alternative; HPR accepts it only if every well-fitting forecast is much closer to it than to the localizer's pose. HPR needs no odometry and returns the selected candidate unchanged, using prediction for selection rather than pose refinement. With one development-set threshold, HPR improves recall within 5 meters and 10 degrees in all 12 main configurations of Hierarchical Localization (HLoc), MASt3R-based pose generation, and JIST-based retrieval, losing successes on fewer than 0.4 percent of queries. On NAVER Gangnam B1, HPR corrects 97 HLoc failures and loses six successes; gains persist within 0.25 meters and 2 degrees on all three HLoc test sets. Median HPR runtime on HLoc candidates is below one millisecond, excluding pose generation. Recovery weakens under less frequent localization.
### Title:
          NoduleFlow: Automatic 3D Lung Nodule Detection and Segmentation
 - **Authors:** Giulia Romoli, Filippo Ruffini, Francesco Di Feola, Valerio Guarrasi, Matteo Tortora, Paolo Soda
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Low dose Computed Tomography (CT) is the standard imaging modality for lung cancer screening. On CT scans, lung cancer typically manifests as pulmonary nodules, whose manual identification is time-consuming and prone to observer variability, image quality limitations, and suboptimal reading conditions. Moreover, subtle temporal changes are often difficult to quantify reliably in CT scans from the same patient across follow-up examinations. Deep learning-based approaches for the automatic detection and segmentation of lung nodules have demonstrated improved accuracy and efficiency. However, most existing approaches provide only coarse region-of-interest localisation, rely on prior lesion annotations or human supervision, or treat detection and segmentation as separate tasks. In this work, we present NoduleFlow, a framework for lung nodule detection, classification, and segmentation directly from series of CT volumes. Our proposal consists of four interconnected modules and operates in an automated manner at inference time, without requiring prior lesion annotations, manual initialisation, or region-of-interest pre-selection. We further investigate whether nodule localization benefits from the propagation of candidate regions across follow-up scans through inter-scan registration fields, as opposed to conventional independent per-scan analysis. The generalisability of the framework is systematically evaluated across four independent datasets. The results underscore the potential of automated pipelines for lung nodule analysis in screening and diagnostic settings.
### Title:
          G$^2$WAM: World Action Models for Efficient and Geometry Grounded Image-Goal Navigation
 - **Authors:** Ke Gui, Chenghao Shi, Ziyue Wang, Longwen Chen, Xieyuanli Chen, Huimin Lu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 World models capture environment dynamics and support counterfactual reasoning for planning. In image-goal navigation, where the target often lies beyond the current field of view, the agent must not only model environmental dynamics but also maintain a persistent spatial memory of the scene. Existing approaches either introduce spatial context by fine-tuning pretrained models on the target task, which underexploits their spatial priors, or rely on external 3D reconstruction, which is computationally expensive for real-world deployment. In this paper, we propose the Geometry Grounded World Action Model (G$^2$WAM) for image-goal navigation. G$^2$WAM encodes spatial cues through a learned scene-coordinate localization module that anchors the current, goal, and history frames in a common reference frame, yielding a stable and consistent spatial context that enables efficient navigation without explicit 3D reconstruction. Following the world-action-model paradigm, G$^2$WAM jointly predicts future states and actions, eliminating the need for costly cross-entropy method (CEM) style planning. In addition, G$^2$WAM operates entirely in latent space, avoiding the computational burden of pixel-level reconstruction and substantially reducing inference cost. Across four trajectory prediction benchmarks, G$^2$WAM reduces average ATE and RPE by 40.1% and 35.2%, respectively. In closed-loop navigation experiments, it improves the success rate from 54.0% to 63.0% and the success weighted by path length (SPL) from 36.4% to 59.2%, while maintaining high efficiency.
### Title:
          PathPilot: An Adaptive Question-Conditioned Multi-Magnification Navigation Agent for Whole-Slide Image Analysis
 - **Authors:** Zhengyang Xu, Han Li, Jingsong Liu, Ayako Ura, Shihui Zu, Shaohua Kevin Zhou, Nassir Navab, Peter Schüffler
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 The diagnostic capability of WSI agents relies on effective slide navigation to identify diagnostically relevant regions and efficiently integrate evidence across the slide. While in clinical practice, pathologists dynamically examine slides across multiple scales, integrating global and cellular evidence in a sequential manner, existing AI navigation approaches mostly operate at a single fixed magnification or rely on predefined magnification traversal. This mismatch prevents existing methods from modeling cross-magnification interactions and adaptive magnification selection inherent to real diagnostic workflows. We propose PathPilot, a clinically consistent multi-magnification WSI navigation agent that explicitly models cross-magnification localization and evidence-adaptive exploration. Specifically, we introduce a CrossMagLoc that learns magnification-specific diagnostic localization from pathologist viewing behavior and integrates information from adjacent magnifications to identify the diagnostically most relevant region within the current ROI, thereby providing a diagnostically informed starting point for subsequent question-conditioned exploration. We further introduce an EviRegionSearch that starts question-conditioned exploration from this region, progressively accumulating question-relevant evidence and adaptively determining the next navigation action according to the remaining evidence requirement. Their iterative interaction enables PathPilot to sequentially explore across ROIs and magnifications until sufficient evidence is acquired. Extensive QA evaluations across five cohorts demonstrate consistent superiority over competing SOTA methods on inter/external cohorts, respectively, validating question-conditioned evidence acquisition across regions and magnifications that mirrors the sequential, knowledge-driven navigation of pathologists.
### Title:
          A Multi-Task Deep Learning Framework for Pose-Invariant Face Recognition and Facial Landmark Localization
 - **Authors:** Ali Ashkeyev, Zukhra Abdiakhmetova
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Face recognition systems often experience performance degradation under large pose variations due to changes in facial appearance and visibility of discriminative features. This study presents a multi-task deep learning framework that jointly addresses pose-invariant face recognition and facial landmark localization within a unified architecture. To enable simultaneous optimization of identity recognition and facial structure learning, the original Cross-Pose Labeled Faces in the Wild (CPLFW) dataset was adapted into a multi-task learning dataset by associating facial images with identity labels and five-point landmark annotations. The proposed framework consists of two task-specific branches operating on a common facial input: a ResNet50-based branch for identity recognition and an EfficientNetV2B0-based branch with spatial attention for facial landmark localization. The two objectives are jointly optimized using a weighted multi-task loss, allowing the model to learn complementary semantic and spatial facial representations. The proposed framework was evaluated on the prepared CPLFW-based multi-task dataset. Unlike the original CPLFW benchmark, which focuses primarily on identity recognition, the proposed dataset adaptation incorporates five-point facial landmark annotations to enable joint recognition and localization learning. Experimental results demonstrate that the model achieves a validation recognition accuracy of 81.58% and a facial landmark localization mean squared error of 96.38. The results indicate that combining identity classification and landmark regression within a single optimization framework provides an effective approach for learning robust facial representations under cross-pose conditions The proposed method demonstrates the potential of multi-task learning and attention-based feature refinement for unconstrained facial analysis.
### Title:
          DeepGroundRepair: From API-Grounded Generation to Shape-Aware Repair for Deep Learning Programs
 - **Authors:** Xiangyue Ma, Zhen Wang, Xuhui Lu, Xiaoting Du, Chenglong Li, Jun Li, Haotian Li, Zheng Zheng
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Software Engineering (cs.SE)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Deep learning programs are fundamental to modern AI applications, and automatically generating them from natural language requirements can substantially reduce DL development effort. Large language models have demonstrated promising capabilities for this direction. However, generated DL programs often remain unreliable because they must satisfy domain specific API usage constraints and tensor shape dependencies, violations of which can cause API level syntactic issues and runtime failures. Existing retrieval augmented generation and feedback guided code repair methods do not adequately address these DL specific constraints. In this paper, we propose DeepGroundRepair, a three stage framework that establishes a generation localization repair workflow to improve the reliability of LLM-generated DL programs. Its key idea is to progressively narrow the generation and repair spaces using DL specific knowledge and runtime tensor information. Specifically, it grounds generation in task-relevant API knowledge, localizes the upstream causes of tensor shape mismatch failures, and uses runtime tensor shapes to guide parameter repair. We evaluate DeepGroundRepair on the 100 model-level DL programming tasks in DeepEval using six open-weight LLMs across TensorFlow, PyTorch, and PaddlePaddle. We compare it with four baselines: two generation-only methods and their counterparts incorporating traceback guided self debugging. The results show that, relative to these baselines, DeepGroundRepair reduces the combined number of API-level syntactic issues by an average of 5.4 per 10 program files and achieves an average relative improvement of 85.3% in code executability. Compared with the two self debugging baselines, it improves semantic consistency by an average of 18.1% and reduces token cost by an average of 28.4%.
### Title:
          Learning to Design Convex Relaxations for Nonconvex Optimization
 - **Authors:** Jinrun Liu, Bai Yan, Tengfei Liu, Qunfeng Liu, Jian Yang, Shi Cheng, Yuhui Shi, Qi Zhao
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Mathematical Software (cs.MS)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Convex relaxations make nonconvex optimization tractable by replacing hard nonconvex problems with convex surrogates that give valid bounds. Yet their design is still largely manual: a human expert must decide where to relax, which relaxation to use, and how much formulation growth is worth paying. We show that this design process can be learned. The method proposed in this paper, AutoConvexRelax, represents the symbolic formulation of a nonconvex problem as a tripartite graph linking variables, symbolic terms, and their objective or constraint positions. It then learns a policy that composes symbolic rewrites into problem-specific relaxation trajectories as the formulation evolves. We prove that finite action sequences generated by the learned policy preserve relaxation validity under explicit domain, occurrence-context, and representation-consistency conditions. We further establish computable error bounds for mixed relaxations and sufficient conditions for feasible recovery and global-optimality certification. As a first instantiation, we apply the method to nonconvex quadratic problems and fractional problems. On 241 benchmark quadratic instances, the learned policy strengthens McCormick lower bounds by 13.52%. Its mean lower bound is 3.70% below strengthened semidefinite relaxations on the normalized comparison metric, while it uses substantially smaller formulations. The learned policy retains this trade-off on fractional instances and reaches near-semidefinite bounds on beamforming and sensor-network localization instances. These results suggest that convex relaxation design can move from a fixed handcrafted choice to an automated learning process that adapts to problem structures. Code is available at this https URL.
### Title:
          DIME: Scaling Facial Representation Learning via Differential Masked Autoencoding
 - **Authors:** Hao Yu, Haoyu Chen, Hui Wei, Yan Jiang, Nicu Sebe, Guoying Zhao
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Scaling up data and model capacity has driven substantial progress in visual representation learning. However, conventional pretext tasks exhibit diminishing returns on fine-grained facial analysis. We attribute this to a scaling bottleneck: these tasks model each image's appearance and are dominated by identity, pose, and illumination, so subtle within-identity facial differences contribute little to the training signal. We introduce DIME, a differential masked autoencoder for large-scale facial representation learning. DIME builds on a simple observation: two images of the same person share identity-specific appearance, so their difference exposes facial changes. DIME reconstructs both masked images in an identity-matched pair and, through an Explicit Differential Direction Supervision (EDDS) objective, aligns the direction of their reconstructed difference with the true difference, while source isolation makes each reconstruction depend only on its own image. Shared appearance cancels in this difference, and because EDDS ignores magnitude, subtle facial changes count as much as large pose or illumination changes. DIME uses identity labels only to form pretraining pairs, requires no dense annotations, and learns dense facial representations with emergent part-level semantics. We pretrain DIME on 20 million high-resolution facial images consolidated from existing face datasets. DIME's AU detection performance continues to improve as pretraining data scales from 6M to 20M images, with its BP4D F1 increasing by 5.9 points, while MAE gains only 0.5 points on the same subsets. DIME also outperforms specialized facial methods on AU detection, AU intensity estimation, cross-domain anti-spoofing, landmark localization, head pose estimation, face parsing, and face generation.
### Title:
          SonicWorld: Source-Centric Latent Dynamics for Spatial Audio Reasoning
 - **Authors:** Chenlong Wei, Hanling Wang, Mingjin Lou, Yufan Shi, Yandong Sun, Sheldon Yu, Xiaohui Zhu, Xiaobo Jin, Yiwei Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Sound (cs.SD); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Audio and Speech Processing (eess.AS)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Dynamic spatial audio reasoning requires a model to associate changing acoustic observations with the correct source and recover its spatial state at a queried time or interval, a challenge intensified by overlapping speech and source motion. To study this problem, we introduce Sonic-Bench, comprising 2.8K first-order Ambisonics clips and 28.8K target-specific question-answer pairs across 480 simulated indoor scenes. The benchmark covers localization, distance estimation, and motion reasoning in single- and dual-source settings. We further propose SonicWorld, which integrates physical, learned acoustic, and semantic features into persistent source states through learned transitions and recurrent updates, and projects them into an Omni LLM for spatial reasoning. Compared with Spatial-Omni on the Sonic-Bench test split, SonicWorld improves direction accuracy from 31.98% to 34.69% and reduces azimuth error from 57.22° to 48.01°. In zero-shot transfer to Spatial LibriSpeech, SonicWorld retains strong localization performance, achieving 73.98% direction accuracy and 19.29° azimuth error, compared with 27.14% and 70.74° for Spatial-Omni.
### Title:
          Physical SLAM: Physics-aware Simultaneous Localisation and Mapping
 - **Authors:** Guangming Wang, Zhishuo Liu, Haibing Wu, Wanli Ma, Yixiong Jing, Olaf Wysocki, Brian Sheil
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 To reconstruct a predictive 4D representation of the physical world from monocular video captured by a moving camera, we jointly reconstruct dynamic Gaussian radiance fields and infer their underlying physical dynamics. This paper proposes \emph{Physical SLAM}, a physics-aware method for constructing 4D Gaussian fields of deformable objects and predicting their future state when subjected to external actions, given a sequence of RGB-D images. Our method separates the scene into a static background Gaussian field for camera localization and a dynamic object Gaussian field driven by a spring--mass physical model of elastic deformation, inertia, and damping. To integrate visual observations with physical dynamics, we introduce virtual observation forces that gradually pull simulated surface nodes toward observed depth points, avoiding abrupt replacement of simulated node positions. Meanwhile, the physical parameters of the spring--mass model are updated from simulation errors aggregated over the full reconstruction observation window, enabling the reconstructed dynamics to align with observations. To couple appearance, geometry, and physics, we further design a physics-aware Gaussian transformation and surface alignment strategy, where dynamic Gaussians are transformed along the deforming physical surface of the spring--mass model and constrained to remain aligned with the surface during rendering-based optimization. The resulting representation supports observed-frame rendering and action-conditioned simulation, extending SLAM maps to describe how deformable objects respond to manipulation. On the PhysTwin benchmark, our method improves reconstruction and prediction PSNR over PhysTwin by 10.4\% and 11.0\%, respectively. On the TUM RGB-D benchmark, it reduces average camera tracking error by 20.6\%, improves SSIM by 3.6\%, and reduces LPIPS by 8.3\% compared with 4DGS-SLAM.
### Title:
          Why Agentic Workflows Fail: A Taxonomy Derived from Literature and Empirical Data
 - **Authors:** Xin Li, Rami Bahsoon, Tao Chen
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Software Engineering (cs.SE); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Agentic workflows combine large language models with planning, memory, tools, execution environments, and iterative feedback. Their failures are difficult to diagnose because the same user-visible symptom may originate from model behavior, workflow orchestration, external tools, infrastructure, or their interaction. This paper presents a source-aware taxonomy of agentic workflow failures and applies it to evidence from academic literature and GitHub. The taxonomy combines source attribution with three empirical dimensions: a concrete failure-type dictionary, workflow-stage localization, and agent-capability localization. Its central structure is a two-axis localization matrix: workflow stages form the horizontal process axis and agent capabilities form the vertical functional axis. This design moves beyond taxonomies that mainly classify final failure manifestations by localizing intermediate process states inside agent operation. Our empirical comparison shows that literature-derived failures emphasize planning, reasoning, learning, perception, and knowledge-related mechanisms, whereas GitHub-derived failures concentrate on execution-stage, actuation-related, tool, API, and infrastructure failures. The dominant hotspot is iterative execution and actuation. These findings indicate that agentic workflow reliability should be treated as a systems problem rather than only a model-output problem. The taxonomy provides a basis for structured evaluation, root cause analysis, failure injection, and engineering triage.
### Title:
          Origin Attribution for Unsafe Behavior in Self-Evolving Multi-Agent Coding Systems
 - **Authors:** Hanyi Yu, Jiaming Cui
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Multiagent Systems (cs.MA)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 As language-model-based coding agents evolve their programs and reuse code from one another, safety failures can acquire a history that extends beyond the agent in which they are detected. An unsafe change may originate in one agent, propagate through intermediate recipients, and survive subsequent revisions before an audit reveals it. Understanding this history is important for identifying the introducing change and distinguishing inherited behavior from independently occurring failures. We study origin attribution in self-evolving multi-agent coding systems: recovering the agent and program version that introduced an observed unsafe behavior into the target's code lineage, or determining that it arose locally. This task is challenging because rewriting obscures textual similarity, independent occurrences confound temporal ordering, and intermediate recipients can resemble the source. We propose a method that combines behavioral fingerprint matching with historical localization and recursive tracing. It compares candidate implementations beyond the unsafe outcome, retains evidence from multiple historical versions, and follows matching candidates upstream. On 158 queries across 85 controlled scenarios, the method achieves 96.8% exact attribution accuracy, compared with 77.2% for exhaustive fingerprint matching without recursion. Component ablations and evaluation on 34 additional queries using the same method and hyperparameters demonstrate the complementary roles of multiple representatives and recursive tracing. Further checks extend the evaluation to approval-boundary and resource-owner errors. Together, these results show how behavioral and historical evidence can recover the origins of unsafe behavior through code revision and cross-agent propagation.
### Title:
          Gaussian Splatting for Real-Time Scene Reconstruction: Methods, Systems, and Trade-offs
 - **Authors:** Ashim Dahal, Rabab Abdelfattah, Nick Rahimi
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) allows for high-quality real-time novel-view synthesis. Since its inception in 2023, its explicit and highly configurable scene representation has been extended toward sparse-view generation, dynamic reconstruction, efficiency and compression, streaming, and simultaneous localization and mapping (SLAM). Although Gaussian Splatting can provide real-time rendering, rendering an existing representation is distinct from constructing, updating, or maintaining the scene itself. In this survey, we synthesize current literature, categorize them and provide a connection of current approaches and tools towards an end-to-end pipeline. Explicitly, we categorize GS scene reconstruction into five overlapping families: single-shot generation, dynamic representations, efficiency and compression, streaming and immersive systems, and Gaussian Splatting SLAM. We distinguish render-only throughput from task-specific scene generation, update, tracking, mapping, and optimization costs, and combine controlled sparse-view benchmarking with native evaluations to identify cross-family quality, latency, memory, and update-cost trade-offs.
### Title:
          Cross-Project Bug Localization: What Works, What Doesn't, and Why
 - **Authors:** A Eashaan Rao, Sridhar Chimalakonda
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Software Engineering (cs.SE)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Bug localization models require substantial within-project training data that newly active or small open-source projects rarely have. Cross-project learning (CPL) promises to close this gap by reusing labeled bugs from mature projects, but prior CPL studies evaluate a single model against ample-data within-project baselines that data-scarce projects cannot reach. We ask whether CPL's effectiveness is a property of the transfer setting or depends on model architecture. We evaluate three architecturally distinct models, embedding-based BLAZE, convolutional TRANP-CNN, and GNN-based adversarial COOBA, on 63 source to target pairs from 13 open-source Python projects under identical bug corpora, data splits, retrieval candidates, and baselines. The primary baseline is within-project limited training (WP-small, 20% of target labels, the same target-side share as CP-transfer). CPL outperforms WP-small most clearly for TRANP-CNN (Cohen's d = 0.95, win rate 81.0%, p < 0.001, robust to clustering pairs by target project), and less so for BLAZE (d = 0.49, win rate 60.3%, p = 0.002; not significant once pairs are clustered by target). COOBA, under our re-implementation, shows a small gain that does not survive multiple-comparison correction (d = 0.27, p = 0.027). Negative transfer affects 9.5% of TRANP-CNN pairs and 30.2% of BLAZE's and COOBA's. Cross-model direction agreement is only 44-54%: the same pair can help one architecture and harm another, although the models' gains are positively correlated (rho = 0.24-0.42). CPL outcomes are therefore architecture-dependent: practitioners should validate CPL for their chosen model, and CPL studies should report results per architecture.
### Title:
          MAS-Gate: Unlocking Multi-Agent System Lifecycle via Node-Centric Quantification
 - **Authors:** Hehai Lin, Zixuan Wang, Ming Yan, Peidong Wang, Shilei Cao, Minzhi Li, Bosheng Ding, Juepeng Zheng, Chengwei Qin
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Multiagent Systems (cs.MA)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Large language model (LLM)-based multi-agent systems (MAS) can solve complex problems beyond the reach of a single agent by decomposing tasks and coordinating complementary capabilities. Despite extensive research on MAS, existing work largely focuses on specific aspects or individual phases of their development and operation, resulting in fragmented perspectives with objectives and signals that are difficult to reconcile across these different settings. To bridge these disconnected perspectives, we conceptualize MAS development and operation as a three-phase lifecycle spanning design-time orchestration before execution, runtime intervention during execution, and post-hoc attribution after execution, and introduce MAS-Gate, a unified node-centric quantification applicable across these phases. Modeling each execution as a directed acyclic graph, MAS-Gate combines within-node utility, which estimates a node's contribution through changes in reference-answer likelihood, with inter-node strength, which approximates semantic uptake and potential downstream reach. We adapt this shared quantification to three objectives: providing a structure-aware dense reward for Orchestrator training, training an Editor to act on execution prefixes using paired rewards from completed edited and unedited trajectories, and supplying risk-ranked nodes as auxiliary evidence for LLM-based post-hoc attribution. Experiments spanning interaction environments, knowledge-intensive multi-hop question answering, general reasoning, and post-hoc attribution demonstrate that MAS-Gate improves workflow construction, targeted runtime editing, and LLM-assisted error localization over corresponding phase-specific baselines. Together, these results establish MAS-Gate as a unified structural interface for improving MAS generation, dynamic editing, and error attribution throughout the lifecycle.
### Title:
          FrameLite: Decision-Oriented Aesthetic Cropping with a Compact Vision-Language Model
 - **Authors:** Jianze Li, Kai Liu, Ziqing Zhang, Weihang Zhang, Yong Guo, Linghe Kong, Yulun Zhang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Aesthetic image cropping improves composition by balancing subjects, context, and image boundaries. Large vision-language models bring semantic knowledge to this task, but their scale limits lightweight photography assistance. We propose FrameLite, a compact cropping model built on Qwen3.5-0.8B. Our approach is motivated by the observation that a small model can generate better crops than its first answer suggests. We develop three-stage training to turn this potential into stronger direct predictions. Spatial and composition training establishes localization and framing preferences. Quality-Guided Online Supervision (QOS) then learns the highest-quality complete answers among the current policy's generated candidates. Greedy-Referenced Optimization (GRO) further refines the policy using positive and negative quality differences relative to its current greedy answer. Both online stages use annotation-derived feedback, while inference returns one coordinate answer without search or an external scorer. Experiments show that FrameLite outperforms the compared large-VLM croppers across public benchmarks with a substantially smaller backbone. Controlled studies confirm that selecting high-quality training answers improves the final model beyond online sampling alone. We will release our code and model weights at this https URL .
### Title:
          InSight: A Mobile Assistant Helping Visually Impaired People Navigate Unfamiliar Environment
 - **Authors:** Junwei Zheng, Jiale Wei, Di Wen, Yufan Chen, Ruiping Liu, Chengzhi Wu, Kunyu Peng, Kailun Yang, Jiaming Zhang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Human-Computer Interaction (cs.HC)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Navigating unfamiliar outdoor environments is one of the most demanding everyday tasks for people with visual impairments (PVI). However, existing navigation systems cannot simultaneously satisfy the needs of informative perception and precise localization during navigation. This paper explores the core navigation demands of PVI through a preliminary study. Based on the results of the pre-study, we present the design of InSight, a mobile assistant that combines scene descriptions with precise localization to support PVI navigating unfamiliar outdoor environments. The offline mode of InSight runs entirely on device for real-time navigation, while the online mode streams camera frames to a self-hosted server for arbitrary object detection and accurate localization. Finally, a user study with 21 visually impaired participants validates that InSight could assist users in navigating unfamiliar outdoor environments with increased success rate and confidence compared to mainstream navigation applications like Apple Maps.
### Title:
          Semi-Lagrangian Approximation of Master Equations with Individual Noise on Finite Graphs
 - **Authors:** Jianbo Cui, Tonghe Dang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Numerical Analysis (math.NA)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 We develop and analyze a fully discrete semi-Lagrangian method for vector master equations associated with mean field games (MFGs) with individual noise on finite graphs. The method discretizes both the characteristic evolution in time and the population variable on the probability simplex. A central challenge is the degeneracy of the transport coefficients near the boundary of the open simplex, where no boundary condition is prescribed. To overcome this difficulty in the analysis, we introduce an interior localization based on exact density characteristics, which keeps numerical arrivals within compact regions of uniform coefficient control. Combined with a closure argument for the coupled numerical recursion, this localization yields local solvability of the updates and quantitative error bounds on compact subsets of the open simplex. Under additional $C^2$-regularity of the exact solution and suitable space--time mesh coupling, these bounds imply first-order accuracy. Furthermore, the density and value trajectories generated by the numerical master feedback converge to the corresponding MFG characteristics at the same rate. In the potential setting, the associated reconstructed density--flux pair is admissible and asymptotically optimal for the planning problem, with an action gap quadratic in the feedback error. Numerical experiments illustrate the convergence rates, feedback responses to population redistribution, and the effects of graph topology.
### Title:
          Autonomous 3D Visual-Thermal Inspection for Utility-Scale Solar Farms
 - **Authors:** Lachlan Tychsen-Smith, Mark Cox, Brett Wood, David Haddon, Kenrick Anderson, Benjamin Duck, Jim O'Malley, Noel W Duffy, Peyman Moghadam
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Utility-scale photovoltaic (PV) inspection is dominated by aerial surveys, which are effective for identifying module-level hot cells but provide limited visibility of critical components located under the array, including connectors, cabling, fasteners and structural elements. These assets are difficult to inspect consistently at scale, and their defects are often spatially small, partially occluded and difficult to localise from isolated images. We present an automated 3D visual-thermal inspection system (Tricorder) for under-panel PV asset inspection. The approach combines LiDAR-inertial Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (SLAM) with geometrically calibrated RGB and thermal imagery and machine-learning-based object detection and segmentation. Detected assets are associated with the reconstructed 3D environment, allowing visual and thermal observations from multiple viewpoints to be spatially fused and localised. We demonstrate the approach on utility-scale PV data for MC4 connector detection and connection-state recognition, thermal anomaly identification, and detection of structural components including bolts, nuts and torque tubes. The results demonstrate a pathway from image-based inspection toward spatially grounded autonomous inspection, where defects are detected and linked to specific assets and their 3D locations within the solar farm.
### Title:
          MHVF: Training-Free GUI Grounding via Verification-Guided Multi-Hypothesis Search
 - **Authors:** Minghua Chen, Xingming Liao, Weiqiang Zhong, Zhuowei Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 GUI grounding enables web agents to translate natural-language instructions into actionable interface locations. However, dense layouts and small, visually similar controls can lead models toward plausible but incorrect elements, while refinement around a single prediction may leave alternative targets unexplored. We propose the Multi-Hypothesis Verification Framework (MHVF) for training-free GUI grounding. It combines multi-hypothesis exploration with local visual verification using a single pretrained vision-language model. To explore alternative targets, MHVF constructs spatial hypotheses from multiple full-screen predictions and examines the corresponding regions through zoomed re-localization. To resolve ambiguity among the resulting candidates, it verifies their agreement with the instruction in marked local views. Verification scores then guide a second round of local search and rank candidates retained from both search levels for final selection. Experiments with five general-purpose and GUI-specific models across eight benchmark settings demonstrate improvements in grounding accuracy in most settings. MHVF improves UI-Venus-Ground-7B by 4.3 and 2.1 percentage points on Mind2Web and VisualWebBench, respectively, and raises Qwen2.5-VL-7B's accuracy on ScreenSpot-Pro from 23.6% to 53.6%.
## Keyword: transformer
### Title:
          How Vision Transformers Represent Position: A Mechanistic Comparison of Absolute and Rotary Positional Encodings
 - **Authors:** Mahmoud Mannes, Nikhil Maturi, Parva Mehta, Jiwon Jeong, Aravind Singaram Kannappan
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Positional embeddings encode the spatial position of each image patch in a Vision Transformer (ViT), allowing the model to distinguish different spatial arrangements of visual content. However, we know little about how the choice of embedding shapes a vision transformer's internal representation. In this work, we show that Absolute Positional Embeddings (APE) and Rotary Positional Embeddings (RoPE) encode spatial position through different internal mechanisms: APE stores position in explicit features, while RoPE builds it inside attention and rebuilds it when it is withheld. Specifically, in APE models, sparse autoencoders recover causal row- and column-specific features, whereas interventions on RoPE reveal distinct rotary components that separately encode row and column information. Additionally, causal interventions selectively disabling RoPE's row or column rotations show that decodable row and column information can be independently removed and, once rotations are restored, partly reconstructed, in part by axis-specialized attention heads. Both mechanisms are causally important for classification: permuting APE's positional features across patches reduces top-1 accuracy by 24--31 points, and ablating the rotations of RoPE's primary rebuilding head reduces it more than ablating those of any other head. Our findings show that the choice of positional encoding fundamentally changes how spatial information is organized and processed inside Vision Transformers. More broadly, our work demonstrates how mechanistic analysis can reveal how models construct, reconstruct, and maintain structured representations, providing a basis for targeted interventions in vision models.
### Title:
          Enhanced Trading Systems with Intelligent Market Analysis
 - **Authors:** Vedanth Padmaraman, Shreen Anand, Vamshi Vishruth J, Vineet V Pai, Arvind Srinath K.T, Subramanyam Natarajan, Shylaja S S
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 This paper proposes a hybrid reinforcement learning trading framework integrating intraday market data with transformer-based financial sentiment signals. The system extracts sentiment dynamics and semantic event clusters from financial news and incorporates them into a PPO-based trading agent. Experiments on intraday NSE data demonstrate improved risk-adjusted returns compared to price-only baselines.
### Title:
          Rotate the Loop: Blockwise Conformality for Looped Transformers
 - **Authors:** Alexey Zemtsov, Albina Klepach, Vadim Lomshakov, Andrei Polubarov, Nikita Lyubaykin, Alexander Derevyagin, Vladislav Kurenkov, Alexander Nikulin
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Machine Learning (stat.ML)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Looped transformers reuse one shared block across many recurrent steps, so depth becomes a quantity that can be spent at runtime rather than a stack of distinct layers. Those extra steps help only if a write made on one recurrent step is still readable on later ones, and that depends on the linear map applied to the residual between recurrent steps. We show that whether the loop learns to track state is a geometric question about the form of this map, and we propose blockwise conformal injections: the residual splits into coordinate-aligned groups and rotates inside each one, so every group has its own timescale while the geometry stays exact within it. Our experiments show a performance increase on a demanding form of state tracking - last-token accuracy when the rest of the prefix is already correct - and on language modelling, where a signal written earlier has to be retrieved at the end of the sequence.
### Title:
          Two-Stage Multi-modal Learning for Dynamic Audio-Visual Navigation: From Physical Alignment to Scene Aggregation
 - **Authors:** Jia Li, Yinfeng Yu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Sound (cs.SD); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Robotics (cs.RO); Audio and Speech Processing (eess.AS); Signal Processing (eess.SP)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Embodied Audio-Visual Navigation (AVN) requires an agent to locate sound targets by jointly leveraging visual and auditory cues. While prior work has achieved notable success with static sound sources, real-world scenarios often involve moving targets, giving rise to the more challenging problem of \textbf{Dynamic Audio-Visual Navigation (D-AVN)}. Existing approaches largely rely on semantic auditory features, which limits generalization to unheard sound categories and hinders fine-grained spatial reasoning. To address these challenges, we propose a novel \textbf{Two-Stage Multi-modal Learning} framework for D-AVN. In the Physical Alignment stage, the proposed \textbf{Intensity-Calibrated Geometry Encoder (ICGE)} performs physics-informed alignment of sound intensity with visual geometry to capture category-agnostic spatial correspondence. In the Scene Aggregation stage, a Transformer-based \textbf{Cross-Modal Scene Aggregator (CMSA)} dynamically integrates multi-modal features to construct a robust state representation for policy learning. Extensive experiments on Replica and Matterport3D demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art success rates, while significantly improving generalization and navigation efficiency in complex environments and under unheard sound scenarios.
### Title:
          SenseWeather: Multimodal Sensing for Robust Robotic Perception under Extreme Conditions
 - **Authors:** Fucai Ke, Sukai Huang, Junyi Hao, Kal Backman, Naim Rastgoo, Gholamreza Haffari, Hamid Rezatofighi
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Robotic perception must remain reliable when extreme environmental conditions severely degrade the primary RGB observation. Although robotic platforms commonly carry complementary sensors such as depth and infrared, much of the modern perception stack remains RGB-centric, creating a mismatch between multimodal onboard sensing and pretrained downstream models. We study whether complementary sensing can be used upstream to recover a task-compatible RGB observation that preserves existing perception capabilities without modifying or retraining the downstream stack. To this end, we introduce SenseWeather, a controlled multimodal benchmark for robotic perception under severe rain, snow, dust, fog, and darkness, with registered RGB, depth, and infrared observations, clean references, and grounded high-level perception tasks. We further develop Fusion-to-RGB, with two parameter-efficient sensor-fusion realizations based on token-space fusion for a diffusion transformer and residual latent conditioning for an inpainting UNet. Across frozen open-vocabulary grounding and vision-language models, RGB+D+IR fusion improves all paired downstream comparisons over the corresponding RGB-only restorers, including AP50 gains across all ten detector comparisons. Importantly, these perception gains do not consistently follow conventional image-fidelity metrics, and increasing weather severity does not induce monotonic downstream degradation. These results show that complementary sensing can preserve reusable robotic perception under extreme conditions, while highlighting task-level perception robustness as an objective distinct from visual restoration quality.
### Title:
          Next-Concept Modeling: Fast, Parallel Coarse-to-Fine Text Generation
 - **Authors:** Hermann Kumbong, Ishan S. Khare, Iddah Mlauzi, Andrew Woen, Charles Ding, E. Kelly Buchanan, Simran Arora, Azalia Mirhoseini, Christopher Ré
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Diffusion language models (DLMs) offer parallel sampling for faster generation than autoregressive models, an increasingly important advantage as inference becomes a dominant computational workload. However, DLMs degrade quickly with fewer sampling steps, process the full sequence at every step, and require many passes over the dataset to reach good quality. We introduce Next-Concept Modeling (NCM), a two-stage, coarse-to-fine framework: a multiscale residual VQ-VAE encodes text into a hierarchy of discrete codes, and a transformer generates one level at a time, predicting all codes within a level in parallel. At matched parameter scales, NCM achieves better generative perplexity than the strongest DLM baseline on LM1B while running $21\times$ faster. On OpenWebText, NCM is competitive with the strongest DLM while running $10\times$ faster; on XSum, it matches the strongest DLM's ROUGE-L while being $31\times$ faster. On the reasoning task of Sudoku, NCM outperforms autoregressive and diffusion baselines across all difficulty levels, using fewer function evaluations. On the reasoning task of Sudoku, NCM outperforms autoregressive and diffusion baselines across all difficulty levels, using fewer function evaluations.
### Title:
          ClothCode: Generative Modeling for Garment State Estimation and Dynamics Prediction
 - **Authors:** Yingdong Ru, Lipeng Zhuang, Shiyu Fan, Zhuo He, Florent P. Audonnet, Gerardo Aragon Camarasa
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 We introduce ClothCode, a unified generative framework for garment state estimation and action-conditioned dynamics prediction in a shared discrete configuration space. A material-aligned tokenizer encodes garment configuration as residual vector-quantized patch tokens, while task-specific Masked Generative Transformers predict these tokens through parallel confidence-guided refinement. Across three garment templates, ClothCode reduces mean displacement error by 26.6% for state estimation and 37.0% for dynamics prediction relative to the strongest baselines. Dynamics prediction requires only 16.7 ms per window, compared with 556.2 ms for diffusion. Integrated into the same model-predictive controller, ClothCode improves real-world folding and flattening success from 56.7% to 78.9%. Further results and videos are available at: this https URL
### Title:
          Learnable Low-Rank Polynomial Sketch for Effective Linear Attention
 - **Authors:** Zongyue Qin, Haoran Deng, Jason Cong, Yizhou Sun
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Softmax attention in Transformers suffers from quadratic complexity in sequence length, making it impractical for long-context applications. Linear attention alleviates this issue by replacing the exponential kernel with alternative functions that enable linear-time computation. Among existing linear attention approaches, recent studies have shown that polynomial kernels are particularly effective, as they exhibit sparse and spiky behavior similar to softmax attention, which emphasizes large dot products while suppressing irrelevant interactions. However, the exact computation of high-degree polynomial kernels is infeasible for high-dimensional representations. As a result, prior work relies on approximate polynomial kernels. This introduces a non-negligible approximation error. In this paper, we show that, from the perspective of polynomial kernel approximation, existing linear attention methods are still suboptimal. We propose \textbf{L}earnable \textbf{Lo}w-Rank \textbf{P}olynomial \textbf{S}ketch (LLoPS), a principled and flexible framework for approximating polynomial kernels with linear attention. Our method learns a low-rank polynomial sketch that provably achieves a strictly smaller approximation error than existing approaches. Experiment results show that LLoPS achieves the highest performance across extensive benchmarks, comparing with various linear attention baselines.
### Title:
          Speech event detector: Timestamped detection of non-verbal vocalizations and background sounds for rich speech transcription
 - **Authors:** Ayush Kumar Bar, Promila Ghosh
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Sound (cs.SD)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Automatic speech recognition produces words but drops the non-verbal content of a recording: the breaths, coughs, sighs and laughter of the speaker, and the horns, music or barking around them. We describe Speech Event Detector, a 5.8-million-parameter convolutional-Transformer model that outputs probabilities every 40 ms for 54 event classes (20 speaker vocalizations and 34 background sounds), together with post-processing that converts these probabilities into timestamped events and a procedure for merging the events into a Whisper transcript. The model is trained on material drawn from seven public datasets whose labels come in incompatible forms: single detected regions, free-text tags with timestamps, temporally strong ontology labels, symbols embedded in transcripts, isolated recordings and environmental recordings. We unify them using rule-based label mapping, recover timestamps for transcript-embedded labels through CTC forced alignment, locating 6,360 of 8,620 sounds, and combine about 168 hours of real annotated training recordings with synthetic mixtures and augmentation using real background noise. Per-class thresholds for two operating profiles are tuned on real held-out recordings, and performance is estimated using two-fold cross-validation. On whole held-out recordings from four datasets, considering the 48 classes with at least ten held-out events, the sensitive profile detects an average of 49 percent of a class's events, covering 67 percent of all 9,367 events, at a macro precision of 0.30. The balanced profile achieves a macro recall of 0.37 at a precision of 0.36, with macro event F1 scores of 0.32 and 0.33, respectively. The detector runs about 39 times faster than real time on eight CPU threads.
### Title:
          Adaptive-Depth Is a Finishing Stage
 - **Authors:** Andrei Piterbarg, Sean McLeish, Dhruv Pai, Benjamin Keigwin
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Looped transformers reuse the same layer multiple times, allowing inference compute to be adjusted without changing the model. Recent adaptive-depth methods train the model across multiple loop depths from the beginning of training. We introduce fixed-first training, which varies model depth only during a short final phase of training.
