At 200M parameters, training a model of fixed-depth 2 for the first 67% of the run reduces modeled training compute by 45% while keeping mean negative log-likelihood (NLL) across inference depths within 0.5% of training across depths throughout. At 1B parameters, training initially at depth 4 reduces modeled compute by 31% to 37% and wall-clock time by 32% to 39%, while keeping final NLL within 0.8% and downstream differences within the observed seed variation. These results show that training over the full range of depths can be reserved for a short finishing phase with only small changes in performance.
### Title:
          Image Diffusion Models Are Good 3D Human Motion Generators
 - **Authors:** Siyuan Yang, Chong Wang, Hao Cheng, Jun Liu, Bihan Wen, Alex Kot, Hedvig Kjellstrom
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Text-to-motion generation typically relies on dataset-specific skeletal representations, with models trained separately for benchmarks with different joint layouts. We investigate whether image diffusion architectures can model 3D human motion through a common image-compatible representation that accommodates heterogeneous skeletal layouts while preserving motion-specific kinematic structure. To this end, we organize root-centered joint trajectories into topology-informed joint--time images and model global root dynamics through a complementary latent stream. A motion autoencoder conditions local-motion decoding on the root latent, while a text-conditioned Scalable Interpolant Transformer (SiT) jointly generates both latent streams, with supervision on decoded 3D joint positions further constraining generation in motion space. In addition to dataset-specific models, we train a single motion autoencoder on HumanML3D and KIT-ML, followed by a single text-conditioned SiT shared across both datasets, preserving their respective skeletal definitions without skeleton retargeting to a common joint layout. Experiments on HumanML3D and KIT-ML show that our dataset-specific models achieve state-of-the-art performance, while shared training yields further gains on both benchmarks, demonstrating the benefits of shared modeling across heterogeneous skeletal layouts.
### Title:
          BigCell: Generating Gigapixel Whole-Slide Images
 - **Authors:** Srikar Yellapragada, Alexandros Graikos, Zilinghan Li, Kostas Triaridis, Tarak Nath Nandi, Ji Dong K Bai, Beatrice Knudsen, Tahsin Kurc, Prateek Prasanna, Rajarsi R. Gupta, Ravi K Madduri, Joel Saltz, Dimitris Samaras
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Whole-slide images (WSIs) are gigapixel-scale digital scans of histopathology tissue and serve as the primary basis for cancer diagnosis. Existing histopathology generative models operate at the patch scale, producing fixed-size tiles of at most 1024$\times$1024 pixels which encompass only a small fraction of the WSI. In contrast, text reports for clinical diagnoses and slide-level diagnostic labels -- the supervision that exists at scale -- operate at the whole-slide level. We present BigCell, the first approach to generate coherent whole-slide histopathology images at native gigapixel resolution. BigCell trains a flow-matching transformer at 2048$\times$2048 pixels on over 120,000 publicly available WSIs spanning multiple organ types. Conditioning on embeddings from pathology foundation models and using an inference-time sliding-window pipeline, BigCell generates coherent images of up to 32,768$\times$32,768 pixels, outperforming prior tile generators on both tile-level and slide-level fidelity metrics. To make gigapixel synthesis practical, we introduce an adaptive scheduling strategy that prioritizes important slide regions, speeding up image generation by over 3\ttimes with minimal quality degradation. Furthermore, we propose the first text-to-image synthesis at 8k resolution by training a separate model that produces dense conditioning grids directly from pathology text reports. We demonstrate that augmenting with synthetic samples improves slide-level few-shot classification performance by up to $18 \%$ over baselines trained on real data.
### Title:
          On the Depth Transfer of Neural Optimizers via L-Smoothness
 - **Authors:** Maximilian Wong, Chenyu Xu, Jiajin Li, Yiping Lu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Optimization and Control (math.OC)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Scaling deep learning models efficiently requires predictable performance transfer across compute regimes. Establishing reliable hyperparameter transfer allows optimal training dynamics and structural choices to be discovered on low-cost proxy models, avoiding catastrophic failures during massive-scale training. Existing depth-transfer analyses are architecture-specific and heuristic: hyperparameter transfer is only justified through toy blocks, and both rely on infinite-depth dynamics, a specific initialization, and feature-learning criteria. Building on $L$-smoothness framework of Xu et al. (2026), we understand hyperparameter transfer as how do the Lipschitz and $L$-smoothness constants of the loss scale with the depth $K$? We show that inverse-depth residual branch scaling $\alpha=\Theta(1/K)$ is sufficient to make both constants depth-independent at fixed width, uniformly over the bounded parameter domain and for arbitrary $p\to q$ geometries. Because the bounds depend on each residual branch only through its Lipschitz and smoothness constants, the argument applies to general residual networks (including branches of block depth $\geq 2$ such as transformer and ResNet blocks) without assumptions on initialization or correlations between layer weights. Since the smoothness constant controls the curvature penalty in the descent inequality, our bounds motivate a learning-rate scale without an additional depth-dependent curvature correction for updates bounded in the prescribed norm. This provides a geometric explanation of inverse-depth residual scaling and its application to the operator-norm geometries associated with AdamW, Muon, and MOGA. Learning-rate sweeps on GPT-2 (depths 2--128) and CIFAR-100 ResNets (8--32 residual blocks) confirm that Muon and MOGA exhibit stable learning-rate transfer under these prescriptions, with a common optimal rate across a 64-fold increase in depth.
### Title:
          Multimodal Co-Generation With Continuous Bitstream Diffusion
 - **Authors:** Georgios Batzolis, Georgios Kontos, Bruno Trentini, Pranam Chatterjee, Micha Livne, Mark Girolami, Luca Ambrogioni
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 We show that continuous bitstream diffusion (CoBit), originally developed for language modeling, provides a unified framework for joint and conditional multimodal generation using the same training and sampling protocol. Across image-text, speech-text and protein sequence-structure modeling, models are trained separately from scratch, but each uses a single input projection, transformer backbone, bitwise prediction head and binary score-matching objective shared across its modalities. Within each setting, a single checkpoint supports joint generation and the conditional directions included in training by changing which parts of the bitstream are observed. We first validate the approach on MNIST-Sum, a controlled image-text task pairing raw binary images with equations encoded as text, achieving near-perfect conditional accuracy and high joint consistency without learned tokenizers. We then apply the same protocol at larger scale to speech-text and protein sequence-structure modeling. Trained without text pretraining, a single speech-text model performs speech synthesis, recognition, continuation, and unconditional co-generation. Jointly generated speech and text achieve 3.5% word error rate between the generated text and automatic transcriptions of the generated speech. In protein modeling, a single CoBit checkpoint supports forward folding, inverse folding, and joint sequence-structure generation. For jointly generated proteins spanning 100-500 residues, computational refolding of CoBit's sequences yields structures that agree with the generated backbones at a mean self-consistency TM-score of 0.911. Together, these results show that CoBit provides a common generative framework for substantially different modalities and forms of cross-modal dependence, without introducing a domain-specific objective or sampling procedure.
### Title:
          Deep learning from a control vantage point
 - **Authors:** Álvaro Rodríguez Abella, João Pedro Silvestre, Bahman Gharesifard, Paulo Tabuada
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Systems and Control (eess.SY); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Machine Learning (cs.LG); Optimization and Control (math.OC)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 This article offers an overview of recent developments in deep learning from a control vantage point. It reviews several classes of models including feedforward and residual neural networks, transformers, large-language models, and selective state space models while explaining how control theory plays an important role in understanding their training, properties, or architecture design. The article explains how residual neural networks can be interpreted as the discretization of a continuous-time control system thereby enabling the use of control theoretic techniques for its analysis. To illustrate the power of this bridge between control and deep learning, two technical results are discussed: 1) the ability of a residual neural network to approximate arbitrary continuous functions is a non-standard controllability problem that can be solved using geometric nonlinear control and 2) transformers, the key architectural component of most large language-models, can be seen the discretization of a consensus algorithm and thus large language-models exhibit consensus. Another point of contact between control theory and deep learning is the connection between backpropagation, the algorithm used to train neural networks, and optimal control. The article addresses how the older connection between these problems, established in the 80's using discrete-time models can be reconciled with the more recent approach using continuous-time models. Several open problems and directions for future research are discussed, including selective state-space models, a recent class of models inspired by the models used in control.
### Title:
          Design Choices for Multimodal Transformer Learning with Missing and Longitudinal Healthcare Data
 - **Authors:** Rita Cordeiro Mendes, Maria Manuel Pereira, Carlos Santiago, Catarina Barata
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Multimodal learning offers the potential to improve predictions by integrating complementary information from heterogeneous data sources. However, multimodal datasets are frequently incomplete, raising important questions about how models should handle missing information. Although recent approaches increasingly rely on Transformer-based architectures for multimodal fusion, incomplete multimodal data introduces challenges such as missing modalities and intra-modal missingness, which motivate key architectural design choices, such as tokenization granularity. In addition, many datasets are longitudinal, with observations collected across multiple time points. The sequential nature of the Transformer architectures provides a natural framework for modeling such data, but introduces additional considerations, such as how temporal information should be represented. In this study, we systematically investigate these design choices using three different cancer datasets. We compare modality-level and feature-level tokenization and evaluate attention masking and imputation as strategies for handling missing information, both naturally occurring in the datasets and synthetically induced. We further investigate approaches for incorporating temporal information in our training process and how to model it. Experiments are conducted with unimodal and multimodal configurations, as well as different levels of missingness for binary survival classification and multiple time encoding strategies for discrete-time survival prediction. Our results show that while multimodal outperforms unimodal results, missing data heavily impacts predictions and thus effective design choices such as tokenization granularity, and intra-missing handling is essential for reliable prediction.
### Title:
          Learning to Extrapolate in Depth with Looped Transformers
 - **Authors:** Huitao Yang, Jianhao Huang, Zixuan Wang, Baharan Mirzasoleiman
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Looped transformers reuse a single block across iterations, raising a natural question: how far can a block trained at one depth be safely extrapolated to greater depths? We study this question for all-pairs graph connectivity, where harder graphs require longer-range information propagation. We show that under exchangeability and symmetric positive initialization, population training at depth K converges to an update rule governed by a single effective parameter that balances graph propagation against fitting structure unresolved at training depth. This trade-off creates a finite extrapolation window: too few test-time loops provide insufficient propagation, while too many eventually create false connections. We derive explicit endpoints for this window and bound the graph hardness solvable beyond training as a function of training depth and hardness. In particular, a test depth chosen solely from the training distribution can guarantee recovery on substantially harder graphs, with a certified depth gain that grows exponentially as training depth outpaces training hardness. Experiments validate the predicted windows and show how compute budget and initialization affect the optimal training depth.
### Title:
          EdgeLLM-Nano: Auditing Polar-Guided Key-Cache Compression and Consistency-Anchored Pruning on a 33.6 MB On-Device Language Model
 - **Authors:** S M Mahbub Murshed, Hayden Bright, Rezaul Karim, Ali Shiri Sichani
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Deploying language models (LMs) on the edge presents unique memory, latency, and power challenges absent in high-performance computing. KV-cache compression emerges as a promising approach to address these challenges. However, the effectiveness of current KV-cache compression methods has not been thoroughly examined on edge devices. Moreover, we show that existing evaluation methods do not always involve the KV-cache under test. We propose EdgeLLM-Nano, a measurement study of KV-cache compression using a nano-scale decoder-only LM (transformer) testbed that has 8.39M parameters, i.e., 33.6 MB, to enable end-to-end examination of each backend, seed, and power sample. Our results give a negative verdict for a polar-guided key-cache quantizer (round-to-nearest (RTN) control causes an order of magnitude less damage), as well as a consistency-anchored progressive weight pruner (one-shot magnitude-based pruning performs as well as, if not better). We also introduce Long-Context Decode Fidelity (LCDF) evaluation, a token-by-token examination of teacher-forced log-losses using the KV-cache under test. Unlike perplexity that gives the same 5.461 score to all methods, LCDF distinguishes different KV-cache compression backends thanks to its paired statistics across seeds and effect sizes. Our companion Raspberry Pi 5 measurement shows all compression backends we tested to be less energy-efficient per token compared to uncompressed FP16 inference. Except for KIVI that is close to lossless in terms of LCDF, our findings indicate KV-cache compression can be improved. LCDF offers a simple and effective way of shedding light on KV-cache compression methods, whose negative impacts have been overlooked by perplexity.
### Title:
          Bidirectional Adaptive Transformers for Multimodal Anomaly Detection
 - **Authors:** Yuxin Jiang, Yunkang Cao, Weiming Shen
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 The effective integration of features derived from RGB images and 3D point clouds presents a substantial challenge in the field of multimodal anomaly detection (MAD). Existing MAD methods typically rely on static fusion paradigms built upon frozen pre-trained encoders. While these frameworks facilitate spatial alignment, they lack the mechanism to establish latent semantic correspondence, thereby inducing cross-modal interference that degrades anomaly discriminability. To mitigate these limitations, this study proposes a Bidirectional Adaptive Transformers (BAT) framework, which constructs comprehensive and semantically aligned multimodal representations by leveraging complementary attributes across modalities. The BAT framework introduces a Bidirectional Feature Alignment (BFA) module, which employs a mutual predictive mechanism to bridge the gap between 2D textural patterns and 3D geometric structures. This bidirectional interaction explicitly enhances the textural granularity of point cloud descriptors while augmenting the spatial contextual awareness of RGB features. To ensure that the latent representations possess sufficient informational density for alignment, BAT incorporates a parameter-efficient adaptation architecture. By utilizing lightweight image and point cloud adapters, the framework facilitates specialized fine-tuning of pre-trained models, mitigating the information loss inherent in frozen encoders. Extensive experiments conducted on MVTec 3D-AD and Eyecandies confirm that BAT achieves state-of-the-art anomaly detection performance, significantly outperforming existing MAD methods.
### Title:
          Competition Domains Matter: Selective Head Globalization for Training-Free Sparse Attention in Video Diffusion Transformers
 - **Authors:** Tiantian Liu, Meng Wan, Zhiqiang Liang, Jue Wang, Ningming Nie, Yangang Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Training-free sparse attention is a practical route to efficient video diffusion transformer inference, yet existing methods typically fix the competition domain at each query row for every attention head. Prior work primarily addresses how to estimate token-level importance within this fixed domain; whether the domain itself should adapt to head heterogeneity remains under-specified. We introduce EG-Sparse, a training-free framework that routes low-entropy heads to a grouped cross-head pool while retaining per-row local selection for high-entropy heads, using only scores already materialized by the sparse pipeline. A controlled diagnostic fixes the ranking score and varies only the competition rule: grouped cross-head selection reduces low-entropy-head reconstruction error by 9.8%, whereas forcing high-entropy heads into an all-head global pool increases it by 7.9%. Across four configurations, EG-Sparse achieves higher video quality while reducing attention density and GPU denoising latency compared with strong training-free sparse-attention baselines. Mechanism ablations further show that selective domain adaptation outperforms uniform, random, and fixed domain-assignment policies. These results support competition-domain adaptation as a distinct design axis within centroid-guided training-free sparse attention.
### Title:
          A Hybrid Kinematic GCN and Elastic Transformer for Efficient Indian Sign Language Recognition
 - **Authors:** Pushp Garg, Himangshu Sarma, Mrinmoy Ghorai
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Sign Language Recognition (SLR) presents complex challenges due to variable signing speeds and spatial intricacies. Specifically, Indian Sign Language (ISL) requires precise modeling of its inherently bimanual nature and is bottlenecked by the scarcity of large-vocabulary benchmarks and the inability of standard models to handle irregular signing velocities. To address this, a structurally-aware hybrid framework for efficient, gloss ISL recognition is proposed. To minimize computational redundancy, an entropy-driven SVD sampler that dynamically extracts informative keyframes is used. Because this subsampling creates irregular physical time gaps, the proposed architecture employs a Continuous Dual Positional Encoding paired with a distance-aware Elastic Transformer to preserve natural signing rhythm. Spatially, a Kinematically-Conditioned Adaptive GCN explicitly models bimanual geometry using unified bone-joint features. To validate our framework, a 120-class, 9,453-video native ISL dataset is created. Augmented by Physical Node Dropout and ArcFace optimization to mitigate subject-specific memorization, our framework achieves a competitive 89.8% accuracy, establishing a strong baseline for complex sign language modeling.
### Title:
          One-Way Coupling via Patchwise Adaptive Normalization in Transformer Surrogates
 - **Authors:** Emmanuel Gottlob, Hugo Thimonier, Ludovico Buizza, August Martens, Alexandre Gavaudan, Thiago Guzella
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Many physical systems exhibit one-way coupling: exogenous fields such as medium density or obstacle geometry influence the dynamics without being affected by them. We investigate how explicitly representing these fields can improve the accuracy and computational efficiency of transformer-based PDE surrogates. Building on spatially adaptive normalization, we introduce Patchwise Adaptive Normalization (PAdaNorm), which partitions exogenous fields into patches aligned with the state tokenization. We evaluate PAdaNorm on datasets from three different domains, using a Walrus-inspired transformer architecture. Across the three PDE families, PAdaNorm is on par with or up to 16% better than parameter-matched baselines in mean long-horizon variance-scaled RMSE on 60-step autoregressive rollouts. We also find that PAdaNorm better maintains hard physical constraints over long rollouts, such as preventing flow into impenetrable solid regions in porous media. When the conditioning is static, PAdaNorm caches the conditioning embeddings once and reuses them across the entire rollout, reducing inference FLOPs per step by up to 36% compared with parameter-matched baselines. An ablation over eight conditioning variants shows that modulating a single attention axis (temporal or spatial) suffices and that a lightweight shared MLP across blocks remains competitive with per-block projections.
### Title:
          AcrossVAM2.0: Conflict-Aware Point Supervision for Parameter-Efficient Video Diffusion Adaptation
 - **Authors:** Nan Wu, Yee Hung Hong
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Explicit point correspondence is attractive for adapting video generators: it describes how visible surfaces move, but can overwrite the appearance prior learned by a large pretrained model. We study this tension in a 5.01B-parameter text-to-video diffusion transformer. ACROSSVAM2.0 combines a confidence-weighted track-and-visibility head, zero-initialized residual feedback, and asymmetric PCGrad that removes only the auxiliary component opposing the primary flow-matching gradient on shared LoRA parameters. The design preserves the generator exactly at initialization and protects its primary update during adaptation. In a four-way ablation trained for 1,000 steps with three independent seeds, the full method lowers KLT forward-backward cycle error in all three seeds relative to a matched flow-only continuation (mean seed-level difference -0.00711, SD 0.00505), while robust sharpness remains positive in all three. An expanded 24-prompt evaluation (216 videos) yields 9.7% lower aggregate CoTracker3 cycle error than the matched control and near-matched performance on six VBench dimensions; however, the seed-level tracking effect is heterogeneous and I3D-FVD regresses from 0.1824 to 0.2022. These results support a bounded claim: conflict-aware adaptation improves correspondence consistency without broad perceptual degradation, but does not uniformly improve video quality. We release equations, pseudocode, matched outputs, source tables, and artifact provenance.
### Title:
          BridgeWAM: Bridging Video and Action Experts via Latent Bridge Queries
 - **Authors:** Jiaxin Fang, Ruitong Liu, Zeyu Lai, Xingyu Wang, Mingyuan Jia, Pingyue Sheng, Weikang Wan, Mengdi Xu, Yuxiang Gao
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 World Action Models (WAMs) commonly couple video and action experts through dense, layer-wise Mixture-of-Transformers (MoT) attention, exposing action prediction to irrelevant visual features while enforcing matched expert depths. This coupling hinders selective visual-feature use and introduces redundant computation. We introduce Bridge-of-Experts (BoE), instantiated as BridgeWAM, which aggregates visual, language, and proprioceptive priors through 32 Latent Bridge Queries (LBQs) into a shared, limited latent space. Joint video-action supervision lets action gradients guide the Video Expert toward representations better suited for action prediction, while the Action Expert learns to selectively retrieve task-relevant information through cross-attention. Decoupling expert depths and reusing LBQs during action denoising yield a 5.06B model with a 30-layer Video Expert and a two-layer Action Expert, achieving 98.25% success on LIBERO and 72.24% pooled success on LIBERO-Plus, demonstrating effective task execution and strong generalization with an ultralight action head. Ablations support joint representation adaptation and unified LBQ conditioning. Relative to Fast-WAM, BridgeWAM reduces single-sample inference FLOPs by 28.72% in simulation profiling and real-robot latency to 77.1 ms (a 2.47-fold speedup). Qualitative experimental results further indicate preferential attention to target objects and task destinations across perturbations.
### Title:
          Amharic-English Cross-Language Question Answering Using Statistical Machine Translation, Named Entity Recognition, and Semantic Indexing
 - **Authors:** Emebet Bekele, Fekade Getahun
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 The predominance of English-language Web content creates an information-access barrier for speakers of low-resource languages such as Amharic. This paper presents the design, imple-mentation, and evaluation of a bidirectional Amharic-English cross-language question answering (CLQA) system that allows a user to formulate a natural-language question in either Amharic or English and retrieve an answer from documents written in the other language. The system integrates a statistical machine translation (SMT) component for query translation, a maximum-entropy named entity recognition (NER) component for Amharic text processing, semantic-based indexing, question analysis, passage retrieval, and answer extraction. A domain-diverse Amharic-English parallel corpus was used to train the SMT component, while monolingual cor-pora supported language modeling. Documents were normalized, tokenized, stemmed, indexed, and semantically enriched before retrieval. The original evaluation used precision and recall as the principal effectiveness measures. For Amharic-to-English retrieval, the system achieved 72% precision and 79% recall; for English-to-Amharic retrieval, it achieved 65% precision and 70% recall. These findings show that a modular translation-and-retrieval architecture can provide useful cross-language access even under low-resource conditions. This revised article preserves the original experimental claims while situating them in the context of subsequent multilingual transformer and Amharic QA research.
### Title:
          Prior-Fitted and Frozen Scorers for Multimodal Contrastive Learning
 - **Authors:** Tillmann Rheude, Roland Eils, Benjamin Wild
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Multimodal contrastive learning typically relies on manually specified scoring functions, such as cosine similarity, to quantify the similarity between representations from different modalities. Several such functions have been recently proposed to extend contrastive learning beyond two modalities. In principle, however, any function that assigns high compatibility to matched samples and low compatibility to mismatched ones could serve as a scorer. Motivated by this broader view, we instantiate the scorer with a Transformer and find that even a randomly initialized model, when kept frozen, can outperform strong handcrafted multimodal contrastive baselines, whereas optimizing the scorer jointly with the encoders is less effective. Building on this observation, we introduce the Prior-Fitted Scorer (PFS): a Transformer scorer pre-trained on synthetic multimodal data generated from structural causal model (SCM) priors before transferring it to real-world data. The SCM exposes the scorer to diverse dependency structures, including shared, pairwise, and private information, providing a structured prior over higher-order cross-modal similarity. Across four real-world datasets, frozen Transformer scorers are competitive to contrastive baselines, and prior-fitting further improves the random initialization. More broadly, our results suggest that multimodal contrastive learning need not rely on manual similarity functions: Transformers can provide strong inductive biases, while causal pretraining can further shape these biases.
### Title:
          Stackelberg Game with Multi-Agent Transformer for ISRS-Aware Resource Re-optimization in Multi-Band Optical Networks
 - **Authors:** Jiaxin Liu, Rentao Gu, Miao Zhu, Zeguang Zou, Hui Li, Yuefeng Ji
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Networking and Internet Architecture (cs.NI); Data Analysis, Statistics and Probability (physics.data-an); Optics (physics.optics)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 The rapid expansion of generative AI is driving computing infrastructures toward interconnected AI data centers. Multi-band optical networks have become the backbone of such connectivity. To accommodate increasingly dynamic traffic demands, these networks require continual resource re-optimization to improve resource utilization and reduce service blocking. However, multi-band transmission amplifies inter-channel stimulated Raman scattering(ISRS), inducing nonlinear coupling among services. This coupling complicates re-optimization, as local adjustments perturb the global power profile, creating interdependent optimization decisions across services. Moreover, the nonlinear interactions vary with the subset of services selected for re-optimization, and inappropriate selection may reduce re-optimization effectiveness or diminish achievable benefits. To address these challenges, we propose a Stackelberg Game with Multi-Agent Transformer (SG-MAT) approach for ISRS-aware resource re-optimization. SG-MAT employs a leader-follower structure, in which the MAT-based follower models nonlinear coupling and generates coordinated channel reallocations, feeding the utilization improvements to the leader. Using this feedback, the leader identifies critical service subsets, approximating a Stackelberg equilibrium to guide global decisions and enabling consistent and efficient re-optimization. Simulation results show that SG-MAT achieves the highest utilization improvement and reduces the blocking ratio by approximately 30% under moderate traffic loads, with computational complexity comparable to benchmarks.
### Title:
          Blending experimental electron density information to protein-ligand interaction
 - **Authors:** Seonghyun Park, Shuan Chen, Jun Jin Choong, Noboru Kuno, Gyubok Lee, Kaushalya Madhawa, Keisuke Ozawa, Hikaru Shindo, Sungsoo Ahn
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Machine learning models for protein-ligand interactions commonly rely on atomic coordinates, which are constructed by fitting molecular models to experimental electron-density maps. However, most models represent each complex as a single fitted structure, which can omit information about conformational heterogeneity, positional uncertainty, and local density support for individual atoms at binding interfaces. To fill this gap, we present CoDE, a Coordinate and Density Encoder, pre-trained on experimental density maps. We represent each binding site on a shared three-dimensional voxel grid combining atomic occupancy, experimental electron density, and its gradient magnitude. Using approximately 100K curated complexes, we pre-train a channel-wise vision transformer to jointly reconstruct masked atomic and density channels, learning a representation that integrates structural and experimental information. We evaluate the frozen encoder on two downstream tasks: (i) binding-affinity regression under leakage-controlled splits and (ii) pocket-conditioned ligand generation. Across these settings, CoDE improves over an identically pre-trained coordinate-only encoder and remains competitive with task-specific baselines, showing that density complements fitted atomic coordinates for protein-ligand interaction modeling and that the density-blended representation is useful for structure-based ligand generation.
### Title:
          Transfer Learning for Rhythm Game Chart Generation from Music Transcription and Across Games
 - **Authors:** Sojeong An, Nathan Pruyne, Chris Donahue, Dasaem Jeong
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Sound (cs.SD)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Rhythm games ask the player to act in time with a recording, following a chart: a sequence of actions, each with a time and, depending on the game, a lane, type, or playfield position. Charts are hand-written per recording and difficulty. Automatic generation from audio has been studied with pipeline and end-to-end approaches, and recent work reuses pre-trained audio encoders, but never compares them with one trained from scratch. None of these targets automatic music transcription (AMT), yet most chart times are note onsets, which AMT models locate. We freeze a pre-trained audio encoder, an AMT model in particular, and train only a linear projection and a Transformer decoder on charts. With MuScriptor, this fits human charts more closely than encoders trained from scratch and places more accurate note types than MERT, a music encoder pre-trained without transcription labels. Joint training on three osu! modes raises event F1 on the mode with the fewest charts from 0.492 to 0.506, while charts from Clone Hero, similar in layout, lower it to 0.482. Transfer between games thus appears to depend on charting style and the music covered.
### Title:
          Cross-Material Magnetic Core Loss Prediction via Operating-Condition Matching and Partial Optimal Transport
 - **Authors:** Cong Yao, Yu Zheng, Zehao Hou, Rui Xu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Cross-material magnetic core loss prediction requires adapting to new material responses with limited measurements. Global feature alignment overlooks differences in source and target excitation coverage. This motivates selecting comparable samples and controlling each source measurement's alignment contribution. This paper proposes T-PCOT, a Transformer transfer method based on condition-supported partial optimal transport. Frequency, flux excursion, temperature, and waveform descriptors define a sparse graph of comparable operating conditions. A capacity-constrained transport plan distributes alignment across source measurements and leaves costly target mass unmatched. The plan is constructed from measured inputs before training and guides local waveform-feature consistency. An importance-weighted sampling scheme preserves both uniform source regression and the transport objective during minibatch optimization. Experiments on three material pairs and three target label budgets show that T-PCOT reduces mean relative error (MRE) and 95th-percentile error (P95) relative to Transformer-based multiple-kernel maximum mean discrepancy (T-MKMMD) in all nine comparisons. Macro MRE decreases from 12.60% to 9.65%, and macro P95 from 30.94% to 25.06%, corresponding to relative reductions of 23.41% and 19.01%. Controlled comparisons and ablations examine the roles of input processing, condition correspondence, and source capacity. The results demonstrate the predictive advantage of organizing cross-material alignment through operating-condition support over the compared global alignment method. The proposed method achieves state-of-the-art predictive performance in magnetic core loss transfer learning.
### Title:
          HodgePhaseNet: Discrete Hodge Decomposition of Semantic Flows in Transformer Attention
 - **Authors:** Kiyotaka Kasubuchi, Kazuo Fukiya
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 WavePhaseNet introduced frequency-resolved semantic representations, QuantumPhaseNet introduced gauge-covariant phase connections, and ModularPhaseNet made a finite cyclic phase channel and its cycle holonomy computable in an ordinary Transformer. These constructions are complementary, yet they do not by themselves separate an observed collection of pairwise relations into a globally consistent direction, circulation around local compositions, and circulation that persists around a larger unfilled cycle. HodgePhaseNet addresses this separation by representing semantic transitions as an antisymmetric real-valued 1-cochain on a finite oriented simplicial complex. With vertex-edge incidence matrix $B_1$ and edge-face incidence matrix $B_2$, every such field has a unique orthogonal decomposition $\Omega = B_1^T\Phi + B_2\Psi + H$, where $B_1H=0$ and $B_2^TH=0$. The three component fields are unique even when their potentials are not. We give explicit projectors, a sparse implementation, integrability and gauge results, a verified numerical example, and a redesigned attention interface. The attention bias is defined on allowed ordered query-key pairs, remains consistent under arbitrary choices of edge orientation, and preserves a decoder's causal information boundary by constructing its complex from the visible prefix. Gauge invariance of the attention output is conditional on using invariant or appropriately compensated features. A simple difference of vertex embeddings has no coexact or harmonic component; a genuine relation encoder is therefore indispensable. Local circulation is not automatically contradiction, and harmonic flow is not automatically missing knowledge. These interpretations and any reduction in factual error require controlled empirical evaluation. This paper presents a mathematical and architectural proposal, not external natural-language performance results.
### Title:
          Characterizing the Separability Gap in Prior-Data Fitted Networks
 - **Authors:** Julius Kobialka, Manuel Hülskamp, Thomas Nagler, David Rügamer
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Machine Learning (stat.ML)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Prior-data fitted networks (PFNs) are transformers pretrained on simulated datasets to approximate Bayesian posterior predictive distributions. Standard PFN theory assumes a separable task prior, under which the feature distribution and the mechanism generating labels from features are independent. We show that common PFN priors are non-separable, even in simple settings. Consequently, standard pretraining targets a predictive distribution based on a task posterior updated using both the labeled context and the query features. We characterize its difference from the predictive based on the context posterior alone and show that this difference vanishes under posterior concentration. Under misspecification (i.e., when the true joint distribution lies outside the prior's support), however, prior non-separability can favor better feature fit at the expense of conditional label fit. We illustrate the consequences for feature attribution, Bayesian optimization, and martingale checks of Bayesian behavior. The same dependence also enables PFNs to learn from additional unlabeled observations. We demonstrate this with SemiTabICL, a PFN explicitly pretrained to use these observations, which improves accuracy over a matched supervised baseline. Our findings highlight the need for explicit rules on unlabeled data use in PFN evaluation and establish the dependence between feature and label distributions as a consequential prior design choice.
### Title:
          Recurrent In-Context Learning for Tabular Foundation Models
 - **Authors:** Tejas Vaidhya, Gaurav Sahu, Irina Rish
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Tabular foundation models adapt to new datasets through in-context learning (ICL), and, like most transformers, they tie computational depth to the number of independently parameterized layers. We ask whether the ICL stage of such a model needs a distinct set of parameters for every layer, or only depth. We introduce Tab-Loop, which replaces the 12 untied ICL blocks of TabICLv2 with 4 tied blocks applied $k$ times and changes nothing else: the encoders, decoder, synthetic prior and training recipe are shared with an untied baseline trained for the same compute. At matched pretraining compute (18.2 EFLOP) and matched inference compute (12 block applications), Tab-Loop matches the baseline with 62\% fewer parameters (10.4M vs.\ 27.6M): held-out log loss is 0.4583 vs.\ 0.4588, mean accuracy on 200 TALENT datasets is 84.10\% vs.\ 83.92\%, and Elo on 38 TabArena datasets is 1409 vs.\ 1396. A 4-block untied control with Tab-Loop's parameter count, which sees 32\% more training tables for the same compute, trails both (83.46\%; Elo 1343). Because weights are shared, depth becomes a choice made at inference. Accuracy rises with the number of loops through the trained range and holds beyond it, and continued training with deeper loops extends the useful range, reaching the baseline's stage-2 scores at 4--5$\times$ its inference compute. At this scale, the ICL stage of a tabular foundation model needs depth more than it needs distinct parameters.
### Title:
          Retraining-Free Depth Pruning of Vision Transformers: A Cross Domain Study of Layer Redundancy
 - **Authors:** Alessandro Viespoli, Loris Nanni
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Transformer architectures have achieved strong results in natural language processing and computer vision, but their deployment is limited by high computational, memory and storage costs, motivating research into model compression techniques, such as pruning, while preserving performance. Prior work has shown substantial redundancy in large language models (LLMs), and redundancy in vision transformers has also been investigated, but it remains less systematically analyzed than in LLMs. In this work, we extend similarity-based redundancy analysis to vision models by measuring cosine similarity between layer inputs and outputs to identify transformations with minimal impact. We study redundancy patterns across several transformer architectures and find that deep vision transformers such as DINOv2 and SwinV2, like language models, exhibit significant redundancy. We then evaluate how this redundancy can be exploited to improve efficiency, analyzing the trade-off between inference speed and performance degradation under different pruning strategies. Our results show that moderate pruning can yield substantial acceleration with limited impact on accuracy, and that 20% of the DINOv2 blocks can be removed without any retraining, for a 1.24x throughput gain and a 20% lower energy per image at a relative accuracy loss below 1% on ImageNet-1K. We further propose a model selection pipeline that automatically identifies the best pruned configuration for a given dataset, reducing FLOPs by 13% to 27% on average across architectures with an average accuracy change between -0.3 and +1.0 points.
### Title:
          CowCare: A Unified Visual Framework for Viewpoint-Robust Cattle Tracking and Spatiotemporal Heat-Stress Analysis
 - **Authors:** Xin Liu, Weiran Li, Minghao Zhu, Mina Han, Zhenbo Li
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Reliable individual monitoring is essential for dairy cattle welfare and early heat stress detection in Precision Livestock Farming (PLF). However, translating raw multi-camera videos into automated diagnostics remains challenging due to disconnected tracking and recognition pipelines and complex physical environments. Dense mutual occlusions and cross-camera viewpoint variations fragment identities, while environmental noise, including camera vibrations, and limited agricultural data obscure subtle heat stress cues such as rapid breathing and flank undulations. To bridge low-level visual features and high-level behavioral diagnostics, we propose CowCare, a unified framework combining viewpoint-robust tracking with spatiotemporal heat stress recognition. Its tracking module, CowTrack, maintains cross-camera identity consistency through a View-Decoupled Aggregation Network (VDA-Net). Instead of relying on static global representations, VDA-Net aggregates localized textures under heavy occlusion and uses a Viewpoint Decoupling Transformer (VDT) to suppress transient viewpoint variations. CowTrack further incorporates a causal online self-supervision mechanism for continuous adaptation to dynamic farm scenes. The resulting tracklets are subsequently processed by TSDF-Net, a fine-grained video understanding network. Its Temporal Stabilization and Aggregation Module (TSAM) suppresses high-frequency jitter, while a Diffusion Semantic Prior (DSP) uses frozen pre-trained diffusion semantics to enhance subtle behavioral signals and mitigate overfitting. Extensive evaluations on two real-world farm benchmarks demonstrate the framework's effectiveness: CowTrack achieves 83.48% HOTA and 87.72% IDF1 for global multi-object tracking on MVCow, and TSDF-Net achieves 87.72% Top-1 accuracy on HSCow. These results establish CowCare as a robust end-to-end framework for automated livestock health monitoring.
### Title:
          BrainAid: Efficient Brain Tumor Detection Using Lightweight CNNs and Explainable AI for Resource-Constrained Environments
 - **Authors:** Sai Sriram Gonthina, Ross Gore, Bikash Chandra Singh, Pronaya Bhattacharya, Sachin Shetty, Abhishek Phadke, Sandip Roy
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 A brain tumor is an unusual growth of cells in the brain that can be cancerous or not, affecting normal brain function and causing serious risk to health. Existing solutions for brain tumor classification, such as expert manual evaluations of magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scans, are generally inconsistent as different professionals have different criteria and can be resource-intensive, leading to delayed diagnoses. Additionally, while deep learning models have demonstrated potential, they typically require significant computational resources, motivating the need for more efficient and accessible diagnostic tools that can be deployed in real-time healthcare settings. To address these limitations, this study presents BrainAid, which makes efficient use of Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) and Vision Transformers (ViTs) for effective classification of four-class classification of glioma, meningioma, pituitary tumor, and no-tumor MRI scans, outperforming all the standard models such as VGG16, Inception-V3, ResNet-50 and so, while integrating Explainable AI (XAI) to improve interpretability. The BrainAid is trained to classify tumors with a high precision of 98%, achieving an F1 score of 98% and an accuracy of 98% using publicly available dataset. The model is deployed on a Raspberry Pi, demonstrating efficiency in low-resource environments. This system shows the feasibility of real-time, edge-based tumor classification, providing quick diagnostic support for medical professionals and enhancing patient care in resource-limited healthcare facilities and underserved areas.
### Title:
          RELAGRAM: Recursive Latent Graph Model for the Capacitated Vehicle Routing Problem
 - **Authors:** Vladimir Kirill Bickov, Neil Yorke-Smith
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Neural and Evolutionary Computing (cs.NE); Optimization and Control (math.OC)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Latent recursion repeatedly processes internal representations through the same neural network. Recent work has shown this approach to be effective on structured reasoning tasks such as Sudoku and maze solving. However, whether latent recursion can support neural routing remains unclear. We introduce RELAGRAM (Recursive Latent Graph Model), a novel approach that brings latent recursion to graph combinatorial optimization. RELAGRAM encodes a capacitated vehicle routing problem (CVRP) instance as edge tokens; a compact transformer recurrently updates the latent representations of these tokens. An output head maps the answer state to an edge heatmap, from which an autoregressive decoder constructs a feasible solution. On CVRP-100 instances, the 0.53M-parameter RELAGRAM achieves gaps to a PyVRP reference of 13.4%, 9.8%, and 8.3% under greedy decoding, eightfold symmetry augmentation, and 256-candidate search, respectively. Trained only on CVRP-100, RELAGRAM generalizes zero-shot to CVRP-500 and CVRP-1000, achieving gaps of 15.6% and 21.7% under 256-candidate search. Together, these results demonstrate that latent recursion is a promising approach to neural routing.
### Title:
          ShellElastic: Scalable Elastic Pretraining with Simultaneous Maximal Updates and Hyperparameter Transfer
 - **Authors:** Can Jin, Yan Kang, Yifan Gong, Chongjian Ge, Mingze Xu, Yuanjun Xiong, Dimitris N. Metaxas, Hongwu Peng
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Foundation models need to support diverse inference budgets, yet training a separate model for each budget duplicates pretraining effort. Elastic models train several nested sub-networks inside one weight-shared supernet, but existing recipes face three obstacles: variable per-step workloads that hinder distributed scaling, limited hyperparameter (HP) transfer across model sizes, and no guarantee that all sub-networks are maximally updated at once. We present \Ours, an elastic transformer that organizes hidden dimensions into a dense core and successive shells, so that every hidden matrix is block lower triangular. One full-width forward pass contains every sub-network's exact hidden states as prefixes, so joint training keeps a fixed computation graph and costs about as much per step as training the widest sub-network alone. We further introduce \muE, an extension of maximal update parameterization (\mup) to \Ours and other elastic architectures. Its scaling rules provably keep all sub-networks of a supernet maximally updated simultaneously (\muI), and keep doing so when the supernet is widened or given more shells; sweeps confirm that HPs tuned on a small non-elastic proxy stay near-optimal for elastic families and larger supernets (\mbox{\muT}). A single \Ours run produces sub-networks that outperform MatFormer and fully elastic baselines at matched parameter counts, and approach separately trained models that together take several times the steps.
### Title:
          Single-step Retrosynthesis via Reinforcement Learning from Forward Feedback
 - **Authors:** Zixun Lan, Zhenfu Liu, Yi Li, Xinheng Wang, Mingmin Chi, Qibiao Wang, Jie Zhu, Xiaohui Tian, Fei Ma
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Single-step retrosynthesis is a one-to-many inverse problem, yet most models are trained and evaluated against a single recorded reactant set. Reference-based training gives no explicit positive signal for unrecorded alternatives, and exact-match evaluation rejects non-matching predictions regardless of chemical plausibility. We propose Retrosynthesis via Reinforcement Learning from Forward Feedback (RetroRLFF), which optimizes reactant generation using both reference agreement and forward consistency with the target product. Starting from a supervised decoder-only Transformer, RetroRLFF generates complete reactant candidates and uses a frozen forward reaction model to predict products. A sequence-level reward combines reference agreement and forward consistency while penalizing invalid outputs. Direct Advantage-Based Policy Optimization updates the policy without human preference annotations or a preference-trained reward model. On USPTO-50K, RetroRLFF outperforms all 14 baselines in candidate-level round-trip accuracy at k = 1, 3, 5, and 10. With approximately 50 million parameters, it achieves 90.1% top-1 round-trip accuracy, exceeding R-SMILES, the strongest competing baseline on this metric, by 8.5 percentage points. Compared with its supervised policy, round-trip accuracy increases from 83.1% to 90.1%, while exact-match accuracy is maintained at 57.9% versus 57.5%. In an anonymized expert assessment of 131 randomly selected test cases, organic synthesis experts prefer RetroRLFF over EditRetro in 67.9% of comparisons. These results support forward feedback as a training signal for improving forward consistency while retaining top-1 reference recovery. Expert assessment provides complementary evidence of synthetic feasibility on the selected cases, although neither forward consistency nor expert preference establishes experimental feasibility.
### Title:
          Compute-Optimal Scaling Laws for Spiking Language Models
 - **Authors:** Dan Saunders, Benjamin Ren, Catherine He
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Computation and Language (cs.CL); Neural and Evolutionary Computing (cs.NE)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Spiking neural networks target energy-efficient language modeling through sparse, binary activations, but the efficiency-accuracy tradeoff of spiking language models is uncharacterized at scale. We present the first Chinchilla-style IsoFLOP scaling study of a spiking language model, sweeping SpikeGPT-family models (RWKV backbone) from width 384 to 1536 across compute budgets from $3\times10^{17}$ to $10^{19}$ FLOPs on FineWeb-EDU. We report two findings. First, the compute-optimal frontier is $N_{opt} \propto C^{0.56}$, close to the 0.50 that Hoffmann et al. report for dense Transformers, so spiking does not change how parameters and data should be traded off under a compute budget. Second, against non-spiking RWKV and Transformer baselines trained the same way, the loss gap splits into a backbone cost of about 0.16 nats that does not change with compute and a spiking cost that falls from 0.34 to about 0.23 nats and then levels off. These results give the first compute-optimal recipe for spiking language models and quantify the accuracy cost of their sparsity.
### Title:
          Sensor-Aware Joint Data Fusion and State Estimation under Sparse Multi-rate Distribution Grid Sensing
 - **Authors:** Pooja Algikar, Stefano Fenu, Kumarsinh Jhala, Siby Plathottam
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Distribution-system state estimation is a growing necessity for the modern power grid as the addition of local storage and generation add system complexity that an operator or aggregator is not able to directly monitor. Classical methods work well when all system elements are time-synchronized and at least partly observable, but multi-rate monitoring of sparse heterogeneous sensors typically relies on machine learning approaches. It is a common practice in the literature to structure this task as a two-stage pipeline, first fusing data from disparate sensors and then feeding the fused pseudo-measurements to a separately trained state estimator. We propose a heterogeneous latent graph ODE in which measurement functions and sampling processes are typed and localized on specific buses, paired with a graph- transformer state estimator solving DSSE specifically for multi-rate heterogeneous sensor placements, and demonstrate its effectiveness across multiple IEEE standard topologies as observability degrades.
### Title:
          LexiCan: Lexicographic Canonicalization of Point Clouds for Machine Learning Applications
 - **Authors:** Daniel Sanchez, Djordje Mihajlovic, Federica Valeau, Luka Benić, Raghuwansh Raj, Soutrik Sarangi, Snir Hordan
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Canonicalization resolves the rotational and permutation ambiguity of 3D point clouds by selecting a unique orbit representative, maintaining their geometric information while allowing standard architectures, such as Transformers, to operate on them. Existing canonicalizations, however, exhibit discontinuity jumps on molecular configurations and non-trivial invariance errors in popular molecular datasets, which lead to ambiguity in their representation. To bridge this gap, we introduce LexiCan, a canonicalization that is provably invariant under either $O(3)$ or $SO(3)$, empirically invariant to rotations to within $\sim 10^{-6}$ angstroms on the QM9 and QM-sym molecular datasets, and mitigates discontinuity jumps on QM-sym, while running orders of magnitude faster than an existing canonicalization. Finally, non-invariant models perform better on QM9 property prediction and ModelNet40 shape classification with LexiCan than with that canonicalization.
### Title:
          DeltaNet-PC: Test-Time Memory Without Backpropagation Through Time
 - **Authors:** Todd Morrill, Richard Zemel
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Test-time memorization of sequential information is a foundational capability for any intelligent system that needs to rapidly store and retrieve data, as demonstrated by the attention mechanism in large language models. However, Transformers and linear attention variants are trained via backpropagation through time (BPTT), which has drawbacks such as its enormous memory requirements. Predictive coding (PC), an algorithm that optimizes a prediction-error energy function with local updates, offers a compelling solution but still lacks test-time memory for sequential data. We develop a solution to this problem by treating the per-step memory update $\Delta S_t$ as a latent variable, as opposed to a slow weight to be learned. By taking the log probability of a standard PC-based probabilistic model, we derive a PC-native solution for verbatim sequence memory, and relate our update rule to common choices in the linear attention literature (e.g., DeltaNet). We present an extensive set of empirical tests to probe our system's properties, including its scaling capacity, memory footprint, and ability to infer a context from evidence (e.g., bank (river) vs. bank (money)). We show how DeltaNet-PC can be used for storing single sequences of data, multiple sequences of data, and key-value associative memory pairs with perfect recall accuracy.
### Title:
          Understanding Unified VLMs: From Attention Probing to Guidance Tuning for Multimodal Generation
 - **Authors:** Zhengyang Luo, Xiaoyu Qiu, Zhiheng Tan, Yuchen Liang, Yingbin Liang, Liyue Shen
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Unified vision-language models (VLMs) perform both text-to-image (T2I) and image-to-text (I2T) generation with one shared Transformer backbone, yet how the backbone processes the condition in either direction remains poorly understood. We study this question from the perspective of conditional guidance: we probe every attention head to show the activation difference between two branches of classifier-free guidance (CFG) with different conditional guidance. On two unified VLMs of autoregressive and masked-diffusion paradigms, the probing reveals a subset of heads that are more sensitive to the change of condition while others are agnostic to it. This characteristic pattern holds in both T2I and I2T generation and is largely stable across data samples, demonstrating the internal mechanism of unified VLMs. Motivated by this, we propose a new inference-time intervention approach for unified multimodal generation, named \textit{HeadTuned-CFG}, which uses the probed condition-change awareness to select and tune the conditional guidance at the attention-head level. Experiments show consistent benefits in both multimodal generation directions of unified VLMs without additional training, and in particular substantial improvements over strong baselines in T2I generation. These results further support the key finding of our probing analysis: the observed pattern of functional specialization is an intrinsic property of unified VLMs.
### Title:
          ProWorld: Multi-View World Model with Proprioceptive Feedback for Closed-Loop Policy Evaluation
 - **Authors:** Zijun Wang, Tianyi Yan, Haokun Lin, Panwen Hu, Bohang He, Siyu Ren, Xiaoyu Guo, Weijia Liufu, Shuoshuo Xue, Fengjiao Chen, Zipeng Ye, Xuezhi Cao, Zutao Jiang, Hanhui Li, Xiaodan Liang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Evaluating robot manipulation policies through repeated physical trials is costly. We study whether an action-conditioned world model can serve as a closed-loop test environment whose policy success rates track those on real robots. Beyond realistic video, the model must return the camera views and proprioceptive state needed for the policy's next action and preserve failed-action outcomes to avoid overestimating policy capability. We introduce ProWorld, a world model that jointly predicts multi-view video and robot state, then feeds both back to a frozen policy for repeated interaction. ProWorld comprises a shared transformer for joint video-state flow prediction and Shared-Time View Encoding to distinguish cameras while preserving temporal synchronization. To reduce interaction hallucinations that turn missed grasps into successful lifts, we augment training with failed interactions collected in high-fidelity simulation, reducing inflated success rates in the generated environment. ProWorld achieves the highest overall TriWorldBench score among the compared methods. Across three policies and four local manipulation tasks, it attains a Pearson correlation of 0.95 and a Spearman correlation of 0.94 between generated and real success rates. These results support joint visual-proprioceptive feedback and failure-aware training as a basis for world-model-based policy evaluation.
### Title:
          Channel Match: Calibrating Shape Latents for Few-Step 3D Generation
 - **Authors:** Di Yang, Zhihao Li, Yanhai Xiong, Yufei Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Recent native 3D generators achieve high-quality asset creation by modeling structured 3D latents with flow-based transformers, but their high-resolution generation remains computationally expensive due to costly iterative sampling. In this work, we introduce a few-step distillation framework for native 3D generation, enabled by continuous self-consistency training with a custom JVP FlashAttention kernel and an analytic velocity regression objective for sparse structured 3D latents. To stabilize joint optimization, we further introduce an adaptive loss weighting strategy that automatically balances them without manual tuning. Beyond acceleration, we reveal a previously overlooked failure mode of few-step native 3D generation: sampling-induced latent distribution drift preserves geometric correctness but disrupts downstream PBR texture synthesis, causing texture artifacts on geometrically valid assets. To address this issue, we propose Channel Match, a training-free adaptive per-channel latent calibration method that restores the latent distribution without modifying either generator. We validate our approach on TRELLIS.2 at the $1024^3$ resolution setting. Our two-step model achieves $4.5\times$ faster geometry sampling while maintaining geometry quality comparable to the many-step teacher, and Channel Match further improves rendering quality by recovering the latent interface between geometry and appearance generation.
### Title:
          BaT-SAM: Introducing Inductive Bias into SAM via a Mixture of Convolutional Experts for Brain Tumor Segmentation
 - **Authors:** Sumiaya Jannat, Md Mahedi Hasan, Md Shamimul Islam, Md Nur Hossain
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Accurate brain tumor segmentation from magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) is essential for diagnosis and treatment planning; however, manual delineation is labor-intensive and time-consuming. Existing approaches are either task-specific with limited generalization or rely on user-provided prompts during inference. We present BaT-SAM, a parameter-efficient adaptation framework that enables fully automatic brain tumor segmentation with the Segment Anything Model (SAM). BaT-SAM injects local inductive bias into SAM's frozen Vision Transformer (ViT) encoder via Conv-LoRA, where image features are processed by a sparsely gated mixture of multi-scale convolutional experts. This design compensates for the limited translation-equivariant inductive bias of vanilla ViTs while fine-tuning fewer than 5M parameters (less than 1% of the full model). To remove the need for user prompts, we further introduce a lightweight self-prompting box regressor that is jointly optimized with the adapter using scheduled sampling and predicts tumor locations directly from image embeddings. On the BRISC2025 benchmark, BaT-SAM achieves a weighted mean IoU of 0.832 while outperforming state-of-the-art benchmarks. It further demonstrates strong cross-dataset generalization, achieving Dice scores of 0.702 on Figshare and 0.784 on the LGG MRI Segmentation dataset.
### Title:
          MultiFlow: Vision-Driven Multimodal fMRI Encoding via Stimulus-Informed Source Flow Matching
 - **Authors:** Bui Van Hung, Hoang-Son Vo, Mai Duc Minh Huy, Dang Thanh Vu, Soo-Hyung Kim
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Current fMRI encoding models frame prediction as deterministic regression, yet identical stimuli evoke a distribution of neural responses (arising from physiological noise, hemodynamic variability, and cognitive fluctuations) that a single point estimate cannot capture. This work introduces MultiFlow, a generative fMRI encoding framework based on Conditional Flow Matching (CFM) and a formulation termed Stimulus-Informed Source Flow Matching (SISFM). SISFM generalises CFM by conditioning the source distribution on the stimulus: a learnable temporal convolution shapes the source mean with an HRF-length receptive field, while a variance head shrinks its scale, shortening the expected transport path. SISFM is instantiated with a Diffusion Transformer (DiT-X) decoder parameterising the velocity field, with sub-layer Attention Residuals, heterogeneous fusion of ten frozen vision, audio, language, and audiovisual backbones, and per-subject Yeo-network heads. On the Algonauts 2025 dataset, a single MultiFlow checkpoint recovers most of the benefit of large deterministic ensembles: it trails the 1000-model TRIBE ensemble in-distribution by 0.003 PCC (0.3163 vs. 0.3195), and the strongest in- and out-of-distribution ensembles (VIBE-20, MedARC-50) by 0.010 and 0.009 PCC, using a single trained model rather than an ensemble of 20-1000. The same checkpoint also provides stimulus-conditioned uncertainty estimates and sample-based diversity.
### Title:
          Video-Based Sexual Harassment Detection Using Fine-Tuned Video Transformers
 - **Authors:** Jubayer- Al- Mahmud, Md Shamimul Islam, Md Nur Hossain
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Sexual harassment represents a pervasive societal challenge, with a substantial proportion of incidents remaining unreported due to insufficient evidence and delayed intervention mechanisms. Smart surveillance systems capable of detecting such incidents in real-time could assist law enforcement and serve as a deterrent. However, video-based harassment detection poses significant challenges, including variations in clothing, illumina- tion, partial occlusions, low resolution, and diverse camera view- points. Despite its social importance, this problem has received limited research attention, primarily due to the scarcity of large- scale annotated video datasets. To address this gap, we extend the Sexual Harassment Video (SHV) dataset to 600 short video clips sourced from YouTube and annotated into harassment and non-harassment categories using consistent labeling guidelines. We fine-tune three state-of-the-art video transformer models, VideoMAE, ViViT, and TimeSformer, pretrained on Kinetics- 400, for binary harassment classification. Experimental results show that all three models achieve competitive classification performance, with TimeSformer attaining the highest test accu- racy of 92.27%, outperforming VideoMAE 91.71% and ViViT 91.16% on the extended SHV test set. Our analysis reveals that performance is notably influenced by dataset size, video quality, frame sampling strategy, and model architecture. This work presents a comprehensive evaluation of modern video transformers for this sensitive real-world task.
### Title:
          A Sovereign, Indic-First, Reasoning Model
 - **Authors:** Sutanshu Saksena Raj, Prasanjith Pasunuti, Parijat Chatterjee, Daksh Maan, Purusharth Malik, Nayanjit Sarkar, Avinash Benki
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Gnani-Evon-v3.3-30B-A3B is a long-context hybrid Mamba-Transformer mixture-of-experts model for reasoning, instruction following, and tool use in English and eight Indian languages. Starting from NVIDIA's Nemotron-3-Nano-30B-A3B checkpoint, it retains the 52-layer backbone and changes the vocabulary, training data, and post-training objectives. An in-place expansion from 131,072 to 163,072 tokens reduces mean Indian-language fertility by 38.6%. The new embedding rows are initialized from their upstream decompositions and warmed before the backbone is updated. Continual pretraining then adds broad English-Indic data, high-quality annealing, and direct training at a 131,072-token sequence length. The central result appears after post-training. Reinforcement learning across four verifiable task environments improves mathematics, graduate-level science, and instruction following, but degrades Indic extractive question answering because the rewards do not require an answer in the requested language. The share of Indic extraction items answered in English rises from 8.7% to 91.7%. Accuracy conditional on answering in the target script roughly doubles, which is consistent with preserved reading ability but is measured on a self-selected subset. Two unreleased studies test repairs. A scoped Indic reward restores the language convention. A 26B-A4B generative judge outperforms a 235B-parameter model on RM-Bench, and distillation restores 81% of the capabilities displaced by optimizing against it. Relative to the architecture-identical starting checkpoint, the released model gains 5.2 points on an Indic aggregate while giving up 1.7 points of English retention. The unreleased Indic-aware branch trades further, gaining 14.6 Indic points at a cost of 5.4 in English. A planned FP8 release reduces the artifact to 32GB with near-zero mean change across 32 paired benchmarks.
### Title:
          Sori: Streamable and Controllable Model for Non-musical Audio to Symbolic Music Transformation
 - **Authors:** Kyungsu Kim, Yejin Kim, Kyogu Lee
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Sound (cs.SD)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 We introduce Non-musical Audio to Symbolic Music Transformation (NASMT), a novel task that converts non-musical audio into symbolic music representations. Unlike automatic music transcription, which assumes musical input and a unique correct score, NASMT is inherently one-to-many and requires evaluation along two axes: input correspondence and musicality. We present Sori, an encoder--decoder Transformer that addresses this task through domain adversarial training (DAT), which bridges the gap between musical and non-musical audio in the encoder's feature space, and classifier-free guidance (CFG) with a continuous parameter~$\gamma$ that controls the trade-off between correspondence and musicality at inference time. Across objective metrics and a listening study, Sori provides effective continuous control via $\gamma$ and compares favorably with instrument-agnostic transcription models. Sori supports real-time streaming on a consumer CPU. Audio examples are available at this https URL.
### Title:
          GNFormer: Gaussian Neural Field Transformer for Efficient Camera-based Occupancy Prediction
 - **Authors:** Guangcheng Chen, Li He, Hong Zhang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Camera-based occupancy prediction provides geometric and semantic scene understanding for robot perception and has been adopted in autonomous vehicle and UAV perception systems. Gaussian primitives offer a promising alternative by potentially representing only occupied regions, thereby concentrating computation and representational capacity where needed and reducing wasted computation and memory in empty space. However, existing Gaussian-based methods suffer from poor primitive utilization, as ineffective training objectives leave some Gaussians in empty space, while the limited expressiveness of Gaussian functions restricts their ability to model complex shapes. To address these limitations, we propose a novel 3D representation, Gaussian Neural Field (GNF), which can accurately map voxel positions to semantic occupancies. In GNF, Gaussians and a neural network jointly parameterize the field, where the Gaussians define its spatial support and provide features for neural decoding, while the neural network enables more expressive occupancy modeling than Gaussian functions alone. We further develop the Gaussian Neural Field Transformer (GNFormer), a streamlined and flexible method that predicts GNFs for camera-based occupancy prediction. Across Occ-ScanNet, SSCBench-KITTI-360, and nuScenes-SurroundOcc benchmarks, GNFormer achieves the highest IoU and mIoU and the fastest inference among the compared single-frame methods. In the experiments, GNFormer can represent indoor and outdoor scenes with only 256 and 1024 Gaussians, respectively, substantially fewer primitives than prior Gaussian-based methods. The code will be released.
### Title:
          Sleep Sound Event Detection Powered by Learnable Multi-Resolution Adaptive Line Enhancer
 - **Authors:** Chanwoo Park, Chanwoo Kim
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Sound (cs.SD)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Sleep-disordered breathing, including snoring, hypopnea, and obstructive apnea, is the hallmark of obstructive sleep apnea, whose severity is quantified by the Apnea-Hypopnea Index (AHI). As conventional polysomnography is costly and clin- ically demanding, microphone-only detection has emerged as a noninvasive alternative for screening. We propose ALE- Confidence Fusion Sound Event Detection (ACF-SED), the first framework that directly injects the per-frame confidence map of an Adaptive Line Enhancer (ALE) into Transformer atten- tion biasing and feature modulation, rather than as mere pre- processing. A Multi-Resolution ALE Bank aggregates three parallel NLMS filters at distinct decorrelation delays, while a Confidence-Guided Cross-Path block couples event and noise streams via gated cross-attention.
