On the Audio-Polygraphy Dataset for Sleep Apnea Analysis, ACF-SED achieves state- of-the-art detection across Event-F1, Segment-F1, and PSDS, enabling end-to-end AHI estimation.
### Title:
          Coarse-Grained Predictive Resource Allocation Framework enabling In-Premise XR Collaborations
 - **Authors:** Sourav Mondal, Elaine Wong
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Networking and Internet Architecture (cs.NI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Immersive extended reality (XR) applications are key enablers of human-centric connectivity in next-generation networks. However, meeting their high-throughput, ultra-low-latency, and high-reliability demands while preventing cyber-sickness remains a critical challenge, especially in environments with non-deterministic wireless connectivity. Fiber-To-The-Room (FTTR), extending widely deployed Fiber-To-The-Home (FTTH) infrastructure, offers a promising solution. Yet, satisfying the stringent Quality-of-Experience (QoE) requirements of XR collaborators demands robust resource allocation across the multi-tier cascade of FTTH, FTTR, and WiFi segments. In this paper, we propose a coarse-grained traffic forecasting-empowered proactive resource allocation and intelligent in-premise roaming framework. Here, artificial intelligence (AI) model complexities are scaled to the computational capabilities of each network segment. At the FTTR segment, we employ a hybrid temporal convolutional and bi-directional long short-term memory (TCN-BiLSTM) network for multi-modal XR traffic forecasting, integrating passive optical network (PON) and WiFi access points with multi-link operation (MLO) and orthogonal frequency-division multiple access (OFDMA). The resulting long-horizon forecasts reduce inference frequency, enabling coordinated FTTR-WiFi resource allocation and seamless network-orchestrated mobility across rooms. At the FTTH segment, we deploy Transformer-based time-series models (e.g., Informer, Autoformer, PatchTST, and TFT) to predict the complex XR traffic aggregated in FTTR segments for transmission over the FTTH backhaul. Evaluations show Transformer-based models are up to $\sim$75% more accurate in long-horizon forecasting than the hybrid model. Extensive simulations confirm the proposed framework significantly outperforms traditional methods in satisfying XR QoE requirements.
### Title:
          MERIT: Scaling KG Foundation Models with Relation Transformers
 - **Authors:** Cosimo Gregucci, Daniel Hernandez, Antonio Vergari, Steffen Staab
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Knowledge graph (KG) foundation models (KGFMs) predict links on unseen graphs zero-shot. KGFMs have been pioneered by ULTRA which builds a relation graph from co-occurrences that match motif templates, and passes messages over it to obtain relation representations. More recent KGFMs such as MOTIF and TRIX increase the size of this relation graph, and FLOCK replaces message-passing with probabilistic random-walk ensembles. However, larger relation graphs mean more messages, and more walks mean more sequences to encode, so they improve performance at higher training and inference cost. This cost hinders their use in more complex reasoning tasks, such as complex query answering. To reverse this trend, we design MERIT, the first KGFM with a transformer relation encoder: every relation name attends to every other, and the motifs enter as a soft prior rather than as edges. Over 56 benchmarks, MERIT matches the zero-shot MRR of SoTA KGFMs while being 6x faster than TRIX and over 100x faster than FLOCK at inference. Fine-tuned for complex query answering like UltraQuery, MERITQuery improves over it at matched cost.
### Title:
          The Mathematical Collective Unconscious: Jungian Archetypes within the Latent Space
 - **Authors:** Andrew Breen
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Carl Jung theorised that the human psyche contains an inherited framework, the collective unconscious, populated by universal archetypes. As large language models (LLMs) are trained on vast corpora of textual data, this paper investigates whether they spontaneously construct distinct archetype-like regions within their high-dimensional latent spaces. The paper proposes that these regions function as spatial constraints that naturally organise semantically related narratives. To evaluate this, we applied cosine similarity and angular distance metrics across thirteen experiments utilising OpenAIs text-embedding-3-small model. The paper contains two experimental sections the first section explores these ideas using synthetically constructed narratives, the results of which act as a baselines for experiments conducted in the second section where the synthetic data is replaced by human-authored literature (Shmoop corpus), unedited dream reports (DreamBank corpus), and culturally distilled folklore (Andrew Langs Fairy Books). The results show that archetypal roles occupy distinct, measurable regions in the latent space. Crucially, these regions remain compact and consistent because of narrative structure rather than simple word overlap. Additionally, text category dictates spatial distribution: dream reports concentrate within Trickster and Shadow regions, while traditional folklore distributes evenly across all tested archetypes. We conclude that transformer latent spaces encode stable regions which appear to correspond to recognised Jungian archetypes.
### Title:
          DRanker: A Transformer-Based Multi-Task Ranking Model for the Disney+ Homepage
 - **Authors:** Priya Nirmal Singh Khokher, Daniel Nemirovsky, Benjamin P. Whitesell, Ethan Sukrae Lee, Eric Wulf, Tom Chee-Duffy, Nicholas Flores
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 We present DRanker (Disney+ Ranker), a transformer-based multitask ranking model serving personalized recommendations on the Disney+ homepage. We describe the encoder-only architecture over enriched engagement-history sequences paired with a lightweight causal debiasing mechanism, and the inference stack that enables transformer-based ranking at scale. Through iterative development, we systematically evaluated more complex alternatives and found that simpler designs consistently outperformed them on offline metrics, qualitative behavioral evaluation, and in production. Deployed globally, DRanker drives over 1% lift in our key engagement metric and 0.5% lift in daily active users across all experiences powered by the ranker. We share practical lessons on data engineering, debiasing, and architectural choices that shaped our ranking system at scale.
### Title:
          History Replays Itself: Unleashing Long-Context Capability in Linear Attention
 - **Authors:** Li Nanbo, Firas Laakom, Yilan Zhang, Piotr Piękos, Yimeng Chen, Liangyu Wang, Yifan Xu, Mateusz Ostaszewski, Dandan Guo, Wenyi Wang, Yanning Dai, Yuhui Wang, Jürgen Schmidhuber
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Linear attention promises the efficiency needed for long-context language modelling, yet its fixed-size recurrent state must simultaneously support ongoing computation and preserve information from an ever-growing history. This coupling has reinforced a prevailing view that the state's memory capacity defines the model's long-context capability. We challenge this presumed ceiling: \emph{recurrent computation need not bear the full burden of long-term storage}. We introduce NoteBank, a sparse episodic memory that archives selected text before future queries are known, uses the backbone's native features for writing and addressing, and reads retrieved evidence through its native forward computation via replay. This \emph{training-free, plug-and-play} interface requires no external embedding model, separately trained retriever, or separate neural reader. At one million tokens, it raises Gated DeltaNet's mean accuracy across six RULER retrieval tasks from 1.7% to 70.3%, with no observed significant retrieval degradation from 4K to 1M tokens. This recovery adds $\leqslant$0.025% to analytical FLOPs, with persistent context storage approximately 1/2,600 that of a Transformer's full MHA KV cache at matched width, depth, and precision. Experiments extend from three 1.3B recurrent backbones to Mamba-2-8B and post-trained Qwen3.5-9B, demonstrating gains on LongBench v1/v2 and MemoryAgentBench. Together, these results show that sparse evidence storage and native replay can substantially expand the long-context capability of pretrained linear attention models while retaining the efficiency of recurrent computation.
### Title:
          SlotAVS: Object-Centric Audio-Visual Segmentation via Bi-Modal Slot Attention
 - **Authors:** Arnav Sharma, Anand Mishra, Renu Rameshan
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Audio-Visual Segmentation (AVS) requires predicting pixel-wise masks of sounding objects by grounding acoustic signals in visual space. In multi-source scenes, concurrent sound sources often remain entangled within shared visual representations, leading to merged masks or unstable specialization when using standard alignment-focused transformer architectures. To address this, we present SlotAVS, an object-centric framework driven by competitive bi-modal slot attention. While robust foundation models provide rich semantic priors, SlotAVS shifts the paradigm from global cross-modal fusion to explicit competitive binding. By initializing discrete object slots from temporal audio sequences and enforcing strict slot-wise competition over spatial visual tokens, our method structurally mitigates the blending of distinct acoustic entities. Further, to stabilize instance-level learning, we introduce a Hungarian matching strategy that optimally aligns slots with ground-truth objects using spatial and boundary overlap. Coupled with a numerically stable, confidence-aware mask fusion mechanism, SlotAVS robustly aggregates multi-source predictions. Extensive experiments on the AVSBench dataset demonstrate the efficacy of our approach, achieving 88.6% mIoU on the Single-Source (S4) split and 71.7% mIoU on the Multi-Source (MS3) split. These results show that explicit competitive binding is critical for complex audio-visual scene understanding and effectively leveraging foundation-model priors without relying solely on their capacity.
### Title:
          An Explainable System for Offensive Content Detection in Nastaliq Urdu: Corpus Curation, Transformer Benchmarking, and Word-Level Attribution
 - **Authors:** Muhammad Shahid Iqbal Malik
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Automated moderation systems are deployed at scale, yet they remain opaque to the users and moderators affected by their decisions, and their coverage is almost entirely confined to English. For Urdu written in the Nastaliq script, no such system exists, and the annotated resources it would be built on are drawn largely from Twitter/X, where character limits constrain how users write. Interpretability has received little attention, leaving it unclear what such models actually learn. This study addresses these gaps in three steps. First, we curate and publicly release a corpus of 7604 Nastaliq Urdu Facebook comments, extending an earlier corpus by correcting text-level inconsistencies and re-examining the original labels. Second, we fine-tune four Urdu-capable transformers under a common grid-search protocol and compare them against twelve feature-classifier baselines on identical data partitions. The best configuration, Multilingual Representations for Indian Languages (MuRIL), reaches 87.05% accuracy and 87.06% macro F1, improving on the strongest baseline by 5.52 percentage points, a margin that exceeds the 95% confidence interval on test accuracy; the two leading transformers differ by less than the test set can resolve, and performance is stable across five optimizers. Third, we compute word-level SHAP attributions adapted to right-to-left rendering. Attribution mass concentrates on high-frequency slurs but extends to syntactically neutral function words and to political vocabulary characteristic of the source pages, indicating that aggregate performance may rest partly on lexical and topical regularities rather than a generalizable representation of offensiveness.
### Title:
          Benchmarking Deep Learning Models for Anomaly Detection in Real-World Cloud Telemetry
 - **Authors:** Sumit Dhyani, Sougata Mukherjea
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Distributed, Parallel, and Cluster Computing (cs.DC)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Modern cloud platforms generate high-dimensional, noisy telemetry from thousands of microservices. Unlike classical anomaly detection benchmarks, cloud anomalies are short-lived, irregular, and largely non-periodic, making timely detection challenging. We present an empirical benchmark of deep learning models for anomaly detection using a real-world IBM Cloud Console dataset. We evaluate GRU, Transformer, TimesNet, and Autoformer architectures using the Numenta Anomaly Benchmark (NAB), a timing-aware metric that accounts for early detection and false alarms. TimesNet achieves the highest NAB scores due to strong early-detection sensitivity. Transformer models also detect anomalies early but incur higher false-positive rates. An improved GRU autoencoder, incorporating robust scoring and persistence rules, achieves competitive performance with a more stable false-alarm profile. Overall, the modest performance differences suggest that real cloud anomalies are dominated by short-term deviations rather than persistent periodic structure, emphasizing the importance of timing-aware evaluation and robust post-processing.
### Title:
          KhyontekAI at CheckThat! 2026: Multilingual Dense Retrieval and Cross-Encoder Reranking for Scientific Web Claims
 - **Authors:** Bhargab Jyoti Bhuyan, Pritam Deka
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Information Retrieval (cs.IR); Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Scientific claims shared through social media platforms frequently reference research findings without explicitly linking to the corresponding scientific publications. Automatically retrieving the correct scientific source for such claims is a challenging multilingual retrieval problem involving semantic matching, paraphrasing, noisy social media text, and cross-domain language variation. In this work, we present a multilingual two-stage retrieval framework for CLEF 2026 CheckThat! Task 1 on Source Retrieval for Scientific Web Claims. Our system combines fine-tuned dense retrieval models with cross-encoder reranking for improving multilingual scientific document retrieval. We evaluate multiple transformer-based dense retrieval architectures including multilingual-E5, BGE, and Contriever under different metadata configurations. We further investigate multilingual joint training, retrieval depth analysis, metadata enrichment, and language balancing strategies for improving retrieval effectiveness. Experimental results demonstrate that metadata enrichment consistently improves retrieval effectiveness, while fine-tuned cross-encoder reranking provides additional gains in retrieval precision. Our final multilingual system achieves MRR@5 scores of 0.6222 for English, 0.5112 for German, and 0.6254 for French, with an overall average MRR@5 score of 0.5862.
### Title:
          Frequency-Step Cache: Separating Synchronization and Repair for Efficient Autoregressive Video Diffusion
 - **Authors:** Kerry Xu, Param Misra, Zachary Stenglein, Athri Karunamurthy, Fanjiang Ye, Yuke Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Autoregressive (AR) video generation enables long-horizon synthesis but requires repeated forward passes through large diffusion transformers. Training-free caching reduces this cost by reusing intermediate computation, yet existing controllers often couple two decisions: when to synchronize the cache exactly and how much computation to repair between synchronizations. We introduce Frequency-Step Cache (FSC), which separates these roles. FSC schedules exact full-computation anchors on a dense-to-sparse trajectory, while a lightweight latent-space risk based on frequency-resolved drift and token age controls inter-anchor repair through whole-step replay, selective token recomputation, or full repair. On SkyReels-V2, whole-step reuse provides most of the acceleration, while selective token computation is most effective as fidelity repair. A controlled ablation shows that using one reactive signal for both synchronization and repair reduces fidelity at matched latency, with the same separation advantage observed on FramePack and Matrix-Game. Across multiple autoregressive video backbones, FSC improves speed-quality trade-offs. On SkyReels-V2, FSC-Speed reaches 2.36x speedup versus MotionCache's 2.275x while improving SSIM by 0.019 and PSNR by 0.5 dB. At MotionCache-matched speed, FSC-Quality improves SSIM by 0.031 and PSNR by 1.0 dB and lowers LPIPS by 0.024. Without retuning, FSC-Quality transfers to SkyReels-V2 14B at 2.12x speedup versus 1.79x for MotionCache.
### Title:
          Harmonic Representations in Symbolic Music Transformers are Localized and Controllable
 - **Authors:** Adi Silberschein, Megan Wei, Tamar Rott Shaham
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Sound (cs.SD); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Transformer models trained on symbolic music have become remarkably capable. However, it is yet unclear how they represent music structure, and whether understanding their internal representations can be used to unlock methods for model control. We examine this through a case study of harmonic analysis: when a music transformer navigates a cadence, what does it internally "know" about its harmonic context, and can that knowledge be used to affect the generation? Using Aria, a LLaMA-based symbolic music transformer evaluated on Bach chorales, we find that harmonic decisions are causally localized progressively from middle layers onward. Linear probes show that mode becomes decodable in the same layer range where patching identifies growing causal effect, while key identity emerges later. We then confirm these representations are actionable for music generation control: steering via latent directions corresponding to harmonic changes reliably produces a deceptive or minor resolution, while preserving structural coherence and generation quality. This demonstrates that, at inference time, the model's internal harmonic knowledge can be directly manipulated to control the harmonic output.
### Title:
          Softmax-Free Attention in Spiking Transformers as the Zero-Temperature Limit of Two-Level Systems
 - **Authors:** Roberto C. Sotero, Pouya Taghipour, Jose M. Sanchez-Bornot
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Spiking Transformers achieve energy efficiency by discarding softmax normalization in favor of thresholded, binary attention. However, this removal breaks the thermodynamic framework used to analyze attention networks, as the normalized measure and partition function cease to exist. We restore this theoretical foundation by proving that softmax-free spiking attention operates exactly as the zero-temperature limit of a product of independent two-level systems. This correspondence places spiking attention strictly in the frozen ordered phase of the thermodynamic phase diagram for all inputs. Consequently, the entropic cost of organizing attention collapses, replaced by a Landauer-type physical floor governed by action-potential generation. Furthermore, this ordered-phase pinning mathematically explains the optimization difficulty caused by vanishing surrogate gradients. We validate this theory with forward-relaxation experiments, placing both standard and neuromorphic attention mechanisms on a unified thermodynamic axis.
### Title:
          BARTER: Compact Dynamic Sparse Training with Standard Dense Kernels
 - **Authors:** Yueyuan Sui, Payal Mohapatra, Qi Zhu, Stephen Xia
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Dynamic sparse training (DST) aims to reduce training costs while maintaining accuracy. Existing DST methods mask out parameters but keep matrix dimensions unchanged. Training speedups therefore depend on specialized support, such as sparse kernels. We introduce BARTER, a structured DST framework that reduces training cost using standard dense kernels by keeping training tensors physically compact while dynamically reallocating capacity across layers. Unlike existing works that require computing gradients for all parameters, BARTER instead uses statistics collected only from active units during ordinary backpropagation to prune and clone attention heads and feed-forward channels under separate fixed global budgets. At each topology update, it resizes the affected parameter tensors and remaps their optimizer states, so subsequent training steps operate directly on the compact model. Training and fine-tuning experiments with Transformer-based vision models on two image classification benchmarks at densities from $0.1$ to $0.7$ show competitive Top-1 accuracy relative to full-tensor DST baselines. On an NVIDIA L40, BARTER achieves speedups of up to $6.29\times$ in measured training segments and reduces peak allocated GPU memory by up to $81.3\%$ relative to these implementations. An iPhone 13 Pro Max training benchmark additionally shows a $2.78\times$ speedup over the full-tensor DST baseline.
### Title:
          LizTail: Unifying Shape Adaptation and Spatial Parallelism with Segment-Reconfigurable Systolic Arrays
 - **Authors:** Gang Liu, Chen Yang, Junyan Hu, Jianguo Chen, Lixin Duan
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Hardware Architecture (cs.AR)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Systolic arrays are widely used to accelerate neural-network inference, yet fixed two-dimensional arrays struggle with the substantial diversity in both matrix geometry and available parallelism across CNNs and Transformer-based large language models. This mismatch is particularly severe for skinny matrices, small-batch autoregressive decoding, and operators containing multiple independent partitions, leading to low processing-element (PE) utilization and repeated spatial folding. Existing reconfigurable arrays typically improve either shape adaptation or distributed execution, whereas supporting both through fine-grained routing incurs considerable interconnect, buffering, and control overheads. We present LizTail (named after lizard tail autotomy, where predefined fracture planes allow segments to detach and remain independently active, analogous to LizTail's predefined reconfiguration boundaries), a multi-mode systolic array that enables constrained segment-level reconfiguration by partitioning a $128\times128$ array into 32 regular $128\times4$ segments and adding connectivity only at predefined endpoints. The resulting substrate supports trimming, long-path extension through $180^\circ$ turn-back connections, and distributed execution in which each subarray can itself be trimmed or extended. Differentiated PE datapaths and segment-aware CEB/SIB buffers provide the required compute and data-delivery flexibility without unrestricted PE-level routing. A hardware-constrained mapper selects the execution organization, dataflow, task partition, and tiling. Across representative workloads, LizTail achieves geometric-mean speedups of $5.02\times$ on CNN inference and $25.67\times$ on LLM decoding over a TPU-like array, while reducing EDP by up to $38.23\times$. LizTail is open-sourced at this https URL.
### Title:
          Fisher information and the geometry of memorization in neural networks
 - **Authors:** Daniel T. Bernstein, Chase Goddard, Luca Di Carlo, Vudtiwat Ngampruetikorn, David J. Schwab
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Disordered Systems and Neural Networks (cond-mat.dis-nn); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 A fruitful approach towards understanding generalization in machine learning involves characterizing how models separate generalizable structure from idiosyncratic or even corrupted features of training data. Fisher information has emerged as a diagnostic tool for this separation, leveraging loss geometry to distinguish generalization from memorization. Here we investigate this connection in vision models trained on CIFAR-10 with controlled noise that can be learned only through memorization. We show that projecting model weights onto the top Fisher eigenvectors decouples general task performance from noisy sample memorization, and we investigate how this decoupling presents differentially across layers. We show that the same phenomena are present in small transformers trained to solve a synthetic in-context learning task, and are reproduced in closed form in a simple, analytically solvable linear model. Our results reveal that Fisher information captures aspects of the geometric structure underlying how neural networks allocate capacity between generalization and memorization.
### Title:
          Hardware-Friendly Token-Group Activation Quantization for Low-Bit Mamba Super-Resolution
 - **Authors:** Jinwoo Chung, Jangho Kim
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Mamba-based super-resolution models are efficient alternatives to Transformer-based restoration backbones, but their low-bit post-training quantization remains challenging because selective scan, Mamba's input-dependent state-space operator, induces different activation ranges across spatial tokens. Thus, a single layer-wise clipping range poorly matches all tokens. We propose Token-Group Quantization, a hardware-friendly activation PTQ framework that statically approximates a hardware-unfriendly post-scan grouping oracle, where tokens with similar preferred clipping ranges are assigned to the same group. To avoid runtime post-scan grouping in static low-bit inference, a lightweight predictor infers the groups from pre-scan features available before the quantized scan path. During calibration, we form pseudo-labels by clustering post-scan activation descriptors that capture similar rounding--clipping behavior. At inference, the frozen predictor maps pre-scan features to groups and selects entries from offline-calibrated group-wise clipping-bound tables, without runtime statistics collection, dynamic clipping updates, or post-scan group assignment. To address low-bit restoration degradation, we further introduce a Frequency-Preserving Refinement (FPR) objective for clipping-bound calibration. FPR aligns Fourier amplitude and phase between full-precision teacher and quantized student outputs to preserve high-frequency structures. Across PTQ protocols, bit-widths, backbones, and tasks, our method consistently outperforms prior Mamba and SR PTQ baselines, transfers to Transformer SR and Vision Mamba classification, composes with rotation-based quantization, and achieves 2.23x speedup on Jetson Orin Nano over FP16. Code is available here.
### Title:
          Spectral Considerations on Unsupervised Time-domain EEG Representation Learning with Deep Networks
 - **Authors:** David Aquilué-Llorens, Abel Puigseslloses, Angel Blanco, Claire Braboszcz, Aureli Soria-Frisch
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Electroencephalography (EEG) is a non-invasive, scalable source of quantitative biomarkers for the early diagnosis of brain disorders, and deep representation learning has become a powerful paradigm for extracting information-rich latent features from raw signals. However, neural networks tend to under-represent high-frequency information, a concern that is especially acute for EEG given its oscillatory nature and characteristic $1/f^\chi$ power decay, which risks masking disease-relevant high-frequency signatures. In this study, we systematically investigate the spectral constraints of unsupervised time-domain feature extraction across three autoencoder architectures (LSTMVAE, VAEEG, Transformer) and a state-of-the-art EEG foundation model. Using synthetic experiments and empirical validation, we show that while autoencoders capture macro-scale shifts such as alpha slowing, they remain biased against higher frequencies due to a magnitude-driven masking effect induced by the Mean Squared Error loss under 1/f-decaying spectra. Increasing latent dimensionality mitigates this attenuation but sacrifices latent compactness and generative stability. We argue that, as the field advances toward large-scale foundation-level feature extractors, it is imperative to move beyond simple time-domain reconstruction and to integrate spectral-domain loss terms and gradient-balancing techniques, so that the subtle high-frequency transients critical for diagnosing complex brain disorders are preserved in the latent representation.
### Title:
          Memory-Efficient Integer-Only Training of Deep Neural Networks
 - **Authors:** Hazem Hesham Yousef Shalby, Fabrizio Pittorino, Manuel Roveri
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Training neural networks in reduced precision can yield significant efficiency gains, yet all existing methods that scale to standard computer vision benchmarks retain floating-point in at least one stage, typically the weight update or optimizer state. We present a training algorithm that scales to modern deep neural networks, in which every tensor (weights, gradients, and activations) is stored as INT8, and every operation (forward pass, backward pass, weight update, optimizer update) is performed in integer arithmetic. Specifically, tensors are stored as signed integer mantissas paired with explicit power-of-two exponents, on which integer-only operations are performed using five custom composable building blocks (products, sums, divide-by-constant, nonlinear functions, and weight updates). This allows complete implementation of trainable integer layers, including convolutions, batch and layer normalization, softmax, and GELU. A per-group velocity mechanism with independent exponent tracking preserves gradient precision across neurons and channels with diverse magnitudes. As every tensor fits in INT8, training memory is roughly 4x smaller than the FP32 baseline. We validate our training algorithm on CNNs (ResNet-18/34/50/101, MobileNetV2) and Vision Transformers (ViT-Ti / B), demonstrating for the first time that training neural networks with INT8 tensors and integer arithmetic can achieve competitive accuracy on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100 and ImageNet at a reduced memory budget.
### Title:
          FON: A Draft-Revise Dual-Organ Language Model without Sequence-Level Quadratic Attention
 - **Authors:** Helong Hu (1), Hongdan Pan (2), Shuiqing Hu (3) ((1) Zhanjiang Campus, Guangdong Ocean University, Zhanjiang, Guangdong, China, (2) Maoming Administrative Service Center, Maoming, Guangdong, China, (3) China Pacific Insurance (Group) Co., Ltd. Foshan Branch, Foshan, Guangdong, China)
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Self-attention assigns two functions to one mechanism-where to read and how to mix-at the price of O(L^2) training, an O(T) decode state, and position signals bound to the training length. Existing attention-free alternatives all optimize the single function of generation. We propose FON (Fusion-Organ Network), which differentiates one weight stack into a drafting organ (multi-tap learnable delays + content-adaptive recursion; causal, O(S) training, O(1) decode state, no sequence-level quadratic attention) and a revision organ that re-processes the written prefix through multi-scale ripple kernels injected via weak gates, never generating. At 30M parameters, iso-parameter and iso-token budget, FON reaches perplexity 131.03 on WikiText-103, beating Transformer (197.73) and Mamba2 (152.53); the advantage persists under all depth-aligned baselines and at the 70M tier (-20.8%). At 8x length extrapolation FON degrades by ~8-9% without positional-encoding engineering, between ALiBi and RoPE. The revision channel provides a structural pathway for in-domain infilling that causal architectures lack in principle (+19% over on-demand coupling), at a perplexity cost indistinguishable within measurement precision. We report all costs and boundaries: impaired long-range retrieval, synthetic-task disadvantages, weaker out-of-domain code infilling than a same-recipe Transformer, and a 6.5-12.3x wall-clock gap.
### Title:
          BehaviorFormer: Discovering Behavioral Patterns via Clusterable Next-Token Prediction
 - **Authors:** Tobias Schneider, Danial Fahrner, Soren Antebi, Daniel Uedelhoven, Lennard Bodden, Sandra Halscheidt, Rafet Sifa
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Human-Computer Interaction (cs.HC)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Discovering behavioral patterns in open-ended games is challenging due to the lack of clear objectives or ground-truth annotations. In this work, we extend a next-token prediction Transformer with the deep clustering algorithm DCSS to learn meaningful representations with well-separated clusters. In a controlled Minecraft session with 26 players, the method discovers 23 distinct behavioral patterns that generalize across the studied players, further refining structures present in self-supervised representations into clearly separable clusters. We group the clusters into seven interpretable categories covering stationary, roaming, travel, mining, base work, logging, and farming behavior.
### Title:
          More Interactions Make Communications More Efficient: A Directed Information Bottleneck Approach to Task-Oriented Communication
 - **Authors:** Zhirun Zheng, Yiqin Deng, Phone Lin, Yuguang Fang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Information Theory (cs.IT)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Task-oriented communication improves communication efficiency by transmitting semantic information required for downstream tasks, but may incur redundancy with previously transmitted information. To address this issue, we propose a variational directed information bottleneck joint source-channel coding (VDIB-JSCC) framework that transmits only previously unacquired information. Specifically, we use directed information to characterize the causal flow of task-relevant incremental information and theoretically demonstrate its advantages over mutual information and conditional mutual information. Based on this metric, we formulate a directed information bottleneck (DIB) objective that balances task-relevant information preservation and redundant information compression. We further derive a variational formulation, termed VDIB, whose tractable upper bound serves as the training loss. The encoder integrates a Vision Transformer for frame-wise semantic extraction and a Mamba-based causal state-space model for capturing temporal dependencies and extracting incremental information, while the decoder uses a Mamba-based state-space model for task inference. Experiments on CIFAR-10 and CIFAR-100 video streams demonstrate that VDIB-JSCC achieves higher classification accuracy with less transmitted information than state-of-the-art semantic communication methods. These results show that leveraging historical knowledge enables task completion with less information, improving communication efficiency.
### Title:
          BAT3R: Robust Online 3D Reconstruction with Bayesian Adaptive State Updates
 - **Authors:** Lijian Li, Yuanpeng He, Yudian Zheng, Chung-ju Huang, Linyu Li, Dongming Jin, Wenpin Jiao, Zhi Jin, Chi-Man Pun
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Feed-forward transformer models have driven remarkable progress in 3D vision,yet their quadratic complexity renders them impractical for long video sequences. Streaming 3D reconstruction addresses this bottleneck by processing frames sequen?tially with constant memory. However, existing recurrent architectures inevitably suffer from progressive degradation over long sequences, as they fail to account for the intrinsic reliability of observations and the accumulated confidence of the historical state, leading to severe error accumulation. To address these issues, we introduce BAT3R, a training-free Bayesian adaptation framework comprising two synergistic modules: State Innovation Gating (SIG) and Bayesian-guided Adaptive State Updating (BASU). SIG acts as a distributional regularizer that anchors state evolution to its geometric initialization, effectively arresting representational drift during long-term inference. Building on this, BASU reformulates state update as a recursive Bayesian inference process within a Kalman filtering framework. By jointly modeling the temporal reliability of observations via information-theoretic gain and spatial relevance via cross-attention maps, BASU achieves an asymmetric spatio-temporal state fusion: well-consolidated historical regions are protected from redundant updates, while genuinely novel scene content is aggressively incor?porated. Extensive experiments on standard benchmarks (7-Scenes, TUM, ScanNet, KITTI, etc.) demonstrate that BAT3R consistently outperforms state-of-the-art online 3D reconstruction methods, with the most significant gains observed on long sequences and complex scenes.
### Title:
          WorldRoPE: Probabilistic World-Centric Rotary Embedding for Multi-View Attention
 - **Authors:** Mijeong Kim, Kyujin Lim, Bohyung Han
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Rotary Positional Embedding (RoPE) is widely used to encode relative positions in Transformers. In multi-view settings, however, patch coordinates are defined independently in each image: the same 3D point can therefore have different coordinates across views, making them insufficient for establishing cross-view correspondences. We introduce WorldRoPE, a probabilistic world-centric RoPE for multi-view attention. At each layer, WorldRoPE back-projects each patch token into a set of volumetric Gaussian primitives in a shared world frame. These primitives represent multiple plausible 3D supports for a token, jointly capturing alternative depth hypotheses, depth uncertainty, and the finite spatial extent of its patch frustum. When a key token's support is projected into a query view, it induces a distribution over where that key may appear in the query image. WorldRoPE integrates the rotary operator over this distribution in closed form, combining rotations at the projected primitive centers according to their mixture weights with covariance-dependent attenuation. This makes attention less sensitive to uncertain key locations in the query view, without rasterizing or warping token features. The formulation reduces to deterministic RoPE when the support collapses to a point. Across layers, the predicted geometry guides cross-view attention, while the updated features refine the geometry used at the next layer. Experiments on novel view synthesis, depth estimation, and 3D geometry reconstruction demonstrate the effectiveness of WorldRoPE for multi-view Transformers.
### Title:
          RIFT: Token Influence Follows the Path of Least Resistance
 - **Authors:** Tingan Jin, Shuhang Dong, Haosong Li
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Which context tokens drive a language model's answer? Graph-based tools such as attention rollout and attention flow answer this from a single forward pass, yet their faithfulness in decoder-only models is rarely tested against interventions. We view the layers of a decoder as a causal network whose conductances are attention weights, and read a token's influence on the answer from the resistance between them. We propose RIFT (Resistance-based Information Flow in Transformers), which scores a token by the series resistance of its path of least resistance to the answer, found exactly by a classic shortest-path dynamic program. On synthetic in-context retrieval tasks and extractive question answering over Wikipedia paragraphs, RIFT predicts the effect of replacing a token with a Spearman correlation of 0.52-0.68; predicting a single replacement, it reaches 77-96% of the agreement between two different random replacements. It needs no gradients and no extra forward passes, scoring every token of a prompt in 0.9-3.4 ms on one CPU thread, whereas the replacement experiment needs a forward pass per token. On 576 held-out prompts from four models of two families (Qwen3 and Llama 3.2), it outperforms token distance, attention rollout and attention flow in every pre-specified comparison, by 0.23 to 0.88 in mean Spearman correlation, and it is the best or tied-best of all attention-based scores we test. Aggregates that add up all routes (attention flow, effective resistance and rollout) instead mostly encode token position: as a simple proposition shows, early tokens have many more parallel routes to the answer.
### Title:
          LoopQuant: Accurate and Efficient Post-Training Quantization for Looped Transformers
 - **Authors:** Zhixiong Zhao, Zukang Xu, Xing Hu, Lifeng Liu, Guangyu Sun, Dawei Yang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Looped Language Models (LoopLMs) improve parameter efficiency by recursively reusing shared Transformer modules, yet their repeated computation over evolving recurrent states poses new challenges for low-bit deployment. Existing post-training quantization (PTQ) methods, largely designed for feed-forward Transformers, fail to account for cross-loop activation dynamics, heterogeneous loop sensitivity, and recursive error propagation, leading to substantial performance degradation at low precision. To address these challenges, we propose LoopQuant, a loop-aware PTQ framework for accurate and efficient low-bit quantization of LoopLMs. LoopQuant is built on three core components: 1) Cross-Loop Distribution Canonicalization (CLDC) learns a shared quantization-friendly representation across recurrent states while preserving parameter sharing; 2) Interaction-Aware Loop-wise Precision Allocation (ILPA) allocates activation precision under a fixed bit budget by modeling task-level loop sensitivity and cross-loop interactions; and 3) Loop-Conditioned Task Calibration (LCTC) further aligns quantized inference with the low-bit recursive trajectory through lightweight task-space calibration. Extensive experiments across LoopLM architectures demonstrate that LoopQuant consistently outperforms existing low-bit PTQ methods. On Ouro-2.6B, LoopQuant under W4A4 surpasses all W4A8 baselines in performance, while reducing model memory by 65.3% with only 3.4% additional storage overhead over naive INT4 and achieving a 1.7 times decoding speedup over BF16. These results demonstrate a strong balance among accuracy, compression, and inference efficiency, providing a practical and deployment-friendly solution for low-bit LoopLM inference. The code is available at [this https URL].
### Title:
          Tikhonov-Stabilized Bezier Representation Forecasting for Training-free Diffusion Acceleration
 - **Authors:** Lei Zhu, Mujie Lin, Ruochong Zheng, Guangyi Wang, Hao Li, Peng Jin, Chang Liu, Jie Chen
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Diffusion models, particularly Diffusion Transformers, achieve strong image and video generation quality but remain expensive at inference time due to repeated denoiser evaluations. Feature caching offers a deployment-friendly acceleration strategy by reusing or predicting intermediate representations without retraining the generator or modifying the sampler. However, existing cache-then-forecast methods often rely on Taylor-style finite-difference extrapolation, which can become unstable over longer cache intervals, and typically predict local module outputs whose errors may accumulate through subsequent denoiser blocks. We propose \textbf{BeziCast}, a training-free diffusion acceleration framework that forecasts output-proximal denoising representations using low-order B'ezier trajectories. BeziCast estimates B'ezier control points via Tikhonov-stabilized fitting, providing a smooth, capacity-controlled temporal parameterization that avoids explicit high-order derivative estimation and decouples trajectory capacity from cache-query density. To moderate aggressive extrapolation, we further derive equivalent forecasting weights and introduce a convex-hull-inspired guardrail that detects high-risk predictions and softly pulls them toward a conservative simplex-projected estimate. Extensive evaluations across advanced image and video diffusion models demonstrate the effectiveness of BeziCast. In particular, BeziCast accelerates FLUX.1 by up to (4.79\times) and HunyuanVideo by up to (4.11\times), while preserving substantially better generation quality than competing acceleration baselines.
### Title:
          Growing and Transferring Pathways for LLM Reinforcement Learning
 - **Authors:** Haolin Tian, Yikang Li, Tonghan Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Reinforcement learning (RL) can substantially improve the reasoning capabilities of large language models, but an effective training run requires considerable resources. Reusing the computation acquired through RL across models would extend the value of that training. We propose GrowRL, an RL method that organizes learned updates into compact computational pathways through structural growth. GrowRL freezes the pretrained backbone and adds an independent causal attention branch and a feed-forward network branch at each Transformer layer. Zero-initialized output projections preserve the pretrained policy at initialization, and RL updates only the added pathways. The learned pathways remain explicit after training, allowing transfer to structurally compatible target models and subsequent adaptation with the target backbone frozen. The architecture also supports sequential transfer of additional pathways. By keeping the learned attention and feed-forward computation separate from the pretrained weights, GrowRL provides an explicit structure for preserving and reusing the outcome of an RL run. Experiments across five backbones show competitive mathematical reasoning and out-of-distribution performance with a small number of trainable parameters. Learned pathways provide immediate gains across all three transfer directions, with performance after only 10 target-side updates already close to that after 100 updates in every case. These results support computational pathways as parameter-efficient and reusable units of reinforcement learning adaptation.
### Title:
          CT0: Cybersecurity Transformer
 - **Authors:** Víctor Mayoral-Vilches
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Cryptography and Security (cs.CR)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 General-purpose frontier models are large, expensive to run, and increasingly reluctant to engage with authorised defensive- and offensive-security work. We ask the opposite question: how small can a cybersecurity-specialist transformer be while remaining genuinely useful to a security practitioner? We introduce CT0, a Cybersecurity Transformer architecture, and CT0-Micro, its first incarnation: a compact (~4B-parameter) model built on the open base model, whose backbone is specialised by two-phase on-policy distillation on our Cybersecurity AI (CAI) dataset. CT0 serves two modes from one set of weights: System 1 answers typed security decisions (is this file suspicious, is this request an attack, did the model just refuse) in a single forward pass without generating a token, System 2 is the usual tool-using agent, and a confidence gate spends generation only where the fast path is unsure. Specialisation lifts the agentic path: CT0-Micro solves 21.1% of cybench-subtask against 7.6% for its base, above every open model of comparable footprint and the densest cybersecurity capability per parameter we measured. The fast path is what the architecture buys: CT0-Micro screens eighty files per second for indicators of compromise, catches its own refusals at 96.5% accuracy in 47 ms where the model judging itself reaches 76.5% in 254 ms and misses half of them, flags jailbreak requests at AUROC 0.966 in 8 ms, and, generating only for the facts it is unsure about, extracts vulnerability facts more accurately than either mode alone at a fifth of the generative cost. All of it is built in about 314 RTX PRO 6000 GPU-hours and served from one workstation GPU, with no policy layer between operator and target. Across eighteen benchmarks and use cases run with one checkpoint and head, we report where the design helps, where it does not, and what it still lacks.
### Title:
          HyBRA-EEG: Hyperbolic Global-Local Brain-Region Aggregation for Self-Supervised EEG Representation Learning
 - **Authors:** Xin Liu, Feng Lu, Wei Li
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Electroencephalography (EEG) representation learning is challenged by noisy signals, inter-subject variability, and heterogeneous electrode montages in different datasets and recording settings. Recent self-supervised EEG pretraining methods improve transferability by converting heterogeneous recordings into unified token or sequence representations, but channel-aware designs may still encode montage- or dataset-specific regularities. We propose HyBRA, a self-supervised framework based on hyperbolic global-local brain-region aggregation. HyBRA maps heterogeneous channel observations into a fixed regional token space by combining electrode coordinates, region priors, and feature-dependent hyperbolic distances. A channel-evidence injection branch preserves fine-grained discriminative cues during aggregation. The resulting tokens are processed by a Region-Time Transformer and pretrained with masked reconstruction of latent features and region-level signals. HyBRA outperforms the compared methods in average balanced accuracy on six benchmark datasets. Under a controlled six-fold evaluation on OpenBMI-MI, HyBRA matches CSBrain in balanced accuracy, improves AUROC by 1.84 percentage points and AUPRC by 5.23 percentage points, and uses 96.8\% fewer parameters. Matched comparisons on TUAB and TUEV show higher balanced accuracy for hyperbolic matching than for Euclidean distance or cosine similarity.
### Title:
          JtT: Rethinking Point Trajectory Diffusion for Diverse and Rigid Motion from an Image
 - **Authors:** Gabrijel Boduljak, Yushi Lan, Christian Rupprecht, Andrea Vedaldi
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 We study forecasting rigid-body motion from a single image: generating diverse and physically plausible future trajectories for arbitrary points in a scene, without building rigidity into the architecture or relying on geometric inputs such as depth. Like video generators, recent trajectory generators fail at this task, even in simple synthetic Kubric scenes, despite scaling up model size and training on real data. We show how to solve this with the right architecture and parameterization. Our model, JtT, is a simple diffusion transformer that operates directly in coordinate space and decodes arbitrary and dense query patterns, without the trajectory VAEs or fixed grids assumed in prior work. Through a systematic study of the design space of point trajectory diffusion, we find that diffusing per-frame velocities is the only parameterization that captures rigidity, that it must be paired with the right timestep schedule, and that adding full-resolution RGB at the query points resolves the boundary failures of prior methods. However, evaluating such 2D models rigorously is itself non-trivial. To our knowledge, we are the first to observe that physical plausibility and diversity do not improve together during training: larger models, as used in prior work, memorize the single ground-truth future per training image, becoming more rigid while covering fewer possible futures. Yet prior work has paid little attention to this, so we show how model rankings depend on model size, metric, and stopping time, and adopt a stricter, checkpoint-averaged evaluation protocol. Under this protocol, JtT improves over prior methods on both distributional and rigidity metrics and approaches ground-truth rigidity, with only about 50M parameters.
### Title:
          Towards Modality-Agnostic Seizure Classification: A Unified Deep Learning Framework for Heterogeneous EEG and Intracranial EEG Signals
 - **Authors:** Ramya Sundaram, Muhammad Aslam Jarwar, M Uma, Sunder Ali Khowaja, Qurat-Ul-Ain Mastoi
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Seizure prediction and detection, essential for clinical treatment of epilepsy, remain the crucial task of this neurological disease. EEG and iEEG are widely used to analyze epilepsy due to its capability of capturing discriminative brain activities at high time resolution. However, existing works generally analyze either EEG or iEEG seizure activity separately, where EEG analysis has been well-researched, while more and more works consider iEEG for its higher specificity at the spatial level and signal quality. Currently, most of the existing seizure prediction systems are single-modal in nature, which limits its application to a variety of clinical conditions. Therefore, a common framework to analyze seizures with both EEG and iEEG signals needs to be developed. This paper presents a new framework of multimodal seizure classification integrating the heterogeneous EEG and iEEG recordings from different subjects and sources. A specific framework, called Unified Multimodal Signal Harmonization (UMSH), is developed for harmonizing signals with varied sampling rates and recording conditions. Moreover, to handle the extremely class-imbalanced problem in the data sets, we use a Conditional Generative Adversarial Network (cGAN) based data augmentation technique to generate synthetic samples to balance the class distribution. The proposed framework uses the EEGNet-Vision Transformer (EEGNet-ViT) as the feature extraction and classification network to learn the local discriminative patterns as well as global contextual information in the multimodal seizure data. Experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework for robust seizure classification, emphasizing the potential of integrated EEG-iEEG multimodal learning in advancing next-generation epilepsy monitoring and clinical decision-support systems.
### Title:
          Learning Incompressible 3D Flows from Sparse Samples: A Set Transformer Approach to Divergence-Free Kernel Fitting
 - **Authors:** Ramzi Idoughi, Radouan Boukharfane, Peter Wonka
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computational Engineering, Finance, and Science (cs.CE)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Recovering divergence-free velocity fields from sparse and corrupted observations requires reconciling physical consistency, expressive representations, and robustness to missing data. We introduce DFK-Former, a set-to-field transformer that reconstructs divergence-free velocity fields from sparse, noisy, and irregularly sampled 3D observations. The model is inherently invariant to the number and ordering of input samples and predicts the parameters of a divergence-free kernel representation, producing continuous, mesh-free velocity fields that satisfy incompressibility exactly by construction rather than approximately through soft constraints or projection operators. Unlike existing divergence-free kernel approaches, which estimate kernel coefficients via a separate optimization for each field, DFK-Former predicts these coefficients directly in a single forward pass. This eliminates costly iterative fitting while providing a learned prior that enables accurate reconstruction even when observations are insufficient to uniquely determine the field. To enhance representational power, we introduce adaptive and anisotropic divergence-free kernels that preserve exact incompressibility guarantees while capturing more complex flow structures. Extensive experiments across varying levels of sparsity, noise, and occlusion, including transfer to real Direct Numerical Simulation (DNS) turbulence data, demonstrate substantial gains in both accuracy and efficiency. DFK-Former reduces endpoint error by up to 4.6x compared to per-field divergence-free-kernel optimization, while reconstructing each field in 7.5 ms instead of 9.8 s, yielding a speedup of more than three orders of magnitude. These results establish direct, feed-forward prediction of divergence-free kernel parameters as a practical and effective paradigm for physically consistent velocity-field reconstruction from sparse observations.
### Title:
          Counting with Attention and LayerNorm: Norm Scaling and Representation Geometry
 - **Authors:** Ayyub Shaffy, Manoj Dhakal, Safal Shrestha, Keith W. Ross
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Counting is a simple task on which transformers can nevertheless fail at long sequence lengths. We study the cost of counting in a single attention layer, up to a maximum length $L$, focusing on how softmax attention and layer normalization shape the representation from which the count is recovered. We mainly focus on regression read-out but also consider classification read-out. For regression, we show unnormalized linear attention counts exactly at parameter norms independent of $L$. For softmax attention, regression cannot count exactly with finite parameters; it recovers the count after rounding, but only when the vector parameter norms grow with $L$. We measure this cost by $B_{\min}$, the smallest bound that can be placed on every trainable vector to ensure exact counting after rounding, and derive tight rates. At fixed model dimension, $B_{\min}$ grows as $L$ without normalization, as $L^{3/2}$ under bare post-normalization, and as $L^{3/4}$ with learned post-normalization. The last rate is unexpected: post-normalization discards the magnitude that carries the count and might be expected to make counting harder, yet with a learnable gain it counts at a smaller norm than softmax without normalization. Bare pre-normalization is different: it bounds the attention scores, so beyond a finite length no parameter norm suffices. A classification read-out keeps every norm bounded under every placement, but a dense head stores one output vector per count and costs more in storage. Experiments on the post-normalized model reproduce this split between the two read-outs. Together, our theoretical and empirical findings characterize how self-attention, layer-normalization placement, and read-out choice shape the representation geometry and parameter cost of counting in the minimal single-head architecture studied here.
### Title:
          Image Tokenizers Should Be Trained Between Images
 - **Authors:** Ole Beißwenger, Jan-Niklas Dihlmann, Hendrik Lensch
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Latent diffusion models generate images as codes of an image tokenizer, whose decoder turns them into the pixel image. Recent tokenizers improve how well these codes reconstruct an image and how easily a generator learns them. Yet a generator does not reproduce the codes of training images, and when a generated code lands outside what the decoder expects, the decoded image contains imperfections. Current methods make the decoder more robust to generated codes by adding noise during training. However, generated codes lie between the training codes and resemble their mixture more than one of them plus noise. To improve generated images, decoders should therefore be trained on codes in the space between training samples. We propose InterpTok, which fine-tunes MAETok to decode mixtures of image codes into natural images. Since no target image exists between two training samples, a discriminator judges whether each decoded mixture looks as natural as its two sources. A mixing ratio loss requires the decoded mixture to keep the mixing ratio of the two codes. Together, they prevent collapse to a trivial solution and yield a sharp and natural composition of both images. InterpTok keeps MAETok's reconstruction quality and improves interpolation quality more than threefold, the best of all tokenizers in a recent benchmark. Beyond interpolation, InterpTok improves the generation FID of a matched diffusion transformer from 11.61 to 8.48 without guidance, and even reaches 8.11 at an eighth of the sampling steps, where the generator on MAETok's codes worsens. The generator trained on our tokenizer also reaches the final result of the one trained on MAETok at half the training steps. Our findings show that the space between image codes can and should be explicitly supervised rather than ignored.
### Title:
          One-Layer Transformers Provably Learn In-Context K-Nearest Neighbor Prediction with Chain-of-Thought
 - **Authors:** Lyumin Wu, Yuan Cao
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Machine Learning (stat.ML)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Chain-of-Thought (CoT) enables transformers to solve complex reasoning tasks by generating intermediate reasoning steps. Despite strong empirical success, the mechanisms by which such reasoning abilities emerge during training remain poorly understood, particularly from a theoretical perspective. In this work, we study this question in a stylized yet fully analyzable setting: in-context $\mathrm{K}$-nearest-neighbor ($\mathrm{K}$-NN) prediction with a one-layer transformer. Earlier work has demonstrated that one-layer transformers can be trained to perform $1$-NN without CoT (Li et al., 2024a) by having the softmax attention attend to the nearest neighbor in context. However, this result on $1$-NN cannot be extended to $\mathrm{K}$-NN for $K>1$, as softmax attention is much less naturally suited to attending to the 2nd through $K$-th nearest neighbors. We first give a rigorous negative result showing that this limitation already appears on a well-separated class of binary classification tasks for odd $K>1$. We then give an explicit construction of a one-layer transformer, and show that it can solve $\mathrm{K}$-NN via CoT reasoning. We further prove that, under a stylized training setup, gradient descent can recover this construction, thereby showing that transformers can acquire the capability to solve $\mathrm{K}$-NN through training. In addition, we show that the resulting trained model applies to a broader test class than that assumed in the training analysis. Our results shed light on how CoT expands the ability of transformers to learn and solve tasks that are otherwise hard to solve.
### Title:
          Growing to See: Efficient Pretraining of Self-Supervised Vision Transformers
 - **Authors:** Amir Mohammad Karimi Mamaghan, Ferdinand Kapl, Andrea Dittadi, Karl Henrik Johansson, Kaitlin Maile, Johannes von Oswald, Stefan Bauer
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Progressive depth growth builds large networks by starting with shallow models and inserting layers during training. Recent advances in large language models have renewed interest in this longstanding approach, demonstrating benefits for training efficiency and reasoning alongside changes in internal computations. However, how growing affects representation quality and computational patterns in self-supervised vision transformers remains unclear. In this work, we systematically investigate progressive depth growth in two popular self-supervised families, namely masked autoencoders (MAE) and DINOv3, and analyze how growing affects downstream performance, intermediate representations, and internal computations. Across global and dense tasks, we show that growing improves downstream performance while reducing training FLOPs by 13.9--24.8%, with ImageNet linear-probe accuracy gains of up to 3.36 percentage points. Growing also improves downstream performance at intermediate layers and reshapes internal computational patterns, introducing recurring alternations between global and localized attention in MAE and earlier local-to-global attention progression in self-distilled DINOv3. Together, these findings establish progressive depth growth as an effective and efficient pretraining strategy for improving downstream performance across global and dense visual tasks with less computation, and advance our understanding of how growing shapes visual computation.
### Title:
          DTA-GT: Direction- and Topology-Aware Graph Transformer for Neural Network Representation Learning
 - **Authors:** Yuxiang Zeng, Kun Xie, Yang Wang, Jigang Wen, Xiaocan Li, Guangxing Zhang, Gaogang Xie, Jiannong Cao
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Neural and Evolutionary Computing (cs.NE)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Architecture attribute predictors reduce the cost of neural architecture search by estimating accuracy or latency from a small set of evaluated candidates. The key difficulty is that neural architectures are operation-labeled DAGs: edge direction defines computational flow, but existing predictors often treat direction as a local structural cue. We propose DTA-GT, a Direction- and Topology-Aware Graph Transformer based on encoder-wide directional consistency, which preserves directed computational semantics across node initialization, global spectral encoding, pairwise interaction, and feature update. DTA-GT realizes this principle with direction- and topology-aware node initialization, Magnetic Laplacian spectral encoding, direction-dual structural attention, and a Direction-Sensitive MoE-FFN. Across accuracy and latency prediction on NAS-Bench-101 and NAS-Bench-201, DTA-GT consistently outperforms representative sequence-, GNN-, Transformer-, and hybrid predictors under limited-budget settings. Ablations and Magnetic spectral controls show that the improvements come from coordinated direction-aware stages and edge-orientation-sensitive spectral information, while mechanistic diagnostics confirm that the modules exhibit the intended directional behavior. These results support encoder-wide directional consistency as an effective design principle for architecture DAG representation.
### Title:
          From Parallel to Sequential: Small-Batch Noise Restores Simplicity Bias of Muon
 - **Authors:** Tom Jacobs, Rebekka Burkholz
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Simplicity bias, which can arise from sequential feature learning during training, has been proposed as a general explanation for the success of deep learning. An important driver of this phenomenon is saddle-to-saddle behavior, in which the gradient flow trajectory remains close to a sequence of saddle manifolds while learning one feature at a time. However, Muon, a state-of-the-art optimizer based on spectral descent, does not exhibit such dynamics in the full-batch setting and instead learns features in parallel. How is it then that Muon is so successful in deep learning settings? We show that stochastic gradient noise fundamentally changes this behavior: small-batch noise restores sequential feature learning, providing a mechanism for simplicity bias that is absent from full-batch Muon. We explain this transition through stochastic smoothing of the spectral descent operator underlying Muon. When gradients are small relative to the noise scale, the expected spectral update becomes approximately proportional to the actual gradient, which restores gradient-flow-like dynamics. We formalize this mechanism for deep linear models by deriving the effective continuous dynamics of noisy spectral descent. Experiments on low-rank matrix reconstruction, vision transformers, and large language models (LLMs) support these findings.
### Title:
          Time Window Optimization for Motor Imagery Classification via Temporal Structure Embedded Reinforcement Learning
 - **Authors:** Huan Luo, Na Lu, Xiaopeng Wang, Ziyang Shang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Neural and Evolutionary Computing (cs.NE); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Online motor imagery-based brain-computer interfaces (MI-BCIs) typically decode continuous EEG using fixed-length sliding windows that may be misaligned with neural events. Existing window optimization methods often evaluate windows independently, overlooking temporal dependencies and potentially producing fragmented or physiologically implausible selections. We propose Temporal Structure Embedded Reinforcement Learning (TSE-RL), a framework that incorporates prior knowledge of MI event structure into a constrained Markov decision process for adaptive window optimization. First, TSE-RL assigns each window an explicit semantic role through Discard, Onset, Sustain, or Offset actions, extending binary retain/discard decisions to event-level alignment. Second, a finite-state machine masks invalid actions to enforce the temporal order of MI phases and promote physiologically plausible window sequences. Third, a Transformer-based history encoder captures dependencies across windows, improving training stability and computational efficiency compared with recurrent architectures. TSE-RL is plug-and-play and can be integrated with diverse MI classifiers. Experiments on multiple benchmark datasets demonstrate consistent improvements in decoding performance in both cross-session and cross-subject settings. Visualization analyses further suggest that the learned window selection policy aligns with neurophysiological principles.
### Title:
          DiTSG: Context-Aware Air Traffic Scene Generation with Multi-Agent Diffusion Transformers
 - **Authors:** Seokbin Yoon, Kyungmin Kim, Taejung Kwon, Keumjin Lee, Peng Wei
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Multiagent Systems (cs.MA); Machine Learning (cs.LG); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Understanding how many aircraft, and which traffic configurations, can be safely and efficiently managed by air traffic controllers within a given airspace is fundamental to air traffic management. Air traffic controllers continuously modify aircraft trajectories through interventions such as speed advisories and radar vectoring, and the patterns of these interventions are difficult to capture effectively using existing air traffic simulators. To address this limitation, we propose Diffusion Transformers for Traffic Scene Generation (DiTSG), which learns to generate air traffic scenes from recorded multi-aircraft trajectories. DiTSG employs stacked multi-agent inverted Transformer layers as the denoising backbone of a diffusion transformer, jointly generates the trajectories of multiple aircraft within a traffic scene, and conditions the generation on agent-specific required times of arrival. The resulting trajectories generated by DiTSG preserve realistic inter-aircraft interactions observed in actual air traffic operations. Experiments show that the DiTSG generates diverse traffic scenes that reproduce the statistical properties of recorded traffic while preserving realistic inter-aircraft interactions.
### Title:
          Beyond Model Bias: Cross-Entropy Based Rankings in Literary and Academic Texts Remain Stable across Small Language Models
 - **Authors:** Ankit Bhattacharjee, Indrasabarna Hari, Krityapriya Bhaumik, Arka Das, Piyush Sarangi
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 While language models provide powerful mechanisms for evaluating textual predictability, it remains unclear whether the relative extents of LM surprisal remain stable across models. This study presents empirical evidence of the invariance of ranking based on cross-entropy related measures (e.g. mean, variance of each data-point), across four distinct small scale transformer-based models, for a given dataset of text sources. We consider two stylistic environments: a corpus of contemporary poetry and a corpus of academic research papers. We demonstrate that mean and variance of cross-entropy maintain robust cross-model rank correlation ($\rho > 0.90$) across both regimes. To test whether this agreement merely reflects generic differences in textual complexity, we compare it against six parameter-free compression baselines. The extent of agreement among language models is found to be substantially higher than that among model-baselines, for most of the measures. Geometric analysis of the structurally constrained academic regime reveals that higher-order moments are sensitive to specific models, although strong agreement persists. The findings suggest that relative extents of textual unpredictability may be independent of the language model they are computed against.
### Title:
          Towards Universal Calibration for In-Context Classification
 - **Authors:** Awik Dhar, Sunita Sarawagi, Arpit Agarwal
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 In-Context Learning (ICL) has received a lot of interest recently due to its ability to improve classification accuracy for new tasks without having to fine-tune the model over these tasks. However, the impact of ICL on calibration has been far less studied. Recent work has shown that ICL can have a negative impact on calibration due to biased selection of in-context examples. The existing methods for improving ICL calibration require additional task and model-specific training, detracting from the no-fine-tuning convenience of ICL. In this work, we introduce CALIBFORMER, a universal ICL calibrator for classification tasks, that generalizes to new models and tasks without further fine-tuning on these tasks. Specifically, we design a transformer-based calibrator which uses the model predictions on in-context examples to predict a confidence score for the test input. We show that the calibrator trained on three models and ten tasks, is able to generalize/calibrate ICL predictions on several new tasks and four LLMs unseen during training. Even for frontier models like Gemini, CALIBFORMER is able to obtain over 50% reduction in calibration error.
### Title:
          MARS: Systematic Multi-Level Acceleration for World Action Models
 - **Authors:** Zengyuan Zuo, Yongchang Zhang, Guangyu Zhao, Tianze Zhang, Biao Wu, Meng Fang, Yiwu Zhong
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 World Action Models (WAMs) have emerged as a promising alternative for embodied intelligence by jointly predicting future world states and robot actions. However, their heavy visual encoding and iterative generative inference incur substantial latency, limiting their applicability to high-frequency closed-loop control. Existing training-free acceleration methods typically optimize a single stage, yielding limited speedups or degraded control under aggressive reuse. In this work, we systematically profile the inference overhead of WAMs and observe abundant redundancy across Transformer computation, diffusion denoising, and consecutive policy queries. However, the safe removal of such redundancy depends strongly on its impact on action prediction and control sensitivity. Based on these observations, we introduce \textbf{MARS}, a training-free and plug-and-play framework for multi-level WAM acceleration.
