MARS selectively removes low-impact attention computation, adaptively accelerates diffusion inference according to motion granularity, and speculatively reuses predicted future visual representations across consecutive queries with online verification. Together, these complementary mechanisms provide remarkable acceleration while limiting approximation error. Extensive experiments across LIBERO, LIBERO-PRO, and RoboCasa show that MARS achieves up to $4.00\times$, $3.94\times$, and $2.10\times$ end-to-end speedups, respectively, while maintaining task success rate close to the original policy. Moreover, MARS generalizes across different model backbones and across simulation and real-robot experiments, substantially improving the efficiency--performance trade-off of WAM inference. Our code is available at this https URL.
### Title:
          FunDM: Cheap & Label-Free Decision Models from Frozen Language Models
 - **Authors:** Ujjawal K. Panchal
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Decision models such as Jev score candidate answers or actions. A small decision head trained label-free can recover about 99% of a frozen language model's multiple-choice accuracy while using only two-thirds of its transformer layers. It learns the full model's answer probabilities with KL divergence, without correct-answer labels for training. One backbone pass reads the question and all options together; the head returns probabilities for every option. We call this model FunDM (Fun Decision Model). Using 24 of Qwen3-8B's 36 layers and skipping the LM head, its +1T variant reaches 75.0% mean accuracy over eight benchmarks, versus 75.7% for the full model. On MMLU-Pro, both heads achieve 1.56 to 1.57 times batched vLLM throughput with about one-third lower peak GPU allocator memory. Both heads score higher than released Contrastive Language Models (CLM), fine-tuned CLM, and CLM-shaped heads trained from scratch on the eight multiple-choice benchmarks and coding-agent selection. Separately trained agent heads learn judge probabilities without pass/fail labels and select passing runs for 74.4 to 74.6 of 100 held-out tasks on average, versus 74 for the full-model judge and 52.0 for a CLM-shaped action-trained head. Code is available at: this https URL
### Title:
          AReA: Adaptive Receptive-Field Attention for Efficient High-Resolution Diffusion Transformers
 - **Authors:** Javad Rajabi, Koorosh Roohi, David B. Lindell, Babak Taati
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Generating images beyond the training resolution of Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) remains challenging, as the number of tokens grows rapidly with resolution, full attention becomes expensive, and outputs often suffer from structural distortions and degraded detail. We identify uniform full attention as the common source of both problems. Attending to all tokens at every step causes quadratic cost and exposes the model to more tokens and relative distances beyond the training range, pushing the model out of distribution. We observe that attention's effective receptive field narrows during denoising, from global in early steps that form structure to local in later steps that refine details. We propose AReA, a training-free attention mechanism whose receptive field evolves from coarse to fine, following the denoising progression. In early steps, AReA applies cross-scale coarse attention over downsampled keys and values, capturing global context, while in later steps it applies local attention within progressively shrinking windows. Without additional parameters or training, experiments show that AReA consistently outperforms state-of-the-art training-free baselines across target resolutions and different models, while also being faster and more efficient.
### Title:
          Budgeted KV-Cache Repair for Evolving Contexts
 - **Authors:** Han Yu, Wenhui Zhu, Jincheng Cao, Rui Cai, Yuanda Xu, Zhengze Zhou, Xiwen Chen, Hao Wang, Xuanzhao Dong, Han Shi, Menglin Zhou, Shayan Mohajer Hamidi, Lin Su, Mina Razghandi, Sayan Ghosh, Qing Lan, Rohit Jain, Alborz Geramifard
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Language-model agents revise their working context as information becomes obsolete, but deleting text does not erase its influence on cached computation. Later key-value (KV) states remain conditioned on the deleted content, while recomputing the entire retained suffix can be costly. We introduce budgeted KV-cache repair: recovering task performance by recomputing a user-chosen fraction of the retained suffix. Comparing repair allocations under identical relevance scores and token budgets, we find that refreshing coherent regions can substantially outperform refreshing isolated tokens. This finding motivates Semantic-Unit Refresh (SUR), which allocates the budget to query-relevant text regions using inexpensive embedding scores, without training or an auxiliary transformer forward. To execute these repairs efficiently, a layer-packed executor processes selected states together at each layer while preserving full causal cache access. Across five models, SUR achieves strong recovery in controlled deletion tasks while recomputing only 2-5% of the suffix. On four natural multi-hop QA datasets, it achieves near-full-recompute exact match at a 5% budget with lower post-edit latency. The executor accelerates fragmented refresh by up to 16.1x over interval-wise execution.
### Title:
          On the Statistical Mechanics of Attention
 - **Authors:** Jerry Yao-Chieh Hu, Yu-An Chen, Maojiang Su, Han Liu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Disordered Systems and Neural Networks (cond-mat.dis-nn); Statistical Mechanics (cond-mat.stat-mech); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Machine Learning (stat.ML)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 We revisit the statistical mechanics of Transformer attention via modern Hopfield models. We present a free-energy formalism to characterize parameter regimes and critical thresholds for robust attention inference. The resulting free-energy landscape incorporates randomness in query states and attention weights. Attention then emerges as the equation of motion of the corresponding statistical-mechanical system (i.e., modern Hopfield model) through the standard free-energy minimization principle. This allows us to identify first-order phase transitions in attention between glassy and robust-inference phases. We further extend to attentions with stochastic queries and keys. Together, our results provide a rigorous statistical-mechanics framework for characterizing phase structure and inference reliability across a family of Transformer attention mechanisms.
### Title:
          HairFlow: Modeling 3D Hair Strands with Multi-Modal Conditioned Diffusion
 - **Authors:** Yating Wang, Kui Wu, Ran Yi, Zixuan Lu, Tongtong Wang, Zhongtian Zheng, Lizhuang Ma, Yuefan Shen
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Modeling 3D hair strands is challenging due to complex hairstyle geometry, scarce high-quality training data, and incomplete or noisy input observations. Foundation models offer broader hairstyle priors, but their outputs are not directly usable for strand-based rendering and simulation. Existing approaches require costly per-instance optimization to fit hair priors to meshes from foundation models and 2D orientations from specialized predictors trained on limited hair data, restricting efficiency and generalization. We present HairFlow, a multimodal conditioned diffusion framework for efficient and controllable hair strands modeling. HairFlow conditions strand generation on two complementary representations: a coarse mesh that constrains global shape and multi-view sketches that specify local strand flow. HairFlow combines an Orientation VAE with a conditioned diffusion transformer to generate volumetric fields that are traced into explicit strands. To overcome the lack of paired sketch-strand training data, we introduce an automatic synthesis pipeline that generates geometrically aligned multi-view sketches with diverse styles representative of inference-time inputs. Experiments show that HairFlow generates plausible 3D strands that faithfully follow the input conditions across diverse hairstyles, substantially reduces inference time, and supports flexible multimodal control.
### Title:
          Measure Neural Mappings
 - **Authors:** Ricardo Baptista, Edoardo Calvello, Bohan Chen, Andrew Stuart, Urbain Vaes
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Machine Learning (stat.ML)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 We introduce and define measure neural mappings (MNMs), which combine measure-dependent neural transport with ensemble consistency for learning operators on probability measures. Ensemble consistency requires outputs from growing ensembles sampled from an input law to converge to the specified population output in probability. We establish quantitative continuum limits for attention on noncompact laws under strong sub-Gaussian and tail-integrability assumptions. These laws accommodate unbounded state spaces in scientific machine learning, including Bayesian inference and uncertainty quantification. For fixed self-attention transformers, the expected output Wasserstein error is bounded by the empirical input Wasserstein error plus an additional $N^{-1/2}$ attention term. These self-attention transformers are MNMs under the stated assumptions: pre-norm architectures follow from compact stability after normalization, while post-norm architectures are covered under the stated tail conditions. We prove corresponding cross-attention estimates and extend the results to multihead attention and grouped transformer evaluation, with convergence as the group size grows. For Set Transformers viewed as measure-to-vector mappings, we obtain an $N^{-1/2}$ bound on the expected Euclidean output error under the stated assumptions. Experiments on seven operators in dimensions $d=1,2,3,5,10$ support the predicted particle-count dependence, using one-dimensional Wasserstein computations and Sinkhorn approximations in higher dimensions. The observed Wasserstein and sliced Wasserstein decay rates are close to their respective theoretical sampling benchmarks.
### Title:
          Cross-Market Meta-Learning for Electricity Price Forecasting
 - **Authors:** Delaram Pirhayatifard, Terrence Alsup
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Machine Learning (cs.LG); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Day-ahead (DA) electricity price forecasting is usually posed per market, with a separate model fitted to each grid's own history. This caps accuracy: statistical and tree-based baselines saturate, while higher-capacity sequence models need more training data than a single market's few years of hourly prices can supply. Such methods also treat each node as an independent series, discarding the spatial dependencies induced by network topology and congestion. Because price formation across independent system operators (ISOs) is driven by shared physics and largely common market design, we instead treat cross-market history as a pretraining corpus. We present MetaGrid, a topology-aware transformer, meta-trained across markets so that a single zone-count-independent parameter set adapts to an unseen grid. On a leak-free benchmark of five U.S. ISOs, where every model receives the same prices, DA load forecasts and calendar features, MetaGrid attains the lowest average MAE, improving on the best baseline by 3.1\% and on the same architecture trained from scratch by 7.7\%.
### Title:
          Bottlenecked semantic compression by representation reconstruction
 - **Authors:** Xiaoya Tang, Shilong Liu, Daniel Smolyak, Xiwen Li, Quynh Nguyen, Tolga Tasdizen
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Vision Transformers incur high computational costs when processing long visual token sequences. Existing reduction methods typically rank tokens independently and discard those deemed unimportant or redundant. We propose a reconstruction-guided framework for visual token condensation (ReConV), which summarizes the complete visual sequence with a compact Summary Bank (SMB) and retains tokens whose information is not adequately captured by the SMB, as indicated by their reconstruction residuals. At each compression layer, a lightweight position-conditioned decoder reconstructs the current tokens, leaving shared information compressed in the SMB while explicitly preserving poorly reconstructed details. Experiments on image classification and multimodal understanding demonstrate favorable performance-efficiency trade-offs: ReConV surpasses the uncompressed baseline with 48\% fewer GMACs on ImageNet and enables reusable image-level representations for efficient multi-turn inference. Code is available at TokenDrop.
### Title:
          Act4R: Action-conditioned 4D reconstruction for kinematics-free control
 - **Authors:** Zhaoliang Zhang, Chenhao Yu, Zhimin Shao, Xijun Liu, Cheng Peng
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Precise control of movement across various robotic embodiments is a longstanding challenge. Modeling complex robotic structures requires significant time, dedicated sensors, and manual labeling. Recent advancements in 4D representation learning offer an interesting alternative: can we leverage 4D features lifted from 2D observations to densely map a robot's spatial movement based on the action signal? To this end, we propose Act4R, a framework that builds on 3D foundation model to predict a robot's 3D scene flow with any given action. Act4R introduces a Spatial-Action Encoder, which encodes the current 2D robot state and its action signals into embeddings that represent future robot state in 3D. Then, both the current and future spatial embeddings are decoded into point maps to estimate action-conditioned scene flow. To effectively learn the mapping between action and future state, Act4R introduces spatial-action fusion in its transformer-based encoder, and a spatial alignment loss that guides the encoder for future flow prediction. After training on some samples of a given embodiment, Act4R can perform inverse control by optimizing the signal to achieve the desired scene flow. We evaluate Act4R on a public real-world dataset and synthetic data collected in simulator. Experiments on various robotic platforms show that Act4R outperforms the previous SoTA in scene flow prediction. Our method also performs better in inverse control, both on the action accuracy metric and in closed-loop control in simulation.
### Title:
          Everyone Was Right: Lexical Representations in Language Models Are Both Abstract and Holistic
 - **Authors:** Zachary Nicholas Houghton, Yu Zhou, Dan Pluth, Vijay K. Gurbani, Vsevolod Kapatsinski
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computation and Language (cs.CL)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Lexical representations can encode both abstract information and information specific to particular contexts. We investigate how these forms of information are organized in language models by decomposing contextual word representations into item, class, and word-class interaction components. Across six pretrained transformers and two linguistic categories, we find that the interaction is consistently present, grows across layers, and contains information associated with both item and class. Unlike the nearly orthogonal item and class effects, the interaction overlaps with both and encodes class information beyond the shared class effect. An interaction also emerges in artificial-language learners even when the input contains only additive structure. Together, these findings show that lexical representations combine abstract information with structure specific to particular word-class combinations.
### Title:
          Benchmarking Self-Supervised 3D Medical Vision Foundation Models and Transferability Estimation
 - **Authors:** Jiaqi Tang, Yu Wang, Yang Liu, Jing Tao, Fandong Zhang, Zhou Zhen, Xiahai Zhuang, Qingchao Chen, Yining Yang, Yizhou Yu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Self-supervised pretraining has expanded the pool of 3D medical foundation models, but published evaluations use different datasets, endpoints and fine-tuning protocols. Models also differ in architecture, pretraining objective and data, while downstream targets range from voxel-level masks to patient-level diagnoses and outcomes. As a result, published findings provide little directly comparable evidence for choosing a model for a new task. Direct comparison of adaptation performance requires fine-tuning candidates on labelled target data, at substantial computational cost. We benchmark 23 candidates across the same 14 segmentation, classification and regression tasks spanning multiple anatomies and modalities. No candidate ranked among the top three across all segmentation tasks or across all classification and regression tasks; a convolutional encoder could outperform a transformer trained with the same objective on one task but underperform it on another. We benchmark transferability estimators against the fine-tuned rankings and find that their agreement varies across dense segmentation, classification and regression. InsightRank uses task-appropriate frozen-feature statistics and architecture-aware pairwise comparisons to rank candidates before fine-tuning. Across the 23-model pool, mean Kendall tau-b was 0.431 for segmentation and 0.352 for classification and regression. The segmentation Top 3 contained a task winner on four of nine tasks; the image-level Top 3 contained none on the five tasks, despite better full-ranking agreement than the matched conventional estimators. A three-model shortlist reduced recorded extraction-plus-training job time by 85.8% across seven segmentation tasks. Together, the benchmark provides a common reference for model selection and InsightRank offers a lower-cost way to prioritize fine-tuning, with shortlist performance that differs by target type.
### Title:
          High-Throughput LLM-Based Compression and What It Takes: A Systems Study of Inference Engines and Entropy Coding Optimizations
 - **Authors:** David Adams, Luisa Neubauer, Ping-Lin Kuo, Mihail Stoian, Thomas Rückstieß, Josif Grabocka, Renata Borovica-Gajic, Andreas Kipf
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Databases (cs.DB)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Large language models are attractive text compressors because next-token probabilities can be converted into code lengths. In practice, however, the main bottleneck is often not modeling quality but systems cost: a compressor must obtain logits quickly enough to compete with strong classical baselines. This paper examines systems optimizations for LLM-based compression, including multistream pipelined arithmetic coding and target-interval capture, and evaluates their trade-offs across inference engines, configurations and datasets. We evaluate LLM-based text compression on three different inference engines: Transformers, vLLM, and TensorRT-LLM. Notably, we obtain a speedup of 18.84x compared to prior work's Transformers implementation and a maximum recorded inference speed of 181 kilobytes per second at a compression ratio of 7.17, when running on an NVIDIA A100.
### Title:
          OpenDepth: Repurposing Image Editing Priors for Robust Open-World Monocular Depth Estimation
 - **Authors:** Mengke Zhang, Tianyi Yang, Xinzhuo Li, Anlong Ming
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Recently, the integration of generative priors has revolutionized Monocular Depth Estimation (MDE), achieving state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance with remarkably high data efficiency. However, although methods based on Text-to-Image (T2I) models excel in general scenes, their efficacy degrades sharply when confronted with open-world environmental perturbations, such as illumination variations and adverse weather. Since T2I priors are optimized for semantic synthesis rather than preserving pixel-level structure, they struggle to decouple geometry from appearance under severe visual shifts, easily triggering structural hallucinations. Therefore, our core insight is to model open-world depth estimation as consistent geometric inference across multi-style images, fully leveraging the powerful structure-preserving editing priors of Image-to-Image (I2I) models. Based on this, we propose OpenDepth, a robust open-world MDE framework that introduces "Intra-Domain Awareness" -- the capability to map diverse stylistic inputs sharing identical geometry into a unified latent space. To achieve this, we first align shallow features within the Diffusion Transformer (DiT) to anchor degraded observations to unified geometric representations. Additionally, we introduce a joint RGB latent reconstruction task, requiring the model to recover clean RGB information from degraded inputs. This multi-stage strategy effectively decouples environmental style interference, compelling the model to focus strictly on underlying geometric recovery. Extensive evaluations show that OpenDepth sets new SOTA records for robust zero-shot depth estimation across standard and extreme weather benchmarks, demonstrating exceptional structural integrity and adaptability.
### Title:
          Smartphone-Based, Field-Deployed AI Inspection System for Power Distribution Infrastructure
 - **Authors:** Sampath Balaji Masabathula (1 and 2), Srija Vanapalli (1 and 2), Gangadhar Kanchi (1), Sreenivasa Rao V.A.N (1), Vijaya Lalitha V (1), Sasank Chilamkurthy (3) ((1) Eastern Power Distribution Company of Andhra Pradesh Limited (APEPDCL), Visakhapatnam, India, (2) Andhra University College of Engineering, Visakhapatnam, India, (3) Von Neumann AI (JOHNAIC), Bengaluru, India)
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Routine inspection of power distribution infrastructure remains challenging for utilities operating geographically dispersed networks, where conventional manual inspection can be difficult to perform consistently and at scale. This work presents a smartphone-based AI inspection system for assessing targeted distribution-infrastructure conditions from images captured by field personnel. The proposed workflow integrates image-quality and viewing-perspective validation with AI-assisted assessment of pole lean, crossarm and top-cleat tilt, voltage level, vegetation-clearance risk, and distribution-transformer (DTR)-ground clearance. The system integrates deep-learning models with monocular depth estimation, geometric analysis, and rule-based processing within a common multi-stage inspection workflow designed for practical field operation. The system was pilot deployed across five selected distribution feeders within the Eastern Power Distribution Company of Andhra Pradesh Ltd. (APEPDCL), where 1,222 defect instances were identified across the assessed categories and subsequently tracked through a rectification and verification workflow, with 84 defect instances recorded as rectified during the study period. Feeder-level interruption records were also examined over the study period; however, the available observations do not provide sufficient evidence to establish a reliability improvement attributable to the proposed system. A curated multi-module field-captured dataset, PD-Defect, is also publicly released to support reproducible research in distribution-infrastructure inspection. The field deployment demonstrates the practical integration of smartphone-based AI-assisted inspection into field inspection and maintenance activities, providing a mechanism for systematic identification, documentation, rectification tracking, and verification of targeted distribution-infrastructure defects.
### Title:
          PrT-Flow: Probability-Tractable Flow Matching for Reinforcement Learning
 - **Authors:** Congzhong Sun, Ning Tan
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Diffusion models and Flow Matching can represent flexible distributions over robot actions, but many reinforcement learning algorithms also require the probability of each action. We introduce PrT-Flow (Probability-Tractable Flow Matching), which computes probability density while retaining multimodal, multi-step action generation. Its Jacobian-Tractable Transformer (JTT) makes each sampling step invertible and enables efficient computation of its Jacobian determinant. We train these finite steps with Conditional Euler-step Training (CET), which supervises their displacement over one step during sampling rather than their instantaneous velocity. We prove that, under certain assumptions, CET has the same minimizers as fitting the Euler-solver updates of Flow Matching. PrT-Flow achieves 96% success on Robomimic Lift after 300 epochs of imitation learning, matching the diffusion policy reference, and 96% success on OGBench Cube-Triple after 600k online steps. These results demonstrate that invertible networks with tractable probability densities can learn effective robot policies and support SAC-style actor-critic training. Our code is available at this https URL .
### Title:
          Beyond Model Scaling: Rethinking Sequence Construction for Transformer-based Network Intrusion Detection
 - **Authors:** Zeeshan Zulkifl Shah, Muhammad Ikram, Dali Kaafar, Yipeng Zhou
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Cryptography and Security (cs.CR); Networking and Internet Architecture (cs.NI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Transformer-based network intrusion detection systems (NIDS) have shown strong potential for detecting multi-stage cyberattacks, yet most methods construct input sequences using fixed temporalization schemes rather than treating sequence construction as a systems design decision. In flow-based intrusion detection, timestamp-based windowing often fragments related attack activities while merging unrelated communications, reducing the behavioral context available to transformers. We present a unified pipeline abstraction that decomposes transformer-based NIDS into four design dimensions (traffic representation, temporalization, transformer backbone, and prediction aggregation) and propose session-coherent temporalization, which constructs sequences from communication sessions instead of fixed time windows. Experiments on three complementary intrusion detection datasets show that temporalization is the dominant factor governing transformer performance: session-coherent temporalization more than doubles Macro-F1 over timestamp-based windowing, enabling lightweight transformers to achieve up to 0.95 Macro-F1 at substantially higher inference throughput than pretrained models. We further vary the transformer size from 0.65M to 3.38M parameters and find that larger transformer models provide no measurable accuracy improvement despite substantially higher computational cost, while zero-shot cross-dataset evaluation shows only modest gains under domain shift. These results indicate that preserving communication-session semantics, rather than scaling transformer capacity, is the key to accurate and efficient transformer-based intrusion detection.
### Title:
          TempFit: Panoptic Symbol Spotting via Template Fitting
 - **Authors:** Honghao Xu, Hao Wang, Xiang Liu, Juzhan Xu, Manfred Lau, Kai Xu, Ruizhen Hu
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Graphics (cs.GR)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 In this paper, we introduce TempFit, a novel approach for the problem of Panoptic Symbol Spotting on CAD drawings. Existing methods treat symbol spotting as a per-primitive classification or mask-prediction task, with no explicit constraint enforcing that the primitives assigned to one instance form a spatially connected group. As a result, predictions may erroneously merge primitives from distant symbols into a single instance or fragment a coherent symbol across multiple predictions. Motivated by the professional "retrieve-and-transform" CAD design workflow, we reformulate this task as a Template Fitting problem, where each foreground instance is detected by retrieving and aligning a canonical template from a library, enforcing structural completeness by construction. Building on this formulation, we present an autoregressive transformer that unifies template retrieval and pose prediction within a single end-to-end pipeline. The framework treats the template library as an explicit geometric memory, parsing background regions in parallel via learnable background tokens and synthesizing foreground instances autoregressively while attending to template-specific geometry at each step. Experiments on FloorplanCAD and our newly collected IndustryCAD dataset, a more challenging benchmark with complex industrial symbols, heavy occlusion, and wide scale variation, demonstrate state-of-the-art performance with particularly large gains on IndustryCAD. Project page: this https URL.
## Keyword: autonomous driving
### Title:
          Safety-Oriented Trajectory Ranking from BEV Perception Outputs
 - **Authors:** Leon Pohl, Martin Frauendorfer, Corinna Schmitt, Mirko Maehlisch
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Bird's-Eye-View (BEV) representations are increasingly used as compact inputs for autonomous driving perception and planning. However, lightweight and interpretable methods for evaluating candidate ego trajectories directly on BEV outputs remain useful for vehicular safety workflows, rapid prototyping, and human-in-the-loop validation. This paper presents a modular trajectory ranking pipeline that consumes BEVFusion object detections and map segmentations derived from nuScenes sensor data and ranks manually specified candidate trajectories according to safety-first criteria. The ranking prioritizes avoiding collisions and walkway violations, then smaller end distance to the target, and finally path efficiency. To study whether these rule-based rankings resemble human judgment, we created seven BEV traffic scenarios and compared the script output with preferences from a survey of 21 participants. Across 146 pairwise votes, 124 matched the trajectory ranked first by the evaluator, corresponding to an overall agreement of 84.9 %. The results indicate that simple BEV-centric safety heuristics can approximate human preferences in constrained trajectory choice tasks while remaining transparent enough for analysis, dataset curation, and autonomous driving studies.
### Title:
          Drive3R: Block-wise Streaming Geometric Foundation Models for Autonomous Driving
 - **Authors:** Tianyi Yang, Haojun Yang, Jipeng Xiao, Xuesong Chen, Shaoshuai Shi, Li Jiang, Zhipeng Zhang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 High-fidelity closed-loop simulation for autonomous driving increasingly relies on dense multi-view 3D reconstruction. While recent feed-forward geometric foundation models have revolutionized per-scene optimization, applying them to continuous, long-horizon driving sequences is computationally intractable due to GPU memory limits and the quadratic complexity of global attention. Existing scaling strategies are fundamentally limited, as causal streaming suffers from compounding scale drift and trajectory deviation, while rigid "chunk-then-align" heuristics fragment global context and produce trajectory discontinuities. To address these limitations, we introduce Drive3R, a block-wise streaming geometric foundation model. Its block-wise causal attention resolves the scalability bottleneck of fully bidirectional attention while avoiding the loss of local future context in purely causal streaming. Its confidence-aware re-anchoring further mitigates long-range drift by detecting unreliable streaming states, re-initializing the KV cache, and stitching refreshed segments through SE(3) alignment. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Drive3R establishes a new state-of-the-art on nuScenes, KITTI, and Waymo. Specifically, on nuScenes, it improves over the recent DVGT-2 baseline from 0.1806 to 0.1081 in AbsRel, from 0.6750 to 0.5344 in point-cloud Accuracy, and from 1.5812 to 0.9092 in ATE.
### Title:
          RootSLAM: Square Root Bundle Adjustment for Visual-Inertial SLAM
 - **Authors:** Tomás Santucci, Mateo de Mayo, Daniel Cremers, Taihú Pire
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Efficient perception is a prerequisite for the small, low-power devices targeted by robotics, mixed reality, autonomous driving, and drone applications, all of which rely on Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (SLAM) to estimate their motion within their surroundings. Learning-based SLAM systems achieve state-of-the-art accuracy but are demanding in compute, whereas classical geometry-based methods retain a strong efficiency advantage with little to no loss in accuracy. In this work we present RootSLAM, a fully-featured visual-inertial SLAM system for multi-camera setups built on Basalt. RootSLAM achieves accuracy competitive with the state of the art while running up to 20 times faster, facilitating the deployment of perception systems in resource constrained environments. We adapt the square-root bundle adjustment formulation from structure from motion into the multi-camera visual-inertial SLAM setting through a novel reformulation that leverages the system's pose-graph, letting the global optimizer run in single-precision floating point, thus doubling its performance. We provide a thorough benchmark against the state of the art together with an ablation study of our contributions. Our new implementation, including its map management and loop detection module, pose-graph optimizer, and global mapping components, will be open-sourced.
### Title:
          Verifying Autoware Controller Node by Combining Model Verification and Runtime Monitoring
 - **Authors:** Daisuke Ishii
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Software Engineering (cs.SE)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Formal modeling and verification of autonomous driving systems remain challenging due to the vast operational space and the complexity of the control logic. We propose a hybrid verification approach that combines formal model verification and runtime monitoring for the trajectory-following control node of Autoware, an open-source autonomous driving platform. We formalize the controller as a hybrid system in differential dynamic logic (dL), and introduce an abstract model that captures the essential control dynamics. We then prove safety and liveness properties of the model using the KeYmaera X proof assistant. Complementarily, we monitor the constraints that assure the compatibility between the model and the Autoware implementation across numerical simulation runs using the Autoware toolchain, quantifying to what extent the implementation's behavior is subsumed by the verified model. The experimental results confirmed that (i) monitoring provides a sound evaluation of target following with accuracy bounds, and (ii) the Autoware implementation reaches the target with an accuracy of approximately 0.2-0.4 (m) in position and 0.8 (m/s) in velocity.
### Title:
          SpikeOcc: Towards Resilient Multimodal 3D Occupancy Perception via Spiking Experts
 - **Authors:** Yiding Sun, Zijie Xu, Hongqiang Lin, Jiajun Cao, Dongxu Zhang, Kai Tang, Jihua Zhu, Wenxuan Liu, Zhaofei Yu, Tiejun Huang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Multimodal 3D occupancy prediction underpins safe autonomous driving through comprehensive scene understanding. However, existing methods have largely pursued higher accuracy through sophisticated fusion, leaving resilience to missing sensors underexplored. In this paper, we investigate spiking neural networks (SNNs) as a means of building more resilient multimodal perception. Still, the discrete nature of spiking neurons makes optimization challenging. To overcome this difficulty, SpikeOcc is introduced with two complementary components, a Spiking Mixture-of-Experts (SMoE) and Sensor Availability Learning (SAL). Specifically, SMoE organizes RGB, LiDAR, and joint fusion into specialized pathways, enabling full-width unimodal representations while retaining the original multimodal features via a bounded residual. Beyond sensor dropout, SAL employs a staged curriculum with explicit routing supervision, progressively improving robustness under severe sensing conditions. Remarkably, SAL also yields notable gains for conventional models, highlighting its broader potential for resilient multimodal learning. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SpikeOcc maintains competitive full-sensor accuracy while markedly raising the performance floor under sensor outages. SpikeOcc illustrates the promise of neuromorphic computing, pointing toward more reliable and energy-efficient autonomous driving.
### Title:
          Mobility Helps Learning: Unsupervised Model Adaptation for Object Recognition via Movement
 - **Authors:** Yanan Ma, Yihang Tao, Zhengru Fang, Senkang Hu, Xianhao Chen, Yuguang Fang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Movable agents, such as autonomous vehicles and robots, should be able to autonomously adapt their pre-trained object recognition models to new environments without human annotations. While existing test-time adaptation (TTA) methods address label-free model adaptation, they typically treat test data as independent snapshots, without constructively exploiting a rich, unique source of supervision: the significant variance in prediction quality as an agent observes the same object from different distances and viewpoints. To capitalize on this, we propose \texttt{MoCaFe}, a model-agnostic and hyperparameter-insensitive paradigm that leverages motion-induced prediction variance for unsupervised adaptation. Specifically, we first develop mobility-calibrated filtering of pseudo-labels, which constructs reliable pseudo-labels by fusing cross-view predictions using inverse-variance weights. The filtered pseudo-labels then guide the selection of ``hard samples'' for effective learning. To further suppress pseudo-label corruption, we introduce \texttt{p-MoCaFe}, an optional extension that uses a public dataset to assign weighting factors to data samples, yielding an estimator of the clean loss with bounded bias. Extensive experiments on autonomous driving (nuScenes, KITTI) and embodied-agent datasets show that \texttt{MoCaFe} significantly outperforms state-of-the-art TTA schemes, achieving up to approximately 20% accuracy gain.
### Title:
          A Hardware-Partitioned micro-ROS Mini-Vehicle for Operator-Supervised Neural Driving
 - **Authors:** Gustavo Claudio Karl Couto, Eric Aislan Antonelo, Gabriel George Zipperer
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 We present a low-cost miniature Ackermann vehicle for embedded neural-driving research, organized as a hardware-partitioned perceive-decide-act agent: an ESP32 camera node streams frames over Wi-Fi to a host computer, where neural inference and state estimation run, while an ESP32-S3 chassis controller executes safety-critical actuation in a 100 Hz per-wheel proportional-integral-derivative (PID) loop beneath a micro-ROS client. The partition isolates real-time control and safety on the vehicle while learning-based components run off-board, supervised by an operator from a gamepad. We characterize the complete pipeline during real closed-loop neural driving. Wireless sensing, rather than neural inference, dominates latency, with a 59.5 ms median capture-to-actuation delay; the platform absorbed wireless variability by holding the last safe command under a firmware watchdog, operating across twelve autonomous sessions with zero collisions or manual interventions; and encoder-inertial-measurement-unit (IMU) fusion cut odometry loop-closure error by 22%. These results indicate that carefully partitioned microcontroller-class vehicles support reproducible experiments in embedded AI, wireless control, and operator-supervised autonomous driving. The platform software is open source.
### Title:
          Adversarial Robustness of Camera-Radar Sensor Fusion for Autonomous Driving
 - **Authors:** Ce Zhou, Yubo Wu, Khang Nguyen, Liyang Xiao, Thomas Hou, Qiben Yan
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Cryptography and Security (cs.CR); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Autonomous driving (AD) relies on multi-sensor perception to interpret the surrounding environment. However, the robustness of camera--radar fusion under adversarial conditions remains largely unexplored. This paper presents a systematic measurement study of the robustness of camera--radar sensor fusion in AD. We construct a unified evaluation pipeline on the nuScenes dataset that simulates attacks on both sensing modalities, including eight camera-image perturbations and three radar point-cloud perturbations. We evaluate five camera--radar fusion algorithms across two perception tasks, 3D object detection and depth estimation, and quantify their performance under clean and attacked inputs using standard metrics. Our evaluation reveals a clear asymmetry in the robustness of fusion systems. Camera perturbations substantially degrade both object detection and depth estimation, and moderate attack strengths are already sufficient to cause severe performance degradation. In contrast, the evaluated radar perturbations produce smaller and task-dependent effects, with object-detection models showing particularly limited degradation. Our analysis attributes this difference to the strong dependence of existing fusion architectures on dense camera features, together with the filtering, association, and confidence-weighting mechanisms commonly applied to sparse radar measurements. These results demonstrate that sensor fusion does not inherently guarantee robustness and that the contribution and reliability of each sensing modality must be explicitly considered under adversarial conditions. Based on four empirical findings, we derive measurement-guided design implications for future robust camera--radar fusion, including modality-specific reliability estimation, cross-modal consistency checking, adaptive fusion, and graceful failover under severe sensor degradation.
### Title:
          Directional Pullback Regularization for Long-Horizon Rollout of Latent World Models in Autonomous Driving
 - **Authors:** Shatadal Mishra, Ahmadreza Moradipari, Nejib Ammar
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Learned world models for autonomous driving operate in unconstrained latent spaces, yet the physical systems they represent are governed by strict non-holonomic constraints: ground vehicles cannot realize instantaneous pure lateral motion. We study long-horizon latent rollout modeling for autonomous driving and ask whether rollout quality can be improved by assigning a motion-aware geometry to latent transitions, rather than by redesigning the predictor architecture. Starting from an unconstrained latent rollout baseline, we introduce Directional Pullback Regularization (DPR), a training time method that defines a kinematics-aware anisotropic Riemannian metric in decoded state space and pulls it back through the decoder Jacobian to the latent manifold. The resulting latent-space metric assigns higher cost to latent directions that decode to lateral vehicle motion, while preserving the original encoder-dynamics-decoder backbone. This yields a clean three-way comparison among an Unconstrained Latent Rollout model (ULR), an Euclidean Pullback Regularization ablation (EPR), and the full directional method (DPR). We evaluate all models on open-loop ego-motion rollout over a common 10-second test horizon, using training rollout lengths of 1, 3, 5, and 10 steps. Across all four training regimes, DPR achieves lower 10-second final displacement error than both ULR and EPR. The strongest relative accuracy gain appears for models trained with 3-step rollout supervision, where DPR reduces the 10-second final displacement error by approximately 44 percent relative to ULR. The strongest overall control-relevant tradeoff is obtained for longer rollout-trained models, especially at 10 steps, where DPR simultaneously improves displacement error, lateral motion consistency, and forward motion preservation relative to both ULR and EPR.
### Title:
          XAI-Guided Fourier Domain Generalisation for Pure-Simulation Sim-to-Real Deep Reinforcement Learning
 - **Authors:** Wei Yao, Chuyao Wang, Ziwei Wang, Nabil Aouf
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Deep reinforcement learning has shown strong potential for vision-based autonomous driving, but policies trained in simulation often suffer severe performance degradation when deployed in the real world. Existing sim-to-real methods commonly address this gap through image-transfer-based domain adaptation or policy-agnostic domain randomisation. However, the former requires laborious acquisition of target-domain data and is often limited to the deployment domain, whereas the latter introduces unguided perturbations that may not reflect the underlying sim-to-real discrepancy, leading to suboptimal transfer performance. To overcome these limitations, we propose XFD, an explainable artificial intelligence (XAI)-guided Fourier domain generalisation framework for pure-simulation sim-to-real reinforcement learning. The proposed framework extracts explainability attribution maps from simulated observations, and converts these attributions into policy-conditioned Fourier-domain interventions. By perturbing appearance-sensitive spectral components while preserving task-defining spatial structures, XFD constructs robustness-enhancing visual domains entirely within simulation. Subsequently, the proposed robust policy optimisation guides the policy towards attending to task-critical features while suppressing sensitivity to irrelevant visual information. Extensive experiments in simulation and zero-shot real-world deployment demonstrate that XFD consistently improves traveled distance and reward over standard policy and randomly domain-randomised baseline, especially on unseen maps with cluttered backgrounds. Attribution-based analysis further proves that the proposed policy preserves more consistent task-relevant visual evidence across simulation and real-world observations.
### Title:
          Language2Drive: Do Vision-Language-Action Models Really Follow the Instructions in Autonomous Driving?
 - **Authors:** Zihan You, Zehua Fan, Wenqi Lyu, Aoqi Wang, Ni Wang, Yifei Yang, Linge Zhao, Huan Li, Sining Ang, Yuguang Yang, Jueyu Chen, Xiangyu Li, Qianshan Wei, Jieyuan Pei, Yuxin Wang, Zhiwen Chen, Jiaqi Li, Wenxuan Song, Yanyuan Qiao, Benjin Zhu, Haoang Li, Yan Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO); Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Recent Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models increasingly use natural-language instructions to condition autonomous driving behavior. Yet existing closed-loop benchmarks primarily measure route completion, safety, and rule compliance, leaving a fundamental question unresolved: does language actually determine which feasible behavior the vehicle executes? A trajectory may be safe and successful while still violating or even ignoring the passenger's instruction. To address this, we introduce \ltd, a benchmark designed to directly test this capability. Its key evaluation unit, the paired counterfactual unit (PCU), holds the initial driving scene fixed while changing only the instruction. This design enables us to distinguish isolated instruction success from counterfactual paired success across four instruction-following scopes. To test whether this distinction persists across evaluation regimes, we instantiate Language2Drive in complementary closed-loop CARLA simulation, reconstructed real-world scenes, and open-loop driving benchmarks, including our released CARLA-Language2Drive simulator with purpose-built maps. Evaluating representative driving VLAs across these settings reveals a striking mismatch between strong driving competence and weak language control: 93.2% of successful rollouts do not have a successful counterpart under the alternative feasible instruction. This failure is dominated by one-sided completion, where a model repeatedly executes one preferred behavior but fails when the same scene requires the counterfactual alternative. These results expose two fundamental limitations of current evaluation: isolated success and driving scores, which are widely used by conventional driving benchmarks, can substantially overstate language-following capability, while current driving VLAs themselves lack reliable instruction following under matched counterfactual tests.
### Title:
          ConDenseDrive: Knowledge-Dense, Token-Sparse Visual Encoder for End-to-End Autonomous Driving
 - **Authors:** Shengzhen Li, Yongkang Li, Hao Gao, Chenxi Wu, Yajing Sun, Lu Hou, Wenyu Liu, Xinggang Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 End-to-end driving requires rich spatiotemporal representations to support scene understanding and trajectory planning under strict latency constraints. However, most systems initialize their visual encoders from generic image pretraining and densely process all patch tokens for each camera view and input frame, limiting both representation quality and inference efficiency. To address these challenges, we present ConDenseDrive, a knowledge-dense and token-sparse visual encoder for end-to-end autonomous driving. Our three-stage framework first makes the encoder knowledge-dense by distilling complementary visual, semantic, and geometric knowledge from three vision foundation models. It then adapts the distilled encoder to multi-frame end-to-end driving and integrates spatiotemporal context into register tokens for planning. Finally, it makes the encoder token-sparse by applying register-guided token compression, reducing redundant patch computation and yielding a 2.0$\times$ speedup while improving planning performance. ConDenseDrive achieves 94.5 PDMS on NAVSIM v1 and 90.8 EPDMS on NAVSIM v2, and reaches 37.4 HD-Score in zero-shot evaluation on HUGSIM. Extensive experiments and qualitative analyses demonstrate that visual encoder design provides an effective route to reliable and efficient end-to-end driving.
### Title:
          AUGUstus: Autonomous Driving VLA Model with Unified Generation and Understanding
 - **Authors:** Yuqi Ye, Caigao Jiang, Zhuonan Wang, Congyuan Pei, Chao Qu, Changhao Peng, Junhong Lin, Qize Yu, Zetian Song, Guangzhao Fu, Chongruo Wu, Shiyu Huang, Shuchang Zhou, Honggou Yang, Wei Gao
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (cs.CV); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Vision-language-action (VLA) models show strong potential for end-to-end autonomous driving by leveraging world knowledge and understanding to excel in long-tail scenarios. However, current VLAs have two limitations: 1) Degeneration into vision-action (VA) models. For low latency, regression/flow-based methods directly generate actions but underuse VLM understanding, causing unsafe, unexplainable actions. 2) Autoregressive high latency. Autoregressive methods sequentially generate chain-of-thought (CoT) and action tokens, fully leveraging VLM understanding, but high latency hinders deployment. To enable low-latency deployment while using CoT to assist action generation, we propose AUGUstus, an Autonomous-driving VLA model with Unified Generation and Understanding. We unify diffusion-based action generation and autoregressive CoT understanding in one model, where understanding only serves generation. Joint CoT supervision preserves VLM spatial understanding in shared representation for safer, more accurate trajectories. AUGUstus-mode orders action generation before CoT, so CoT supervises reliable actions during training while inference needs only action output, enabling low latency. AUGUstus-GRPO, a unified GRPO algorithm, jointly optimizes action and CoT, improving action performance. Experiments show AUGUstus achieves 94.6 PDMS on NAVSIMv1 and 90.7 EPDMS on NAVSIMv2, SOTA planning without scorer module. On nuScenes, it achieves average L2 error 0.27 m and average collision rate 0.05%, best among compared methods. To validate AUGUstus practicality, we train on in-house data with 512 NVIDIA H800 GPUs for ~2 days, evaluate on an internal 1,000 km dataset, and preliminarily deploy on a real vehicle, achieving 12 Hz operation. Project page: this https URL
### Title:
          TacitVLA: Reasoning Vision-Language-Action Models Without Reasoning Latency
 - **Authors:** Jack Lu, Yurong You, Boyi Li, Yulong Cao, Thomas Tian, Chaowei Xiao, Xiangyu Chen, Wenjie Luo, Boris Ivanovic, Marco Pavone, Yan Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Textual reasoning before acting has improved vision-language-action (VLA) models, but at the cost of latency: every action conditioned on the reasoning text must wait for autoregressive decoding. We show that actions do not need to wait for or condition on the generated reasoning to benefit from it, because the reasoning supervision already improves the backbone representation. We introduce TacitVLA, a reasoning VLA whose action expert never attends to the reasoning it generates for the current call. On long-horizon tasks where the reasoning text also serves as episodic memory, it can generate each entry during action execution rather than before acting. We evaluate it in a controlled study on four benchmarks spanning manipulation and autonomous driving, memory and non-memory tasks: RoboMME, RMBench, LIBERO, and PhysicalAI-AV. On all four, TacitVLA matches the performance of its explicit-reasoning counterpart at up to 3.4x lower latency, and matches the latency of the same architecture trained without reasoning at up to +17.1% success. In absolute terms, it surpasses the best previously published results on the RoboMME Counting suite (82.7% vs. 66.8%) and RMBench (77.5% vs. 56.5%), and reaches 72.9% on LIBERO-Plus and a 52.5% scene score on the public split of the PhysicalAI-AV closed-loop driving challenge. Project page: this https URL.
### Title:
          OccWAM: Occupancy World-Action Models with Joint Scene-to-Ego Flow Matching
 - **Authors:** Qihang Huang, Wenhui Huang, Collister Chua, Shiyu Fang, Zhan Chen, Songyan Zhang, Zhidong Wang, Daiying Tian, Chen Lv
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Occupancy world models provide a structured, metric representation of future driving scenes, making them a promising basis for end-to-end autonomous driving. However, existing occupancy-aware planners typically couple occupancy prediction and action generation through either hierarchical coupling or parallel multi-task learning. Hierarchical methods use occupancy either as a fully predicted future preceding action generation or as an action-conditioned rollout for trajectory evaluation, whereas parallel multi-task methods predict occupancy alongside planning through a separate branch or auxiliary objective. These interfaces retain either an explicit generation order or a role asymmetry, limiting reciprocal interaction between world prediction and action generation during trajectory formation. We propose OccWAM, an occupancy-native world-action model that jointly generates dense future occupancy and continuous ego trajectories via flow matching conditioned on scene representations. Building on this joint formulation, we introduce scene-to-ego world-action flow matching, a two-stage formulation. The first stage predicts scene-centric occupancy together with an absolute ego trajectory, providing a stable representation of future scene evolution. The same predicted world is then re-indexed into the future ego frames induced by the initial trajectory, where ego motion is represented through adjacent pose increments and a second joint flow refines ego-centric occupancy and motion for action-aligned planning. Unlike video-based world-action models, OccWAM preserves explicit semantic geometry for direct planning reasoning without pixel synthesis. Extensive experiments on Bench2Drive and nuScenes demonstrate state-of-the-art performance in closed-loop driving, open-loop planning, and future occupancy prediction.
### Title:
          Which Trajectory Is Safer? Combining Rule and Learning for Safety Evaluation
 - **Authors:** Xuewen Luo, Linlian Jiang, Bo Yu, Fan Ding, Xinxin Zuo, Yang Wang
 - **Subjects:** Subjects:
Robotics (cs.RO)
 - **Arxiv link:** https://arxiv.org/abs/
 - **Pdf link:** https://arxiv.org/pdf/
 - **Abstract**
 Collision rate remains a dominant metric for evaluating autonomous driving trajectory safety, but its binary nature obscures substantial safety differences among collision-free trajectories. Existing surrogate-safety metrics provide finer-grained assessments, yet often trade off physical interpretability against adaptivity to interaction context. We introduce FutureRisk, a continuous metric that quantifies the latent future risk of a trajectory by how closely and persistently its realized interactions approach unsafe states. At its core, our Context-Adaptive Risk Model (CARM) combines a context-dependent safety-time distribution with an independently supervised critical-event-time distribution. Its safety criterion is bounded around a closed-form physical prior, while a bounded residual provides additional adaptation without abandoning this physical anchor. Experiments on DeepAccident and Bench2Drive show that FutureRisk achieves stronger agreement with native collision labels than existing surrogate-safety measures, discriminates risk among collision-free trajectories, and transfers zero-shot across datasets. Comparisons across four planners further show that FutureRisk reveals safety differences hidden by binary collision outcomes. Together, these results establish FutureRisk as a fine-grained, interpretable, and context-adaptive metric for trajectory safety evaluation. Code is available at this https URL.
